{"id": "ab39085b21b02377", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:06:57.310753+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C56 legal-primal repair completed at draft level: the actual C55 formal GD trajectory can be made jointly interpolable by ONE monotone quadratic auxiliary coordinate with explicit amplitude, producing a globally convex 1-smooth function on R² with UNIQUE minimizer0. Full all-pair law and true distance are proved. But the retained last two QUERY gradients force T²>D_bad/[kappa³(1-kappa)b1² xi_(n-2)²], hence T²>kappa^(-3)>h_max³. Even exact maximal unqueried-terminal completion has limsup M_m*payoff<=1/exp(1)<1, so this stated mechanism cannot separate the actual schedule from q_m=1/M_m. This is a realizable mechanism plus its exact scoped cost, not an unrestricted W bound.", "evidence": "# C56: a legal two-dimensional repair and its unavoidable normalization cost\n\nStatus: **complete scoped joint proof candidate, not verifier-accepted**.\nNo optimization or fact_submit is allocated. The only mathematical\npredecessor is accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, for smooth-convex\ninterpolation, its explicit conjugate construction, and exact unqueried\nterminal completion. The scalar template is defined and analyzed here;\nno unverified C53/C55 claim is used as a premise.\n\nThis is a primal construction for the actual critical schedule. It produces\none globally defined convex smooth function, with all queried data jointly\nlegal, in dimension two. Its limitation is quantitative: the specific\nrepair mechanism cannot retain the very large formal objective ratio.\nNeither this construction nor its obstruction decides the actual worst-case\nperformance of the schedule or the unrestricted minimax problem.\n\n## 1. Schedule and a completely specified scalar formal trajectory\n\nFix m>=128 and put\n\n    epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n    d_j=a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nWrite h=H_m, n=n_m, S=S_m, M_m=1+S, and\n\n    b0=1+a, b1=1+d_1=2-epsilon,\n    h_max=1+d_(m-1).\n\nEvery step is greater than one; the largest step is the central bridge.\nAll choices depend only on the fixed horizon, before an oracle is queried.\n\nDefine signed template moments\n\n    k_2=M_2+6epsilon,\n    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j for j>=2.                       (1)\n\nFor an H2 block whose terminal gradient is the scalar v, prescribe\n\n    g_0=(2-c+6epsilon/b0)v,\n    g_1=v, g_2=c v, g_3=v.                           (2)\n\nStart this block at x_0=k_2 v and use its actual GD steps. Directly,\nb0 g_0+b1 g_1+b0 g_2+g_3=k_2 v, so x_3=v.\nAt a higher H_(j+1) block with terminal gradient v, use the level-j\ntemplate with terminal v on the right and with terminal\n\n    v_left=-k_j v/d_j\n\non the left. Start the parent at x_start=k_(j+1)v. Since its left queried\ndisplacement is (k_j-1)v_left, its left endpoint is v_left. Its bridge\nthen ends at -d_j v_left=k_j v, exactly the prescribed right start.\nThe right endpoint is v. This proves the actual chronological GD recursion\nof the formal positions and gradients at every level.\n\nUse terminal gradient 1 at the root. Thus\n\n    x_0=k_m, x_n=g_n=1,\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.\n\nFor each scalar sample put y_i=x_i-g_i. Every H2 seed endpoint has y=0.\nInside a seed with terminal v, define G0=2-c+6epsilon/b0. Its gradients\nand proximal coordinates, in order, are\n\n    gradients:   (G0, 1, c, 1) v,\n    proximal:    (k_2-G0, 1+d_1+b0 c, 1+a c, 0) v.     (3)\n\nThe template is not legal. In the rightmost seed the two queried indices\ni=n-2 and j=n-1 have gradients 1,c and displacement x_i-x_j=b1.\nTheir sum of smooth-convex residuals, independently of function values, is\n\n    Q_ij+Q_ji=(c-1)(d_1+c)>0,                        (4)\n\nwhere Q is defined below. Thus no scalar function can realize these jets.\n\nFor the size estimates needed in the repair, the critical ratio obeys\n\n    R0=k_2/(c d_2)<1-epsilon/4,\n    |k_j|=(1-epsilon)c^j R0^(2^(j-2)).                (5)\n\nHere is a direct verification of the strict gap. Since M_2=5-epsilon+2a,\n\n    (c d_2-M_2)/epsilon\n      =[6sqrt(2)+epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2\n                                  -lambda^2 epsilon^3]/(1+epsilon)>8.\n\nFor the last inequality subtract 8 after multiplying by 1+epsilon and use\n\n    6sqrt(2)-8-7epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^3\n      >2/5-7/128-12/128^2-6/128^3>0.\n\nIt follows that c d_2-k_2>2epsilon, while c d_2<25/4. This proves the\nfirst inequality in (5); the second follows by squaring the ratio at each\nstep of (1). Every absolute left-child scale |k_j|/d_j is less than c.\nConsequently every seed's absolute terminal amplitude is at most c^(m-2).\n\nThe elementary bounds c<5/2, k_2<6, |G0|<1, and b0<sqrt(2)<3/2 give\nfrom (3)\n\n    |g_i|<3c^m, |y_i|<7c^m                           (6)\n\nfor every sample. For clarity, k_2<6 follows from\nM_2<5+6/7 and 6epsilon<=3/64; and\nG0 lies between -1/2 and 2-12/5+3/64<0. These bounds include the terminal\nsample, with the optimum sample g_star=y_star=0 added separately.\n\nEquation (5) also gives\n\n    |k_m|<c^m exp[-2^(m-4)/m] ->0.\n\nThus the formal choice f_n=1/2 would have an enormous ratio 1/k_m^2. It\nis precisely this initially tiny normalization that the repair must pay for.\n\n## 2. One explicit extra coordinate repairs ALL interpolation inequalities\n\nChoose any curvature\n\n    0<kappa<1/h_max,\n\nand define the monotone quadratic trajectory\n\n    xi_0=1, xi_(i+1)=(1-kappa h_i)xi_i,\n    xi_star=0.\n\nAll xi_i are positive and strictly decreasing. This is a single extra\ncoordinate; no incompatible instances are averaged. For a scalar T>0\nset the proposed two-dimensional jets to\n\n    X_i=(x_i,T xi_i),\n    G_i=(g_i,T kappa xi_i),\n    f_i=[g_i^2+T^2 kappa xi_i^2]/2,\n    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                        (7)\n\nThe entire vector GD recursion is exact:\nX_(i+1)=X_i-h_i G_i. The scalar first coordinate by itself is still not a\nlegal function; the final interpolating function will be jointly convex\nin both coordinates and is not asserted to be a separable sum.\n\nUse the accepted interpolation convention\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2.\n\nDirect expansion gives the FULL all-pair law, including every star pair:\n\n    Q_ij=g_i(y_j-y_i)\n                   -T^2 kappa(1-kappa)(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2. (8)\n\nThe first term follows by substituting f_i^formal=g_i^2/2 and x_i=g_i+y_i;\nthe second is the residual for the quadratic t -> kappa t^2/2. Thus the\nsame function values and the same two coordinates are tested against all\nordered pairs, not merely against one aggregated dual form.\n\nFrom (6), g_i(y_j-y_i)<=42c^(2m). For any two distinct finite indices,\ntheir xi displacement is at least one adjacent displacement, and\n\n    xi_i-xi_(i+1)=kappa h_i xi_i>kappa xi_n.\n\nFor an optimum pair xi_i>=xi_n>kappa xi_n, since kappa<1. Therefore\nthe completely explicit choice\n\n    T^2=84c^(2m)/[kappa^3(1-kappa)xi_n^2]+k_m^2        (9)\n\nmakes every Q_ij<=0. There is no optimization or nearest-feasible-point\nproblem hidden in (9): every quantity is an explicit scalar or product\nof the predetermined steps.\n\nTo produce one actual function, let J={star,0,...,n}, and define on R^2\n\n    H(v)=max_(i in J) {\n       <G_i,X_i>-f_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2 },\n    F(X)=sup_v {<X,v>-H(v)}.                         (10)\n\nBy accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, F is finite everywhere, convex,\ndifferentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant one, and interpolates every\njet in (7). Formally this use of the predecessor is obtained by appending\none zero step to h, so all indices 0,...,n are queried prefix samples.\nThe predecessor explicitly permits that zero step. All the required\nprefix interpolation inequalities were proved in (8)-(9).\n\nThe minimizer of F is UNIQUE and equals zero. Indeed write\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+\n           max_(i in J){<X_i-G_i,v>+||G_i||^2/2-f_i}.\n\nAt v=0, the star term is zero, whereas every other intercept is\n\n    ||G_i||^2/2-f_i=-T^2 kappa(1-kappa)xi_i^2/2<0.\n\nThere are finitely many terms. In a neighborhood of zero only the star\nterm is active, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 there. The set of minimizers of its\nconjugate is the subgradient set of H at zero, which here is {0}.\nThis last assertion can also be checked from the defining supremum:\nF(X)=0 requires <X,v><=H(v) for all v, hence X=0 by taking arbitrarily\nsmall positive and negative v; and H(v)>=||v||^2/2 gives F(0)=0.\n\nConsequently the TRUE squared distance to the closest minimizer is\n\n    dist(X_0,argmin F)^2=k_m^2+T^2.                  (11)\n\nThere is no artificial enlargement from choosing a remote minimizer.\nThe exact normalized terminal payoff of the constructed function is\n\n    P_assigned=[1+T^2 kappa xi_n^2]/[k_m^2+T^2].       (12)\n\nMultiplying F by any L>0 gives a convex function with L-Lipschitz gradient,\nthe same GD trajectory for steps h_i/L, and the same project-normalized\npayoff 2(F(X_n)-F_star)/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2.\n\n## 3. This is a nonzero legal witness, with a fully quantified scale\n\nFor the explicit choice kappa=1/(2S), we have kappa h_i<=1/2, so it is in\nthe permitted range. For 0<=z<=1/2,\n\n    log(1-z)>=-2z,\n\nbecause the derivative of log(1-z)+2z is (1-2z)/(1-z)>=0 and its value\nat zero is zero. It follows that xi_n>=exp(-2kappa S)=exp(-1).\nSince (9) gives T^2>=k_m^2, (12) yields the explicit lower bound\n\n    P_assigned >= kappa xi_n^2/2\n                       >=exp(-2)/(4S).               (13)\n\nThis is an actual legal two-dimensional witness with order 1/S payoff.\nIt does not claim a new worst-case lower bound beyond what simpler\nquadratic or Huber examples already provide. Its purpose is to make the\nproposed deformation of the formal template fully concrete and legal.\n\n## 4. One retained QUERY pair forces a cubic normalization cost\n\nThe cost is not an artifact of the conservative sufficient amplitude (9).\nTake ANY T>0 and ANY function values for which the queried positions and\ngradients (7) are jointly legal with minimizer zero. Keep only the queried\npair i=n-2, j=n-1; no terminal sample is used in this argument.\n\nIn the first coordinate the rightmost H2 seed has\n\n    g_i=1, g_j=c, x_i-x_j=b1,\n    Q_ij^formal+Q_ji^formal=(c-1)(d_1+c)=:D_bad.\n\nFunction values cancel in this sum, so these equalities remain valid for\narbitrary proposed values. In the second coordinate\n\n    xi_i-xi_j=kappa b1 xi_i.\n\nThe necessary sum of the TWO original interpolation inequalities is\ntherefore exactly\n\n    D_bad-T^2 kappa^3(1-kappa)b1^2 xi_(n-2)^2<=0.\n\nThus every feasible repair in this stated geometry must obey\n\n    T^2 >= D_bad/[kappa^3(1-kappa)b1^2 xi_(n-2)^2].    (14)\n\nWe have D_bad>4: c-1>4/3 and d_1+c>3. Also b1<=2 and xi_(n-2)<=1.\nConsequently\n\n    1/T^2<kappa^3, and T^2>kappa^(-3)>h_max^3.        (15)\n\nThis is a necessary inequality for the original queried geometry. Changing\nfunction values or applying maximal completion at the last unqueried point\ncannot remove it. It explains the cubic price: the quadratic coordinate's\nadjacent displacement is proportional to kappa, and its interpolation\nslack is proportional to another kappa times the squared displacement.\n\nFor the assigned terminal values, combining (12),(15) gives\n\n    P_assigned < kappa xi_n^2+kappa^3\n               <=1/[2 exp(1) S]+h_max^(-3).           (16)\n\nHere xi_n<=exp(-kappa S), from 1-z<=exp(-z), and\nmax_(t>=0) t exp(-2St)=1/[2 exp(1)S], as follows by differentiating.\n\n## 5. Maximal terminal completion also pays the cost\n\nKeep only the queried jets i<n with the assigned values (7), together\nwith the optimum. Use the accepted maximal prefix interpolant from\n`1252b8823bb5a290` to complete the last unqueried point. This is a legal\nsingle global function, and its value at X_n is maximal among all\ninterpolants of those prefix jets. The strict negative intercepts in\nSection 2 still apply to every retained non-star jet, so its minimizer\nis again uniquely zero. Thus (11) is still the actual closest-minimizer\ndistance. No terminal gradient or terminal value is imposed in this part.\n\nThere is a uniform upper bound on this maximal terminal payoff. More\ngenerally it applies to any legal prefix with the displayed X_i,G_i and\nminimizer zero, for its ratio normalized by ||X_0||^2. Let i=n-1 be the\nlast queried point. Interpolation with the optimum gives\n\n    f_i<=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2.\n\nThe smoothness inequality on the actual final step X_n=X_i-b0 G_i gives\n\n    F(X_n)<=f_i-b0(1-b0/2)||G_i||^2.\n\nThese two inequalities imply\n\n    2F(X_n)<=2<G_i,X_i>-(1+2b0-b0^2)||G_i||^2.        (17)\n\nHere g_i=c, x_i=1+b0 c, and the second coordinate is (T xi_i,T kappa xi_i).\nSince 1+2b0-b0^2>0, the right side is at most\n\n    2c+(b0^2-1)c^2+2T^2 kappa xi_i^2\n                     <12+2T^2 kappa xi_i^2.\n\nDivide by k_m^2+T^2, apply (15), and use\nxi_i<=exp[-kappa(S-b0)]. The normalized completed payoff satisfies\n\n    P_completed < 12kappa^3+2kappa exp[-2kappa(S-b0)]\n                 <=12h_max^(-3)+1/[exp(1)(S-b0)].     (18)\n\nThe denominator S-b0 is positive. This bound holds uniformly in the\ncurvature 0<kappa<1/h_max and in every feasible amplitude T. In particular\nit covers the explicit repair (9) and exact maximal unqueried-terminal\ncompletion. Every queried-prefix constraint, especially (14), is retained.\n\nAs m tends to infinity, S tends to infinity and b0 stays bounded. Also\n\n    M_m<=1+n h_max<2^m h_max,\n    h_max>(127/128)lambda^(m-2),\n    M_m/h_max^3 < 2^m/h_max^2 ->0.\n\nThe last limit follows from 2/lambda^2<1. Equations (16),(18) therefore\ngive the explicit leading-scale bounds\n\n    limsup M_m P_assigned <=1/[2 exp(1)],\n    limsup M_m P_completed <=1/exp(1)<1.              (19)\n\nHence this whole monotone single-quadratic-coordinate repair mechanism\ncannot supply a witness separating the schedule's actual worst case from\nthe proposed objective level 1/M_m. Its canonical repaired functions have\na genuine unique minimizer, so this failure is not caused by a loose choice\nof normalization point. It is caused by the retained adjacent queried\npair (4), which forces the normalization cost (14).\n\n## 6. Exact scope and the remaining repair requirement\n\nThe constructive statement covers the original C55 formal trajectory\nwith one monotone quadratic auxiliary coordinate and the explicit\nconjugate function (10). The necessary cost (14) does not depend on the\nvalues, while the unique-minimizer assertion is proved for the two\ncanonical functions with assigned prefix values: the full-jet interpolant\nand its maximal unqueried-terminal completion. For arbitrary different\nprefix values or different interpolants, (18) concerns the specified\nminimizer zero; it does not silently assert that zero is their closest\nminimizer.\n\nNo assertion is made about oscillating auxiliary coordinates, multiple\nindependent repair directions, a changed first-coordinate trajectory,\ndifferent knot/value rules, or a completely different adversary. The\nresult also does not claim that every fixed-dimensional witness has this\ncost. The curvature restriction 0<kappa<1/h_max is essential to this\nparticular monotone repair and is displayed throughout.\n\nThe original target quantifies over arbitrary nonnegative schedules,\nincluding zeros and unbounded positive steps. The present construction\nuses a strictly positive schedule with growing bridges; it does not\nreplace those quantifiers. The appended zero step is only a precise way\nto apply the already accepted full-prefix interpolation theorem.\n\nFor the next primal repair, a concrete requirement is now visible: the\nauxiliary coordinates must supply order-one adjacent co-coercivity at\nthe rightmost H2 defect without paying order kappa^(-3) initial energy,\nor the scalar gradients at that pair must be changed. This is a test of\na proposed new construction, not a theorem-equivalent claim that such a\nrepair exists or has negligible cost.\n\nAll new claims here remain draft evidence. There is no new accepted fact,\nno unrestricted upper bound for W_n(H_m), and no resolution of the exact\nZhang--Jiang profile problem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "C56_legal_quadratic_coordinate_repair", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["704f32e7f06cbf56"], "research_path": "research/c56_legal_quadratic_coordinate_repair.md", "verification_status": "UNVERIFIED complete joint candidate; no routine fact_submit allocation", "scope": "single monotone quadratic auxiliary coordinate, fixed C55 first coordinate, canonical full-jet or maximal-prefix interpolant with unique minimizer; not arbitrary repairs", "budget_delta": {"optimization_calls": 0, "build_solve_seconds": 0, "fact_submit_calls": 0}}, "glossary": {"C56": "The actual two-dimensional all-pair repair defined in this finding.", "h": "The predetermined critical word H_m.", "H_m": "The recursive word H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), with a=(1-1/m)/c and c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m).", "k_j": "Scalar formal block moment, k_2=M_2+6/m and k_(j+1)=-k_j²/d_j.", "xi_i": "Monotone auxiliary quadratic coordinate product_(t<i)(1-kappa h_t), xi_0=1.", "kappa": "Auxiliary curvature restricted to 0<kappa<1/h_max.", "T": "Amplitude of the auxiliary coordinate, explicitly chosen in (9) and necessarily bounded below by (14).", "X_i": "Actual two-dimensional position (x_i,T xi_i).", "G_i": "Actual two-dimensional gradient (g_i,T kappa xi_i).", "D_bad": "The value-independent positive defect (c-1)(d_1+c)>4 at queried pair (n-2,n-1).", "P_assigned": "Normalized payoff of the full-jet canonical interpolant, exact formula (12).", "P_completed": "Normalized payoff of the canonical maximal prefix interpolant at the unqueried terminal point.", "h_max": "Largest actual step 1+d_(m-1).", "S": "Sum of the actual steps in H_m.", "M_m": "1+S."}}
{"id": "468f3abd12846a0f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:14:15.043545+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "DOUBLE-ZERO growing Newton mechanism fully closed in a new precise class: chi=z² product_j(z-1/d_j), arbitrary common real PSD metric, and one tilted squared-distance realization F=dist(.,C)²/2+<v,.>-||v||²/2. ALL queried pairs force w_j=0 for every j>=2, so the actual gradient is constant from query2 onward. For every free b in[0,2],2F<=1/[2(sumH_m-2d_0-d_1)+1+b(2-b)], while an actual nonquadratic unique-minimizer instance attains the Huber value. Exact-B limsup<=2/e<1, proved without importing C54. General JOINT positive heights remain open; an isolated surviving level necessarily costs delta_(2^(j-1))>=e^-28 d_j||w_j||²/320. Entire package is an unverified draft, not a full zero-node or target closure.", "evidence": "# A double-zero Newton chain with a tilted projection realization\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate for a precisely stated growing-span\nclass, plus an explicit open positive-height interface. No optimization,\ninstallation or fact_submit. The only fact inputs are 1252b8823bb5a290\n(all-pair interpolation and its explicit function construction) and\nb98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark recursions). All new claims below are\nproved within this joint draft. This is not the original all-schedule target.\n\n## 1. Actual chronology and the surviving double-zero representation\n\nFix m>=128 and, throughout this horizon, keep\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n n=2^m-1, q=n-1.\n\nLet h_0,...,h_(q-1) be H_m with its last d_0 removed. Queries are0,...,q;\nthe final step b is any number in[0,2], including the original b=d_0. Write\n\n s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=s_q, P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),\n mu_j=1/d_j, p_ij=P_i(mu_j).\n\nAlso write S_j=sum H_j, so S_total=S_m and S=S_m-d_0. Useful elementary\nbounds are12/5<c<5/2,3/8<a<1/2 and11/8<d_0<3/2. To check them, use\n12/5<1+sqrt2<17/7 and m>=128: c<2193/896<5/2 and\na>(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8. The other bounds follow immediately.\nThe ratios d_j/d_(j+1) decrease with j because (1+u)/(1+cu) decreases in u;\ntheir initial value is less than(3/2)/(2-1/128)=192/255<4/5.\n\nConsider the real polynomial quotient\n\n                 chi(z)=z^2 product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j).       (1)\n\nIts dimension is m+2, and it contains the double-zero direction which is NOT\nannihilated by the schedule. Every real linear map from this quotient has\nunique vector coefficients u,v,w_0,...,w_(m-1) such that\n\n T[p]=u p(0)+v p'(0)+sum_j d_j w_j p(mu_j).\n\nIndeed the evaluation/derivative map into these m+2 coordinates is injective:\nits kernel consists of polynomials divisible by chi; on degree<m+2 the\nkernel is zero. Equality of dimensions makes it an isomorphism. The positive\nfactors d_j only rescale these coordinates.\n\nThus the actual common-quotient data are exactly\n\n R_i=sum_j p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_j d_j p_ij w_j,\n x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, ||x_0||=||u+Z_0||=1.     (2)\n\nAll vectors are real and their common Gram matrix may be ANY PSD matrix,\nincluding arbitrary initial correlations and singular limits. No mode is\nassumed individually legal and no metric is assumed diagonal.\nThe polynomial identity for P_(i+1) proves x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.\n\nThe first occurrence of d_j has step index2^j-1. Hence\n\n p_ij>0 for0<=i<2^j, and p_ij=0 for i>=2^j.            (3)\n\nBefore that first occurrence all steps are strictly less than d_j, so all\nfactors are positive. At that occurrence a zero factor appears and remains.\nIn particular every R_i and Z_i vanishes for i>=2^(m-1). These queries form\nthe genuine constant-gradient suffix x_i=u-s_i v, g_i=v.\n\nFor arbitrary shared values f_i, the complete nonstar interpolation law is\n\n f_i-f_j+<v+R_i,(s_i-s_j)v+Z_j-Z_i>\n                                  +||R_i-R_j||^2/2<=0.        (4)\n\nThe optimum constraints are\n\n ||v+R_i||^2/2<=f_i\n       <=<v+R_i,u-s_i v+Z_i>-||v+R_i||^2/2.            (5)\n\nEvery ordered pair in (4) remains. These are exactly the accepted fact's\ninequalities, so together with a real Gram realization they are sufficient\nfor ONE actual convex1-smooth interpolant, including every queried value and\nthe optimum. This is a restricted m+2-dimensional temporal system, not the\nunrestricted n+1-node reparameterization of the original problem.\n\n## 2. A definite function-realization proposal: tilted squared distance\n\nFor the chosen mechanism define\n\n                 y_i=x_i-R_i=u-s_i v+sum_j(d_j-1)p_ij w_j.\n\nChoose the shared values\n\n                    f_i=||R_i||^2/2+<v,x_i>-||v||^2/2.       (6)\n\nAt the optimum set R_star=-v and y_star=v; formula(6) still gives f_star=0\nat x_star=0. Substituting (6) into every original Q_ij gives the exact law\n\n                   Q_ij=<R_i,y_j-y_i><=0              (7)\n\nfor ALL ordered pairs i,j in {star,0,...,q}. In particular the original\noptimum constraints are retained, rather than imposed separately on modes.\n\nWhen (7) holds, let C=conv{v,y_0,...,y_q}. For any query i and any c in C,\n<R_i,c-y_i><=0. Since x_i=y_i+R_i,\n\n ||x_i-c||^2=||R_i||^2+||c-y_i||^2-2<R_i,c-y_i>\n                                                >=||R_i||^2.\n\nThus the Euclidean projection of x_i onto C is y_i. The star condition\nlikewise says that the projection of0 onto C is v. Consequently the actual\nfunction\n\n             F_C(x)=dist(x,C)^2/2+<v,x>-||v||^2/2       (8)\n\ninterpolates ALL the prescribed jets and has minimum zero at zero.\n\nFor completeness, Euclidean projections onto a closed convex set obey\n<P_Cx-P_Cz,x-z>>=||P_Cx-P_Cz||^2, obtained by adding the two projection\nnormal inequalities. Therefore I-P_C is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the minimum\nsquared-distance expressions at x and x+d gives differentiability of\ndist(x,C)^2/2 with gradient x-P_Cx. This function is convex, since joint\nconvexity of ||x-c||^2 and convexity of C give the convexity inequality after\ntaking minima over c. Addition of the linear term preserves convexity and\nsmoothness. Its gradient at0 is-v+v=0, proving the minimum assertion.\n\nThe actual minimizer set is also explicit. The star inequalities give\n<v,c>>=||v||^2 for every c in C. Completing the square gives\n\n F_C(x)=min_(c in C){||x-(c-v)||^2/2+<v,c>-||v||^2}.\n\nHence argmin F_C={c-v:c in C,<v,c>=||v||^2}. One may require this face to be\nthe singleton{v}, or require0 to be the closest point in this set to x_0,\nwhen normalizing by the TRUE closest-minimizer distance. The upper result\nbelow holds with any designated minimizer0 and ||x_0||=1, and hence also\nwith that stronger true-distance convention. The lower calibration has a\nunique minimizer, so its denominator is exactly the true distance.\n\nThe same obstruction applies to ANY convex1-smooth interpolant whose queried\nvalues obey (6), not only to the particular representative (8). Only the\nnecessary all-pair law (7) is used in its proof.\n\n## 3. Full obstruction: every level direction j>=2 vanishes\n\nFor EVERY legal data set (2),(6),\n\n                       w_j=0 for all2<=j<m.            (9)\n\nNo linear independence or sign assumption on the w_j is used. This is an\nactual interpolation obstruction, not a negative direction of a formal dual.\n\nProve(9) in descending order, starting with j=m-1. Assume w_ell=0 for every\nell>j and set\n\n                  i=2^(j-1), l=i+1, p=p_ij.\n\nFor j>=2 these are both actual queries before the first d_j step is executed.\nEvery lower mode has already vanished by (3), so\n\n R_i=p w_j, R_l=p' w_j,\n p>0, p'=p(1-d_0/d_j)>0, s_l=s_i+d_0.                (10)\n\nThe last equality uses h_i=d_0: index2^(j-1) is even, so the next ruler\nentry is level zero. All higher mode vectors vanish by the induction.\n\nThe two ACTUAL ordered inequalities (7) for i,l give\n\n p<w_j,y_l-y_i><=0,   -p'<w_j,y_l-y_i><=0.\n\nSince p,p'>0, their scalar common factor must be zero. Expanding the actual\npositions y gives\n\n <w_j,v>=-(1-1/d_j)p||w_j||^2.                        (11)\n\nAt the last queried index q, all modes have vanished, so y_q=u-Sv. The\nactual REMOTE inequality (7) from i to q is therefore\n\n 0>=<w_j,y_q-y_i>\n   =-(S-s_i)<w_j,v>-(d_j-1)p||w_j||^2\n   =(d_j-1)p||w_j||^2[(S-s_i)/d_j-1],                (12)\n\nwhere (11) was substituted and the positive p factor in R_i was divided out.\nBut S-s_i>d_j: the retained suffix after query i includes the later first\nd_j step and also the positive step h_i=d_0. The first d_j occurs before q,\neven for j=m-1. Thus every factor in the last expression except ||w_j||^2\nis strictly positive. It follows that w_j=0, closing the induction.\n\nThe remote pair i,q is load-bearing. Keeping only the adjacent pair would\ngive (11), not the contradiction. The residual's nonzero negative correlation\nwith v is explicitly allowed until the remote queried inequality rules it out.\n\nAfter (9), (3) implies\n\n                         g_i=v for EVERY i>=2.        (13)\n\nAlthough the proposed temporal basis grew with m, legality has collapsed its\nactive directions to the two lowest levels and the double-zero block.\n\n## 4. Quantitative full-class objective and an actual nonquadratic calibration\n\nLet\n\n        T_mass=S-d_0-d_1=sum H_m-2d_0-d_1.\n\nFor every b in[0,2], every legal transcript in this class and EVERY convex\n1-smooth interpolant of its queried jets satisfy\n\n       2F(x_q-b g_q)<=1/[2T_mass+1+b(2-b)].            (14)\n\nThe first two actual steps are d_0 in(11/8,3/2) and d_1=2-1/m in(0,2).\nAdding the two optimum interpolation inequalities gives <g_i,x_i>>=||g_i||^2.\nThus for h in[0,2],\n\n ||x_i-hg_i||^2<=||x_i||^2-h(2-h)||g_i||^2<=||x_i||^2.\n\nIt follows that ||x_2||<=1. By(13), x_q=x_2-T_mass v. The optimum inequality\nat q and final-step smoothness then imply, with t=||v||,\n\n 2F(x_q-bv)<=2<v,x_2>-[2T_mass+1+b(2-b)]||v||^2\n            <=2t-[2T_mass+1+b(2-b)]t^2.\n\nCompleting the scalar square proves(14). This includes the original b=d_0\nand does not constrain the gradient at the unqueried final point.\n\nThe class is nonempty, genuinely nonquadratic, and asymptotically attains the\nHuber scale. Put S_total=sum H_m, p=1/(2S_total+1), x_0=1, u=1, v=p and\nall w_j=0. Choose C=[p,1] in R. Formula(8) gives\n\n                   F_C(x)=dist(x,[p,1])^2/2+px-p^2/2.\n\nThis single function is convex and1-smooth with UNIQUE minimizer0: the face\nminimizing pc over C is the singleton{p}. Every actual iterate of H_m lies in\n[p,1], since x_i=1-p sum_(t<i)h_t and x_n=(S_total+1)/(2S_total+1)>p.\nAll queried gradients are p, all values have the required form(6), the\ninitial distance is exactly1, and the normalized final objective is\n\n                            2F_C(x_n)=1/(2S_total+1).  (15)\n\nNo quadratic with minimum at0 has the same nonzero gradient at two distinct\nscalar queries. Thus the data are nonquadratic. This is a calibration of the\nobstruction, not a new improved lower-bound mechanism. Combining(14),(15)\nshows the exact supremum over this class is Huber-tight up to a factor\n1+O(1/S_total) at the original final step.\n\n## 5. Comparison with the EXACT B_n, with every new bound proved\n\nKeep B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n exactly as defined in accepted\nb98bb807d514e80d. For this class the exact ratio is bounded by\n\n                     B_n/[2T_mass+1+b(2-b)].          (16)\n\nHere is a self-contained sufficient upper envelope for this exact expression;\nit is not a replacement of the target profile. Put rho=log_2 lambda>1 and\nC_k=S_circle(k)+1. The accepted recursions are\n\n C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l),\n G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n C_0=B_0=1.\n\nWe first prove C_n<=(n+1)^rho. For t>0 define D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and\ng(z)=log D(exp z). Direct differentiation, with t=exp z, gives\n\n g''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)\n           [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/D(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive because it is zero at t=0 and has positive derivative\nrho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1] for t>0. Hence g(z)+g(-z)>=2g(0), or\n\n D(t)D(1/t)>=(2^rho-1)^2=2.\n\nFor x,y>0 put u=x^(1/rho), w=y^(1/rho), Z=(u+w)^rho. This inequality gives\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy, so Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y, by the increasing\nderivative of (u+w)^rho-u^rho in u. Thus Z lies above the positive quadratic\nroot Omega(x,y), proving Omega(u^rho,w^rho)<=(u+w)^rho. Zero arguments follow\nby substitution. Induction in the C recurrence proves the claimed envelope.\n\nNext sqrt(y^2+8xy)<=y+4x for x,y>=0 gives G(x,y)<=y+4x. Using\nl^rho+(k+1)^rho<=(l+k+1)^rho for rho>=1, induction proves\n\n                         B_n<=1+4n^rho.               (17)\n\nAll recursions and constants refer to the actual B_n; no unverified comparator\nfrom another finding is being adopted as a lemma.\n\nCounting the ruler levels gives\n\n 1+S_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j, 0<=j<=m,\n A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1), A_mass->1.\n\nIndeed ac<1=lambda(lambda-2)<c(c-2), proving A_mass<1. Since\nc^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m->exp(1) and2/lambda<1,\n\n             S_total/lambda^m->exp(1),\n             T_mass/lambda^m->exp(1).                 (18)\n\nAt n=2^m-1, (17) gives B_n<=1+4lambda^m. Equations(16)--(18) therefore imply\n\n limsup_(m->infinity) B_n sup_class 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n                               <=2/exp(1)<1,          (19)\n\nuniformly for b in[0,2]. This completes a full obstruction at the EXACT\nbenchmark scale for the selected tilted-projection growing-span class.\nIt is not a theorem about all legal data in the double-zero quotient(1).\n\n## 6. What survives: positive joint heights and their exact first charge\n\nThe collapse proof depends precisely on the value law(6). It does not extend\nautomatically to arbitrary jointly legal values, including positive offsets.\nThe surviving interface can be stated exactly without dropping any pairs.\n\nFor arbitrary legal values in (2), let\n\n C_min=f_q-<v,x_q>,\n delta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||^2/2,\n delta_star=-C_min-||v||^2/2.\n\nThe tilted function F(x)-<v,x> has gradient zero at every constant-gradient\nsuffix query and therefore has global minimum C_min there. Its smooth-convex\noptimum inequality implies delta_i>=0 for every query, delta_star>=0, and\ndelta_i=0 on the constant-gradient suffix. At star set R_star=-v,y_star=v.\nThe ENTIRE original interpolation system becomes\n\n              delta_i-delta_j+<R_i,y_j-y_i><=0         (20)\n\nfor all ordered pairs, together with the stated relation defining\ndelta_star. The scalar C_min, all these heights and all common vectors belong\nto the SAME function. Independent mode-wise values cannot replace them.\n\nHere is a quantitative necessary cost showing how a height can avoid the\nzero-height collapse. Suppose some level j>=2 is isolated at the two queries\ni=2^(j-1), l=i+1 as in (10), so higher mode vectors are zero. Write\n\n d=d_j, D=d-1, Delta=d_0, p=p_ij, p'=p(1-Delta/d),\n Vw=<v,w_j>, Bw=||w_j||^2, T_i=S-s_i>d,\n zeta=Vw+(D/d)p Bw.\n\nExpanding (20) for both i,l gives EXACTLY\n\n       Delta p' zeta<=delta_i-delta_l<=Delta p zeta.   (21)\n\nSince p>p'>0, this forces zeta>=0. The actual remote pair i,q gives\n\n              delta_i<=p[T_i zeta-Dp Bw(T_i/d-1)].     (22)\n\nAs delta_i>=0, (22) implies\nzeta>=(D/d)p Bw(1-d/T_i). Combining with delta_l>=0 and(21) proves\n\n delta_i>=Delta p'p (D/d)(1-d/T_i)||w_j||^2.           (23)\n\nEvery displayed factor in this charge is strictly positive. At zero heights,\nit forces w_j=0, agreeing with Section3. With arbitrary positive heights it\nis a necessary cost, not an impossibility theorem: that cost could be paid\nby the same global function's value budget. No bound on that simultaneous\nbudget, and no benchmark-scale construction paying it, has yet been proved.\n\nThe entire isolated right block gives a stronger, scale-dependent charge.\nLet I=2^(j-1), J=2^j-1, assume all higher w_ell vanish, and write\np_t=P_t(mu_j), p_I=P_I(mu_j), p_J=P_J(mu_j), T_J=S-s_J. Every query from I through J has\nresidual p_t w_j, and all the intervening steps comprise the actual H_(j-1).\nFor each such step h_t, the same two-pair calculation gives\n\n delta_t-delta_(t+1)\n    >=h_t p_(t+1) [Vw+(D/d)p_t Bw].\n\nThe remote inequality J,q and delta_J>=0 give Vw>=-D p_J Bw/T_J.\nSince p_t>=p_J and T_J>d, summing yields\n\n delta_I >= (D/d)(1-d/T_J) Bw sum_(t=I)^(J-1)h_t p_t p_(t+1).\n\nEvery intervening h_t/d is at most4/5. Hence p_(t+1)>=p_t/5, while\np_t^2-p_(t+1)^2<=(2h_t/d)p_t^2. The last sum is at least\n(d/10)(p_I^2-p_J^2). Therefore\n\n delta_I >= (d-1)(1-d/T_J)(p_I^2-p_J^2)||w_j||^2/10.  (24)\n\nIn particular there is an absolute explicit lower charge at the scale d_j:\n\n                delta_(2^(j-1))>=exp(-28)d_j||w_j||^2/320.  (25)\n\nHere are bounds establishing this constant without a computation. The word\nidentities in Section5 give S_j< c^j, while d_j>(3/8)c^j; thus S_j/d_j<8/3.\nEvery step before d_j is at most (4/5)d_j. Integrating the derivative of\nlog(1-t) gives log(1-t)>=-5t for0<=t<=4/5, so\np_J>=exp(-40/3)>exp(-14). Also\np_J/p_I=P_(H_(j-1))(mu_j)<=exp(-S_(j-1)/d_j)<=exp(-4/25),\nbecause S_(j-1)>=d_(j-2) and d_j<c^2 d_(j-2)<(25/4)d_(j-2).\nThe elementary bound exp(8/25)>=1+8/25+(8/25)^2/2>4/3 proves\np_I^2-p_J^2>=p_J^2/4>=exp(-28)/4.\n\nThe retained suffix after the first d_j includes d_j and at least\nS_j-d_0>d_(j-1)>(2/5)d_j of further mass, even if its last d_0 is omitted.\nThus T_J>(7/5)d_j and1-d_j/T_J>2/7>1/4. Finally d_j>2 for j>=2, so\nd_j-1>d_j/2. Substituting these three bounds into(24) proves(25).\nIt charges the largest active isolated level to a positive value height.\nIt does not yet add charges over lower levels in the presence of higher\nnonzero vectors, and it does not bound the initial common value budget.\n\nFor clarity, on the constant-gradient suffix all values are exactly\nf_i=f_q+(S-s_i)||v||^2, because adding and reversing the interpolation\ninequalities between two equal-gradient queries forces equality. For each old\nanchor the two directional Q expressions are constant or affine in s_i.\nThus retaining BOTH endpoints of that suffix suffices for its comparisons\nwith any earlier anchor; every earlier/earlier pair is still required.\nThe same endpoint statement holds for the maximum of conjugate jet quadratics\nin the explicit realization. It does not delete any remote earlier pair.\n\nThe original problem remains unresolved. The next concrete question in this\nlane is whether nonzero joint heights can pay (21)--(25) through multiple\nlevels while preserving every old pair and ||u+sum d_jw_j||=1, and produce\nthe required terminal objective relative to the actual B_n. Another\nzero-height or independent-value construction cannot answer that question.\n\nThe earlier no-linear-term squared-distance/cone exclusions do not cover\nthis proposal: the actual gradients here are v+R_i and the minimum belongs\nto the tilted function (8). The proof instead uses its exact ordered\ninterpolation law. Matlas retrieval for collinear normals returned a\nself-contracted-curve lemma and cone projection results; none is imported.\nNo additional external theorem is needed, since the branch reduced to the\nalready audited all-pair interface in fact1252 and the elementary projection\ncalculation explicitly proved above. {"id": "816b673b5988f6c3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:19:54.767973+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C57 completed scoped candidate: arbitrary-dimensional nonlinear auxiliary repairs ordered by any acute cone have SHARP leading payoff lim M_m V_m=1/2 on the actual critical word while retaining the C55 scalar queried transcript. An explicit one-ramp-per-cut construction makes every original interpolation pair legal with UNIQUE minimizer0; a Huber coordinate proves the matching class lower scale. The queried defect forces true squared distance >=4D(S-b0-b1)^2/b1² when0 is closest, and arbitrary values/free terminal completion obey payoff <=1/(2S+1-b0²)+3/(S-b0-b1)^2. This is a class obstruction, not an unrestricted W bound.", "evidence": "# C57: nonlinear ordered repairs have exactly the Huber leading scale\n\nStatus: **complete scoped proof candidate; not verifier-accepted**.\nNo optimization, installation, or fact submission is allocated. The only\naccepted mathematical predecessor used below is `1252b8823bb5a290`, for\nthe necessity and sufficiency of smooth-convex interpolation inequalities\nand their explicit conjugate interpolant. Everything specific to the\ntrajectory, the repair, and its quantitative bound is proved here.\n\nThis continues the actual-primal lane. It enlarges the previous single\nmonotone quadratic auxiliary coordinate to arbitrarily many nonlinear\ncoordinates, and even to vectors ordered by any fixed acute cone. It gives\nan explicit all-pair legal repair and identifies the sharp leading payoff\nof the whole stated class. It does not upper-bound unrestricted W_n(H_m).\n\n## 1. The actual schedule and the scalar data that are kept\n\nFix an integer m>=128, and define\n\n    epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n    d_j=a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j).\n\nLet h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum_(t<n)h_t, M=S+1. Define\n\n    b0=1+a, b1=1+d_1=2-epsilon.\n\nThe rightmost three steps are (b0,b1,b0). Every step exceeds one.\nIn particular S>=n, so S tends to infinity with m. The elementary bounds\nneeded below are\n\n    1<b0<sqrt(2), 1<b1<2, 7/3<c<5/2, d_1+c>3.       (1)\n\nIndeed a<(1+sqrt(2))^(-1)=sqrt(2)-1; d_1=1-epsilon;\nc>1+sqrt(2)>12/5; and, using sqrt(2)<10/7,\nc<(17/7)(129/128)<5/2. Since epsilon<=1/128, these also\ngive d_1+c>3.\n\nHere is a complete definition of the scalar formal transcript. Put\n\n    M_2=1+2b0+b1, k_2=M_2+6epsilon,\n    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j, j>=2.\n\nFor an H_2 block with terminal scalar gradient v, use successive gradients\n\n    ((2-c+6epsilon/b0)v, v, c v, v),                 (2)\n\nstart at k_2 v, and use the three actual GD steps (b0,b1,b0).\nThe last entry in (2) is the block's endpoint gradient. Its endpoint is v,\nbecause\n\n    b0(2-c+6epsilon/b0)+b1+b0 c+1=k_2.\n\nFor H_(j+1), give the right H_j child terminal gradient v and the left\nchild terminal gradient -k_j v/d_j; start the parent at k_(j+1)v.\nThe left endpoint is -k_j v/d_j. The bridge 1+d_j moves it to k_j v,\nthe right child's required start. The right endpoint is v. This verifies\nthe full chronological GD recursion inductively.\n\nChoose root terminal gradient 1. Denote the resulting scalar positions\nby x_i and gradients by g_i, 0<=i<=n. Thus\n\n    x_0=k_m, x_n=g_n=1,\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.\n\nSet y_i=x_i-g_i; in particular y_n=0. No legality is asserted for these\nscalar data. We keep x_i for all i, and g_i for the QUERIED indices i<n,\nwhen adding auxiliary coordinates. The final gradient and value may be\nchosen freely by a legal interpolating function.\n\nThe two final queried indices r=n-2 and s=n-1 satisfy\n\n    g_r=1, g_s=c, x_r-x_s=b1, x_s=1+b0 c.\n\nFor the normalized interpolation residual\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2,\n\ntheir scalar contribution to Q_rs+Q_sr is, for EVERY choice of values,\n\n    D=(c-1)^2+b1(c-1)=(c-1)(d_1+c)>4.              (3)\n\nThis pair is inside the queried prefix. Terminal completion cannot\ndelete it.\n\n## 2. The ordered auxiliary class, including its distance convention\n\nWork in any finite auxiliary Euclidean dimension q>=1, which may depend\narbitrarily on m. Let K be a convex cone that is acute:\n\n    <u,v>>=0 for every u,v in K.\n\nNo assertion is made that every cone has this property. The nonnegative\northant is an example. Consider data\n\n    X_i=(x_i,z_i), 0<=i<=n,\n    G_i=(g_i,p_i), 0<=i<n,\n    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i p_i.\n\nRequire\n\n    z_i in K for 0<=i<=n,\n    p_i in K for 0<=i<n,\n    p_t-p_i in K for every 0<=t<i<n.                (4)\n\nThese are constraints on the auxiliary coordinates of one JOINT\ntrajectory. They do not require a separable objective, independent\none-dimensional auxiliary functions, a fixed rank, a quadratic model,\nor prescribed function values. For the orthant, (4) means nonnegative\npositions and nonnegative, coordinatewise nonincreasing queried gradients.\n\nAssume these queried jets arise from ONE convex differentiable 1-smooth\nfunction F with F(0)=0=min F. Initially write\n\n    R^2=||X_0||^2=k_m^2+||z_0||^2,\n    P=2F(X_n)/R^2.\n\nThe bound below always holds with this specified-minimizer normalization.\nFor a statement about the true closest-minimizer distance, explicitly\nrequire that 0 is a closest minimizer to X_0. The constructions in\nSections 4 and 5 have a UNIQUE minimizer 0, so their R is exactly the\ntrue distance. No automatic closest-minimizer claim is made for arbitrary\nother interpolants.\n\nThe final gradient is unrestricted. In particular the class allows the\npointwise maximal unqueried-terminal completion of any legal prefix\nthat satisfies (4), whenever the stated normalization condition holds.\n\n## 3. A uniform upper bound for the entire class\n\nPut\n\n    N=S-b0-b1=sum_(t<r)h_t,\n    C=1+2b0-b0^2,\n    A=2c+(b0^2-1)c^2,\n    D0=2S+1-b0^2.\n\nAll of N,C,D0 are positive, and 0<A<12 by (1).\n\nFirst add the two necessary interpolation inequalities for r and s.\nWrite Delta=p_r-p_s. The actual step has auxiliary displacement\nz_r-z_s=b1 p_r. Combining its contribution with (3) gives\n\n    D<=b1<p_r,Delta>-||Delta||^2\n       =b1^2||p_r||^2/4-||Delta-(b1/2)p_r||^2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    ||p_r||^2>=4D/b1^2.                             (5)\n\nThis necessary bound uses no ordering assumptions and no function values.\nThe ordering assumption next makes the cost accumulate over the actual\nearlier steps:\n\n    z_0-N p_r=z_r+sum_(t<r)h_t(p_t-p_r) in K.\n\nSince p_r is in K and K is acute, expansion of the squared norm yields\n\n    ||z_0||^2>=N^2||p_r||^2,\n    R^2>=4D N^2/b1^2>4N^2.                         (6)\n\nFor the objective, interpolation at the last query with the optimum and\nsmoothness on the actual last step give, respectively,\n\n    F(X_s)<=<G_s,X_s>-||G_s||^2/2,\n    F(X_n)<=F(X_s)-b0(1-b0/2)||G_s||^2.\n\nThese are necessary for every legal completion, with no terminal gradient\nassumed. Adding and inserting the displayed scalar last-query data gives\n\n    2F(X_n)<=A+2<p_s,z_s>-C||p_s||^2.               (7)\n\nFor t<s, acuteness and p_t-p_s in K imply\n<p_t,p_s>>=||p_s||^2. Therefore\n\n    <z_s,p_s>\n      =<z_0,p_s>-sum_(t<s)h_t<p_t,p_s>\n      <=<z_0,p_s>-(S-b0)||p_s||^2.\n\nEquation (7) becomes\n\n    2F(X_n)<=A+2<z_0,p_s>-D0||p_s||^2\n             <=A+||z_0||^2/D0,                     (8)\n\nwhere the final inequality is the exact completion of the square in p_s.\nDividing by R^2 and using (6) proves the uniform bound\n\n    P <= 1/(2S+1-b0^2) + A b1^2/(4D N^2)\n      < 1/(2S+1-b0^2) + 3/(S-b0-b1)^2.             (9)\n\nSince b0,b1 stay bounded and M=S+1, it follows that\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) M P <= 1/2                 (10)\n\nuniformly over all dimensions, acute cones, jointly legal function values,\nand final completions in this class, with the distance convention stated\nin Section 2. This is stronger in scope than a fixed-curvature analysis:\nthere is no curvature parameter in (5)--(10).\n\n## 4. An explicit nonlinear repair of EVERY interpolation pair\n\nThe class in Section 2 is nonempty for the actual scalar formal transcript.\nHere is an analytic construction with a closed rule and a closed initial\ndistance formula. It is not a nearest-feasible-point optimization.\n\nDefine finite scalars\n\n    Gmax=max(1,max_(0<=i<=n)|g_i|),\n    w_l=Gmax|y_(l+1)-y_l|+1,\n    u_l=sqrt(2w_l/(h_l-1)),       0<=l<n.\n\nUse n auxiliary coordinates, one for each chronological cut l. In\ncoordinate l define the 1/h_l-smooth, convex, differentiable function\n\n    psi_l(t)=0                              if t<=0,\n             t^2/(2h_l)                    if 0<=t<=h_l u_l,\n             u_l t-h_l u_l^2/2              if t>=h_l u_l.\n\nIts two joining values and derivatives agree. Its derivative is\nnondecreasing and is Lipschitz with constant 1/h_l<1. Prescribe\n\n    z_i^(l)=u_l sum_(v=i)^l h_v, p_i^(l)=u_l,   if i<=l,\n    z_i^(l)=0,                  p_i^(l)=0,     if i>=l+1.\n\nThe auxiliary GD recursion holds at every step, including the extinction\nstep l: its position before that step is h_l u_l and its position after\nthe step is zero. Earlier sample positions lie in the affine branch of\npsi_l. The positions and gradients lie in the nonnegative orthant, and\neach gradient decreases exactly once, to zero. Thus (4) holds.\n\nNow prescribe full-vector jets and values, including the terminal,\n\n    X_i=(x_i,z_i), G_i=(g_i,p_i),\n    f_i=g_i^2/2+sum_(l<n)psi_l(z_i^(l)), 0<=i<=n,\n    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                      (11)\n\nThe scalar expression g_i^2/2 is not asserted to come from a legal\none-dimensional function. The final function will be a joint interpolant.\n\nHere is the complete interpolation law. The scalar part of Q_ij is\n\n    g_i(y_j-y_i).\n\nFor two finite indices i<j, an auxiliary coordinate with l<i or l>=j\ncontributes zero in BOTH directions. A cut i<=l<j contributes\n\n    Q_ij^(l)=-w_l,\n    Q_ji^(l)=-w_l-u_l^2 sum_(v=i)^(l-1)h_v.        (12)\n\nTo verify (12), the earlier point has gradient u_l and value\nu_l z_i^(l)-h_l u_l^2/2, whereas the later point has zero position,\ngradient, and value. Direct substitution into Q gives the two formulas;\nthe sum in the second is empty if l=i.\n\nThe triangle inequality supplies\n\n    |g_i(y_j-y_i)|, |g_j(y_i-y_j)|\n       <=Gmax sum_(l=i)^(j-1)|y_(l+1)-y_l|.\n\nBy the definition of w_l, (12) therefore makes BOTH full residuals at\nmost -(j-i)<0. This checks every distinct finite pair, including pairs\nwith the terminal; it is not only an adjacent-pair check.\n\nFor the optimum pair and i<n, the scalar contributions are\n\n    Q_(i,star)^scalar=-g_i y_i,\n    Q_(star,i)^scalar=0.\n\nBecause y_n=0, |g_i y_i|<=Gmax sum_(l=i)^(n-1)|y_(l+1)-y_l|.\nIn each l>=i the forward auxiliary contribution is -w_l, and the reverse\none is -w_l-u_l^2 sum_(v=i)^(l-1)h_v. All l<i contributions are zero.\nThus both optimum residuals are strictly negative. At the terminal,\nX_n=G_n=(1,0,...,0), f_n=1/2, and the two optimum residuals are zero.\nAll ordered interpolation inequalities for (11) have now been checked.\n\nFor completeness the actual function is explicit. Let\n\n    Y_i=X_i-G_i, delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2,\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in {star,0,...,n})\n                            [<Y_i,v>-delta_i],\n    F_base(X)=sup_v[<X,v>-H(v)].                    (13)\n\nBy accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, this is an everywhere finite convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth function with all the prescribed jets and the\nsame actual GD trajectory. It has a UNIQUE minimizer 0, as can also be\nchecked from (13): for i<n, the l=i term contributes w_i>0 to delta_i,\nand every other term is nonnegative. Hence delta_i>0. The star and terminal\nhave Y=0 and delta=0. In a neighborhood of v=0 their zero affine function\nstrictly dominates every nonzero queried affine function. Thus H(v)\nequals ||v||^2/2 in that neighborhood and its subgradient at 0 is {0}.\nThe maximizing-vector characterization of (13), proved in the accepted\npredecessor, identifies the minimizers of F_base with this subgradient.\n\nConsequently the true squared initial distance is exactly\n\n    R_base^2=k_m^2+\n        sum_(l=0)^(n-1) [2w_l/(h_l-1)]\n                              [sum_(v=0)^l h_v]^2. (14)\n\nThe exact normalized objective is 1/R_base^2. This pure extinction repair\nloses the formal large payoff: (6) yields 1/R_base^2<1/(4N^2).\nNevertheless it is a fully specified actual legal object with one common\nfunction, all values, all gradients, and the true initial distance.\n\n## 5. The coefficient 1/2 is sharp for the whole ordered repair class\n\nThe uniform upper bound (10) cannot be replaced by a smaller leading\nconstant for this class. Add one further coordinate to F_base, using the\nsymmetric Huber function with threshold tau>0,\n\n    Huber_tau(t)=t^2/2                     if |t|<=tau,\n                 tau|t|-tau^2/2            if |t|>=tau.\n\nThis function is convex and differentiable, its derivative is 1-Lipschitz,\nand its minimizer is uniquely 0. Choose any amplitude T>0 and put\n\n    tau=T/(2S+1), z_i^H=T-tau sum_(t<i)h_t.\n\nAll sampled positions, including the terminal, exceed tau because\nz_n^H=T(S+1)/(2S+1)>tau. All its queried gradients therefore equal tau.\nThe actual recursion and the nonnegative ordering requirements hold.\n\nOn the enlarged space use the direct sum\n\n    F_total(X,t)=F_base(X)+Huber_tau(t).\n\nIt is convex and 1-smooth in the product Euclidean norm, as follows by\nsquaring and adding the separate gradient Lipschitz inequalities. Its\nminimizer is uniquely 0, and its first-coordinate queried transcript is\nunchanged. Its exact normalized payoff and true distance are\n\n    P_T=[1+T^2/(2S+1)]/[R_base^2+T^2].              (15)\n\nFor fixed m, letting T tend to infinity yields 1/(2S+1). For an explicit\nhorizon-dependent choice take T^2=m R_base^2, which gives\n\n    P_T>= [m/(m+1)]/(2S+1).\n\nLet V_m denote the supremum of the true-distance-normalized payoff over\nthe ordered repair class of Section 2 with 0 a closest minimizer. The\nexplicit unique-minimizer examples above and (9) give\n\n    1/(2S+1) <= V_m\n       <= 1/(2S+1-b0^2)+3/(S-b0-b1)^2,\n\nand therefore the exact class asymptotic\n\n    lim_(m->infinity) M V_m = 1/2.                 (16)\n\nThe lower endpoint is a supremum assertion; no attainment at finite T is\nclaimed. The explicit T choice already suffices for the limiting lower\nbound. This calibrates the obstruction against an actual legal function.\n\n## 6. What a non-ordered repair would have to change\n\nThere are exact identities that expose the assumptions used above.\nFor arbitrary auxiliary positions and gradients obeying GD, define\n\n    J=sum_(t<s)h_t <p_t-p_s,p_s>.\n\nEquation (7), with no ordering assumption, gives\n\n    2F(X_n)<=A+2<z_0,p_s>-D0||p_s||^2-2J\n             <=A+||z_0||^2/D0-2J.                 (17)\n\nFor the earlier queried gradient define\n\n    B=<z_r,p_r>+sum_(t<r)h_t<p_t-p_r,p_r>.\n\nThe exact GD identity is\n\n    <z_0,p_r>=N||p_r||^2+B.                        (18)\n\nIn the ordered class J>=0 and B>=0. Equations (17)--(18) hold without\nthat class restriction, whereas the conclusions J>=0 and B>=0 do not.\nFor example, retaining the unit formal terminal payoff with R^2 of order\nM would require, by (5),(18) and Cauchy--Schwarz,\n\n    B <= R||p_r||-N||p_r||^2,\n\nwhich is negative for all sufficiently large m whenever R=O(sqrt(M)).\nThus such a normalization-saving repair must supply genuine cancellation\nin its cumulative positions/gradients, or modify the scalar queried data.\nSimilarly, if R^2/M tends to infinity and a family has M P>=1+eta for\nsome fixed eta>0, then (17) forces\n\n    limsup [M J/R^2] <= -1/4-eta/2.                 (19)\n\nTo check (19), divide (17) by R^2, use ||z_0||^2<=R^2,\nM/D0->1/2, and M A/R^2->0. This statement is a necessary condition,\nnot an existence claim for an escaping legal repair.\n\nThe original all-schedule profile goal remains open. Equation (16) only\ndiagnoses this fully specified repair class on this critical positive\nschedule. It does not address arbitrary signed or turning auxiliary\ngradients, changed first-coordinate queried trajectories, all possible\nminimizer locations, or unrestricted smooth convex adversaries. It does\nnot replace the original zero-step or large-step quantifiers, and is not\na theorem about W_n(H_m). All new conclusions here remain unverified\ndraft evidence, pending an actual authorized verifier submission.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["c6817249281e382c"], "artifact": "research/c57_ordered_nonlinear_repair.md", "status": "complete joint draft, unverified"}, "glossary": {"C57": "Candidate acute-cone ordered nonlinear auxiliary repair branch", "K": "An acute convex cone in the auxiliary Euclidean space, so all inner products of its vectors are nonnegative", "V_m": "Supremum of the normalized payoff over the explicitly stated ordered auxiliary repair class with0 a closest minimizer", "R_base": "Exact initial distance for the explicit cut-ramp conjugate interpolant", "Gmax": "Maximum of one and the absolute values of all scalar formal gradients", "w_l": "Cut-ramp weight Gmax times absolute proximal-coordinate increment plus one", "u_l": "Positive cut-ramp gradient magnitude sqrt(2w_l/(h_l-1))", "J": "Cumulative last-query auxiliary correlation sum h_t<p_t-p_s,p_s>", "B": "Earlier-query auxiliary cancellation balance <z_r,p_r>+sum h_t<p_t-p_r,p_r>"}}
{"id": "28db18b9be162b29", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:35:49.309512+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "JOINT POSITIVE-HEIGHT policy closed at the exact benchmark scale: in the double-zero plus all resonant-level quotient, use ONE anisotropic metric-distance realization with A>=I and condition number<=2, all normals N_ij<=0, and heights delta_i=<R_i,(A-I)R_i>/2. Arbitrary common cross terms and initial correlations are retained. Highest active j>=2 forces <w_j,A w_j>>=d_j||w_j||², so all preceding steps are <=2 lambda_min(A) and the prefix cannot expand distance. EVERY 1-smooth terminal completion, for every b in[0,2], has 2F<=1/[2S_(m-1)+d_(m-1)/2-2d_0]. Independently rederived exact-B comparison gives limsup B_n payoff<=2lambda²/[e(lambda+2)]<123/124<1. Complete unverified draft; arbitrary heights, weaker Q-only legality and unbounded metric conditioning remain open.", "evidence": "# Joint positive heights with one anisotropic distance metric\n\nStatus: complete scoped UNVERIFIED proof candidate. No numerical optimization,\ninstallation, or fact submission. The fact inputs are 1252b8823bb5a290\n(smooth-convex interpolation and maximal completion) and b98bb807d514e80d\n(the exact benchmark definitions). Every other assertion used below is proved\ninside this package. In particular the sharper benchmark comparison is proved\nagain, rather than treating draft e3c8501149a5c47e as an accepted predecessor.\n\nThe class has ONE common positive-height matrix, ONE function, arbitrary\ncross-mode and initial inner products, and all normal comparisons. It is a\nspecific policy for the open positive-height interface, not that entire\ninterface. The extra normal inequalities below are stronger than ordinary\n1-smooth interpolation with the same displayed heights.\n\n## 1. The physical word and the complete joint construction\n\nFix an integer m>=128 and keep these parameters fixed throughout the horizon:\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n n=2^m-1, q=n-1, S_j=sum H_j.\n\nThe queried prefix h_0,...,h_(q-1) is H_m with its last d_0 removed. The\nunqueried final step is b in [0,2], including the original b=d_0. Set\n\n s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=s_q=S_m-d_0,\n p_ij=product_(t<i)(1-h_t/d_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nHere d_0<1+1/lambda=sqrt(2)<3/2 and d_1=2-1/m<2. Also c>lambda>2,\nso all d_j are strictly increasing and positive.\n\nFor arbitrary vectors u,v,w_0,...,w_(m-1) in a finite-dimensional REAL\nEuclidean space define\n\n R_i=sum_j p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_j d_j p_ij w_j,\n x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, ||x_0||=||u+Z_0||=1.       (1)\n\nThere is no independence, orthogonality, or sign assumption on these vectors.\nTheir Gram matrix is any real PSD matrix, including singular ones. These are\nthe exact images of the polynomial quotient\n\n                  z^2 product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-1/d_j).\n\nIndeed its evaluation coordinates p(0),p'(0),p(1/d_j) are independent: a\npolynomial in their joint kernel is divisible by the displayed polynomial.\nThe functional T[p]=u p(0)+v p'(0)+sum_j d_j w_j p(1/d_j) therefore gives\nx_i=T[P_i], g_i=T[z P_i], where P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z). The identity\nP_(i+1)=(1-h_i z)P_i proves the exact GD recursion in (1).\n\nThe first d_j has step index 2^j-1. All preceding steps are smaller, and hence\n\n p_ij>0 for i<2^j, and p_ij=0 for i>=2^j.              (2)\n\nLet A be ONE selfadjoint positive definite operator and tau its smallest\neigenvalue. The policy to be tested requires\n\n                         I <= tau I <= A <= 2 tau I. (3)\n\nThus tau>=1. A may depend on m, have arbitrarily large scale, act in an\narbitrarily large space, and mix every mode. Its condition number is at most\ntwo; no common eigenbasis with the w_j is assumed.\n\nPut z_i=x_i-A R_i. At the optimum add\n\n x_star=0, g_star=0, R_star=-v, z_star=A v.\n\nRequire the ENTIRE normal array\n\n       N_ij=<R_i,z_j-z_i> <=0, i,j in {star,0,...,q}. (4)\n\nLet C=conv{z_star,z_0,...,z_q} and use the single actual function\n\n F_A(x)=min_(z in C) (x-z)^T A^(-1)(x-z)/2\n                         +<v,x>-<v,A v>/2.          (5)\n\nIts prescribed values are\n\n       f_i=<v,x_i>-<v,A v>/2+<R_i,A R_i>/2.           (6)\n\nThey all use the same matrix A and the same offset. The tilted minimum is\nC_min=-<v,A v>/2, because R_i=0 on the suffix after the highest active level.\nThe positive heights in the existing double-zero interface are exactly\n\n delta_i=(<R_i,A R_i>-||R_i||^2)/2,\n delta_star=(<v,A v>-||v||^2)/2.                     (7)\n\nAll these heights are nonnegative. In particular\n\n 2 delta_i=sum_(j,k) p_ij p_ik <w_j,(A-I)w_k>.\n\nEvery cross term is retained; (7) is not a sum of independently chosen legal\nmodal values. The matrix of these cross terms is PSD because A-I is PSD,\nbut individual entries can have either sign.\n\nFor the original signed 1-smooth interpolation residual from fact1252,\nsubstitution into (6) gives the exact ALL-pair law\n\n Q_ij=N_ij-<R_i-R_j,(A-I)(R_i-R_j)>/2 <=0.            (8)\n\nThe identity includes the optimum, whose value from (6) is zero. The proof\nbelow uses (4), not merely Q_ij<=0. Consequently its conclusion must not be\nextended to arbitrary legal values of form (7) without the normal condition.\n\n## 2. Function realization, effective smoothness, and actual minimizers\n\nFor each sample i, (4) gives <R_i,z-z_i><=0 for every z in C. Since\nx_i-z_i=A R_i,\n\n (x_i-z)^T A^(-1)(x_i-z)\n  =<R_i,A R_i>+(z-z_i)^T A^(-1)(z-z_i)-2<R_i,z-z_i>\n  >=<R_i,A R_i>.\n\nThus z_i is the unique A^(-1)-metric projection of x_i onto C, and (5)\ninterpolates the prescribed values and gradients. This also holds at star.\n\nHere is a direct smoothness proof. For arbitrary x,y let their projections\nbe z_x,z_y, and residuals r_x=A^(-1)(x-z_x), r_y=A^(-1)(y-z_y).\nAdding the two normal inequalities gives\n\n <r_x-r_y,z_x-z_y> >=0,\n <r_x-r_y,x-y> >= <r_x-r_y,A(r_x-r_y)>\n                                      >=tau||r_x-r_y||^2.   (9)\n\nCauchy--Schwarz shows ||r_x-r_y||<=||x-y||/tau. Comparing the two minimum\nexpressions at x and x+d, first with z_x and then with z_(x+d), proves\ndifferentiability of the squared metric distance with gradient r_x: the\nupper error after subtracting <r_x,d> is at most ||d||^2/(2tau), and the\nreverse comparison together with (9) bounds it below by\n-3||d||^2/(2tau). Joint convexity of the squared norm and convexity of C prove\nconvexity after minimizing in z. Consequently F_A is convex and has gradient\nLipschitz constant at most 1/tau<=1. At zero its gradient is -v+v=0 and its\nvalue is zero. It is everywhere finite.\n\nThe actual minimizer set is explicit. The star normal in (4) says\n<v,z>>=<v,A v> on C. Completing the square in (5) gives\n\n F_A(x)=min_(z in C){||x-(z-A v)||_(A^(-1))^2/2\n                                      +<v,z>-<v,A v>}.\n\nTherefore\n\n argmin F_A={z-A v:z in C, <v,z>=<v,A v>}.             (10)\n\nIf normalization uses the closest minimizer, require zero to be the closest\npoint in (10) to x_0, or require the exposed face in (10) to be the singleton\n{A v}. The upper result holds for every designated minimizer zero with\n||x_0||=1 and hence also when zero is the closest minimizer. The example\nbelow has a unique minimizer.\n\n## 3. The highest active level forces a common curvature budget\n\nLet j be the largest index for which w_j!=0. Suppose j>=2. Consider the TWO\nactual queried points i=2^(j-1) and l=i+1. By (2), all lower modes are already\nzero and all higher vectors are zero by the choice of j. Thus\n\n R_i=p w_j, R_l=p' w_j,\n p>0, p'=p(1-d_0/d_j)>0, s_l-s_i=d_0.                (11)\n\nWrite d=d_j, w=w_j, D=<w,(d I-A)w>, V=<v,w>. Then\n\n z_i=u-s_i v+p(d I-A)w,\n z_l=u-(s_i+d_0)v+p'(d I-A)w.\n\nBoth directions of (4) for i,l imply <w,z_l-z_i>=0, because their normals\nare positive multiples of w. Expanding this equality gives\n\n                            V=-p D/d.               (12)\n\nAt the last query q, every residual vanishes, so z_q=u-Sv. The RETAINED\nremote normal from i to q gives\n\n 0>=N_iq=p[-(S-s_i)V-pD]\n              =p^2 D[(S-s_i)/d-1].                 (13)\n\nThe actual retained suffix after i contains the future first d_j step and\nalso the positive step h_i=d_0. These occur before q, even for j=m-1.\nHence S-s_i>d. Equations (12)--(13) force\n\n                  <w,A w> >= d_j||w||^2,\n                  d_j <=2tau.                      (14)\n\nThis is a JOINT curvature cost, not a sum of single-mode height charges.\nOnly the last active level is isolated. All other cross terms remain in\nthe common A and in (4). No sign of <v,w_k> for other levels is inferred.\n\nFor j>=3 there is a third positive-collinear query i+2 before annihilation.\nApplying (12) to the next pair replaces p by p'!=p, so D=V=0. Thus the\nstronger equality <w,A w>=d_j||w||^2 holds then. This refinement is not\nneeded for the upper bound; the two-query argument (14) covers j=2 too.\n\n## 4. Whole-class terminal bound, including arbitrary 1-smooth completions\n\nThe effective smoothness of F_A and its optimum give, at every query,\n\n               <g_i,x_i> >=tau||g_i||^2.             (15)\n\nThis follows by adding the two interpolation inequalities at smoothness\n1/tau, a rescaling of the necessity calculation in fact1252. Therefore\n\n ||x_i-h_i g_i||^2\n     <=||x_i||^2-h_i(2tau-h_i)||g_i||^2.              (16)\n\nIf j>=2, every step up to and including the first d_j is at most d_j<=2tau.\nThe whole actual prefix up to r=2^j consequently has ||x_r||<=1. All residuals\nare zero from r onward, so with\n\n                 T=S-s_r=S_m-d_0-S_j-d_j,\n\none has x_q=x_r-Tv and g_q=v. The value inequality at q, again using the\neffective smoothness of F_A, gives\n\n                     f_q<=<v,x_q>-tau||v||^2/2.      (17)\n\nNow let F be ANY convex 1-smooth function interpolating all the prescribed\nprefix jets and the optimum, not necessarily F_A. It has the same f_q and\ng_q, so its unqueried last step obeys\n\n 2F(x_q-bv)<=2f_q-b(2-b)||v||^2\n       <=2<v,x_r>-[2T+tau+b(2-b)]||v||^2\n       <=1/[2T+tau+b(2-b)], 0<=b<=2.                 (18)\n\nThe last inequality is the scalar square completion 2t-D t^2<=1/D, with\nt=||v||, D=2T+tau+b(2-b)>0, and ||x_r||<=1. Thus the bound covers the\npointwise maximal 1-smooth completion supplied by accepted fact1252. No\neffective-smoothness assumption is imposed on the unqueried extension.\n\nUsing tau>=d_j/2, and the increase of both S_j and d_j with j, (18) gives\n\n 2T+tau+b(2-b)\n  >=2S_m-2d_0-2S_j-3d_j/2\n  >=2S_(m-1)+d_(m-1)/2-2d_0 =: D_m.                (19)\n\nIf no j>=2 is active, every residual vanishes at query2. The first two\nsteps are d_0<3/2 and d_1=2-1/m<2, while tau>=1, so (16) gives ||x_2||<=1.\nThe same proof of (18) works with T=S_m-2d_0-d_1. Its denominator is at least\n2S_m-4d_0-2d_1+1. Subtracting D_m gives\n\n       2S_(m-1)+3d_(m-1)/2-2d_0-2d_1+1>0,\n\nbecause S_(m-1)>=S_2=2d_0+d_1. This also covers v=0 and the all-zero\nresidual case. All quantities in D_m are positive as needed for m>=128.\n\nWe have proved the UNIFORM whole-policy upper bound\n\n     sup_(all data satisfying (1)--(4), all F completions, b in[0,2])\n                      2F(x_q-b g_q) <=1/D_m.         (20)\n\nNo prescribed values, old interpolation pairs, initial correlations, or\nterminal completion variables have been discarded in defining the class.\n\n## 5. Strict comparison with the exact Zhang--Jiang benchmark\n\nKeep B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n exactly as in accepted b98bb807d514e80d.\nSet P_n=S_circle(n)+1. Algebra in those recursions gives\n\n P_0=B_0=1,\n P_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),\n B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l),\n Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nHere is a full proof of the needed comparator. Put alpha=2lambda-1\n=lambda(4-lambda)>0 and Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Its coefficients exceed\none, so Llin>=x+y. Direct expansion using lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives\n\n alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]\n                         =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.\n\nSince Omega is the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy=0, Llin>=Omega(x,y).\nAt z=lambda Omega(x,y), the quadratic whose larger root is G(x,lambda y)\ntherefore satisfies\n\n z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2\n                =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.\n\nAlso z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, placing it to the right of the\nquadratic's vertex. Thus G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y) for x,y>=0.\nMonotonicity of G in y and induction prove\n\n                             B_n<=lambda P_n.       (21)\n\nFor completeness put rho=log_2 lambda>1, Dpow(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and\nell(z)=log Dpow(exp z). With t=exp z, differentiation gives\n\n ell''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)\n          [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/Dpow(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is zero at t=0 and has positive derivative for t>0. Convexity\nof ell implies Dpow(t)Dpow(1/t)>=Dpow(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 and\nZ=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho, this implies\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy, so Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y, as follows by\nintegrating the increasing derivative of t^rho. Hence Omega(x,y)<=Z;\nzero arguments follow by substitution. Induction in P gives\nP_n<=(n+1)^rho. Balanced joins at n=2^m-1 give equality there. Consequently\n\n                         B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).   (22)\n\nThe exact physical mass satisfies\n\n 1+S_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,\n A_mass=a/(c-2) ->1.\n\nThis follows by solving 1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a c^j with S_0=0.\nSince c^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m->exp(1),\n\n S_(m-1)/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1),\n d_(m-1)/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1)/lambda,\n D_m/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1)(lambda+2)/2.               (23)\n\nCombining the EXACT ratio bound B_n/D_m with (22)--(23) proves\n\n limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup_policy,completions,b 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n                <=2lambda^2/[exp(1)(lambda+2)]<1.    (24)\n\nThe strict gap requires no decimal calculation. The exponential series gives\nexp(1)>8/3, and lambda<17/7<5/2. Thus the right side of (24) is strictly less\nthan 3lambda^2/[4(lambda+2)]=(6lambda+3)/(4lambda+8)<123/124.\nThe middle rational function is increasing, as its derivative is\n36/(4lambda+8)^2>0. All comparisons retain the exact B_n; no benchmark\nasymptotic equality was assumed.\n\n## 6. Positive-height nonquadratic calibration at the largest actual level\n\nThe policy is not forced to have zero heights or constant queried gradients.\nThis calibration is not a sharp lower adversary and is not offered as one.\nLet d=d_(m-1), tau=d/2>1, U=sqrt(3)/2, epsilon=1/(2d),\n\n A=diag(d,tau), v=(0,p), p=U/(2S_m+tau),\n u=(0,U), w_(m-1)=(epsilon,0), w_j=0 for j<m-1.\n\nThen x_0=(1/2,U) has norm1, A obeys (3), and\n\n x_i=(d epsilon p_(i,m-1), U-s_i p),\n g_i=(epsilon p_(i,m-1),p),\n z_i=(0,U-s_i p), z_star=(0,tau p).\n\nThe second coordinate stays in [tau p,U], including the final iterate,\nbecause U-S_m p=U(S_m+tau)/(2S_m+tau)>=tau p. Hence C is precisely the\nvertical segment {0} x [tau p,U]. Every query residual is horizontal and\northogonal to every difference of points in C; the star normal points\ndownward and also obeys (4). The ONE realizing function is\n\n F_A(x,y)=x^2/(2d)+dist(y,[tau p,U])^2/(2tau)+p y-tau p^2/2.\n\nIt is nonquadratic, convex, 1-smooth, and has UNIQUE minimizer (0,0), by\n(10). Its gradients vary at every query before the central annihilation,\nand all those queries have strictly positive height\n\n delta_i=(d-1)epsilon^2 p_(i,m-1)^2/2.\n\nThe original final objective and true initial distance are exactly\n\n                2F_A(x_n)=3/[4(2S_m+tau)], dist(x_0,argmin F_A)=1.\n\nThus the curvature cost in (14) is compatible with an actual positive-height\ntrajectory, but that cost prevents this entire metric policy from reaching\nthe benchmark constant. The example uses one active resonant mode; the upper\ntheorem permits all m of them and every common cross correlation. The example\ndoes not establish that all those modes can be independently activated.\n\n## 7. The remaining interface and the abandoned smaller policy\n\nThe conclusion is NOT a closure of arbitrary positive joint heights, nor of\narbitrary A. Even with the same quadratic height formula, retaining only\nthe weaker Q inequalities in (8) does not imply the normals (4). With an\narbitrarily ill-conditioned A, (14) no longer forces every earlier step to\nbe <=2tau, so the norm budget (16) no longer follows. These are the exact\ntwo missing hypotheses for extending this particular proof.\n\nThe preliminary symmetrized modal-energy policy in plan10bd4d79197b0379 set\nf_i=<v,x_i>+C+<R_i,Z_i>/2. Its correct joint law is\n\n Q_ij=-(s_j-s_i)<v,R_i>\n       -<R_i-R_j,Z_i-Z_j>/2+||R_i-R_j||^2/2\n       +(<R_i,Z_j>-<R_j,Z_i>)/2.\n\nThe final antisymmetric term must not be dropped. That policy was not proved\nlegal or excluded. The anisotropic distance policy (3)--(6) was selected to\nmake the full joint height payment and function realization explicit.\n\nMatlas retrieval for nilpotent trajectories and for weighted squared-distance\nsmoothness returned no theorem used here. The exact local text of Taylor,\nHendrickx and Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666, Corollary1 and the proof of Theorem4,\nwas read for context. No published theorem is an external premise: weighted\nprojection, smoothness, all-pair legality and the benchmark comparator have\nbeen proved above from finite algebra and the two accepted fact inputs.\n\nOriginal exact-profile target: unresolved. No new verified fact is claimed.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["468f3abd12846a0f", "10bd4d79197b0379", "e3c8501149a5c47e"], "artifact": "research/joint_positive_height_metric_obstruction.md", "scope": "one common anisotropic metric-distance positive-height policy with condition number at most2; includes every ordinary1-smooth completion"}, "glossary": {"A": "One common positive definite operator defining the metric-distance positive-height policy; I<=A<=2tau I.", "tau": "Smallest eigenvalue of A, at least1.", "R_i": "Residual queried gradient sum_j p_ij w_j, with R_star=-v.", "Z_i": "Residual position sum_j d_j p_ij w_j.", "z_i": "Metric proximal point x_i-A R_i, with z_star=A v.", "N_ij": "Full ordered normal comparison <R_i,z_j-z_i>.", "C": "Convex hull of all metric proximal points z_i and z_star.", "F_A": "Single tilted squared metric-distance function in equation5.", "D_m": "Finite upper-bound denominator 2S_(m-1)+d_(m-1)/2-2d_0.", "p_ij": "Exact temporal product product_(t<i)(1-h_t/d_j).", "S_j": "Actual total step mass of H_j; all horizon parameters fixed at m.", "P_n": "Exact primitive benchmark S_circle(n)+1, used only with recursions from accepted b98.", "w_j": "Arbitrary real residual coefficient of reciprocal node1/d_j.", "u": "Constant polynomial coordinate giving x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i.", "v": "Common gradient on the terminal constant-gradient suffix.", "delta_i": "Joint positive height <R_i,(A-I)R_i>/2; star height uses R_star=-v."}}
{"id": "30e88f2351b2aa4f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:42:25.264060+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact COMMON initial-value budget for the unrestricted joint-height double-zero interface: with eta=delta_star>=0, R0=sum w_j, Z0=sum d_j w_j, and ||x0||=1, Q_(0,star) is equivalent to eta>=delta0+||R0||²+<v,R0>-<R0,x0>. Consequently every theta in[0,1] and b in[0,2] gives 2F_end<=2||v+theta R0||-[2S+1+b(2-b)]||v||²-2<v,Z0>-2theta[delta0+||R0||²+<v,R0>]. This exact shared budget still needs a JOINT lower charge on delta0; it does not authorize summing isolated-level charges or establish the target constant.", "evidence": "For an arbitrary jointly legal double-zero transcript x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, all residuals vanish at queryq. Tilt F by -<v,.>; its global minimum is C_min=f_q-<v,x_q>. Smooth-convex optimum interpolation proves delta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||²/2>=0 and eta=-C_min-||v||²/2>=0. Thus f0=<v,x0>-||v||²/2-eta+||R0||²/2+delta0. Expanding Q_(0,star)=f0-<v+R0,x0>+||v+R0||²/2<=0 gives the stated eta inequality exactly. Since x_q=x0-Sv-Z0, f_q=<v,x0>-(S+1/2)||v||²-<v,Z0>-eta. The 1-smooth final-step descent gives 2F_end<=2<v,x0>-[2S+1+b(2-b)]||v||²-2<v,Z0>-2eta. A convex combination of eta>=0 and the initial inequality, followed by <v+theta R0,x0><=||v+theta R0||, gives the claimed bound. Only necessity from accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used.
