Directly from the concatenation,\n\n  M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j,\n  M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j, A_mass=a/(c-2).             (13)\n\nHere 0<a<c-2. Indeed, writing epsilon=1/m and using lambda^2=2lambda+1,\n\n  c(c-2)-(1-epsilon)\n    =epsilon(2lambda+3)+lambda^2 epsilon^2>0.\n\nThus0<A_mass<1, and for every level j\n\n                      a c^j<(c-2) M_j.                    (14)\n\nBefore any level-j step the elapsed prefix mass P is at least S_j: the\nfirst occurrence of that level is immediately after its left H_j, and\nevery later occurrence is later in the positive word. Since h-1=a c^j,\n\n                (h-1)/(P+1)<c-2,  P+h<=S.                 (15)\n\nCombining(12)--(15) makes x_0<S sqrt(c-2) necessary for any first turn or\nannihilation. This contradicts(1), proving strict sign preservation for\nthe ENTIRE actual word, at every depth and every queried sample.\n\nFor m>=5, c-2<1: lambda<5/2 and1+1/m<=6/5 give c<3. If(3) holds,\nR=|r_0|(1+v_0+r_0^2)>=|r_0|^3>=M>S sqrt(c-2). This proves the claimed\nuniform inclusion without depending on any observed numerical state.\n\n## 5. Exact terminal payoff bound for every one-sign trajectory\n\nApply(10)--(11) to the entire one-sign run, whose total mass is S>=1/2.\nLet q=r_n^2 and w=1+v_n. The actual initial horizontal coordinate obeys\n\n  R>=r_n(S+w+q),  w>=1+S q.\n\nIf r_n=0 the desired bound holds because the numerator vanishes. Otherwise\n(8) gives\n\n  P_par<=(w+(3/2)q)/(S+w+q)^2.                             (16)\n\nSet t=w+q. The constraint on w gives t>=1+(S+1)q, so q<= (t-1)/(S+1).\nConsequently the right side of(16) is at most\n\n              [(2S+3)t-1]/[2(S+1)(S+t)^2], t>=1.\n\nIts derivative has the sign of(2S+3)(S-t)+2. At S>=1/2 the maximizing\npoint t=S+2/(2S+3) lies in the stated domain. Substitution gives exactly\n\n                 E_par(S)=(2S+3)^2/[8(S+1)^2(2S+1)].       (17)\n\nMoreover E_par(S)<1/(2S+1), because\n8(S+1)^2-(2S+3)^2=4S^2+4S-1>0 at S>=1/2. Finally\nS E_par(S) tends to1/4. This proves(2) and a uniform bound on every\none-sign initial state, not only the sufficient domain(1).\n\n## 6. Exact finite benchmark comparison\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d define P_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.\nTheir recurrences, after substituting those shifts, are P_0=B_0=1 and\n\n  P_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),\n  B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l),\n  Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n  G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nHere is a full proof of the needed comparator, so the earlier unverified\nC54 calculation is not used as a premise. Set alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda)\nand Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Both coefficients of Llin exceed1, so\nLlin>=x+y. Direct expansion, using lambda^2=2lambda+1, gives\n\n  alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]\n                   =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.\n\nThus Llin>=Omega(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy=0. At\nz=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial with larger root G(x,lambda y) obeys\n\n  z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2\n      =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.\n\nAlso z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it lies to the right of that\nquadratic's vertex. Therefore G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y) for all\nx,y>=0. Monotonicity of G in its second variable and induction imply\n\n                         B_n<=lambda P_n.                 (18)\n\nPut rho=log_2 lambda>1, D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and ell(z)=log D(exp z).\nFor t=exp z, differentiation yields\n\n  ell''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/D(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket vanishes at0 and has positive derivative for t>0. Convexity\nof ell therefore gives D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 let\nZ=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho. Substitution gives\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy and hence Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y, by integrating\nthe increasing derivative of the positive power. It follows that\nOmega(x,y)<=Z; zero arguments follow directly. Induction in the P recurrence\nnow gives P_n<=(n+1)^rho, and therefore\n\n                     B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).              (19)\n\nBalanced joins in fact attain P_(2^m-1)=lambda^m, although that equality\nis not needed for this upper comparison.\n\nFor the ACTUAL mass(13), A_mass tends to1, c^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m tends\nto exp(1), and (2/lambda)^m tends to0. Hence S/lambda^m tends to exp(1).\nCombine(17),(19) to obtain both the finite inequality and the limsup in(4).\nThe strict comparison lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4 follows from\nlambda<5/2<exp(1), the latter by the exponential series. No decimal limit\nfor the benchmark was assumed or evaluated by the experiment.\n\n## 7. What the bounded experiment changed\n\nThe CLOSED ledger research/max3_c60_trajectory_budget.json records78 full\nstate-schedule attempts and0.8192669660056708 measured seconds, including\ndiagnostic repair and a read-only closeout audit. The remaining18 calls\nexpired. This is separate from the closed656 conic/PEP calls. No optimizer,\nsoftware installation or verifier call was used.\n\nThree exact calibrations checked h=(0),r0=1/2,v0=3/4; h=(2),r0=1,v0=0;\nand h=(10/3,50/9),r0=1/2,v0=0. Their exact terminal payoffs are17/32,0,\nand518/225. All passed numerically, with scaled cubic residuals below\n4e-16. The root formula used was\n2sqrt(A/3)*sinh(asinh((3B/(2A))*sqrt(3/A))/3 for r^3+A r=B, A>0.\n\nThe first50 actual runs used ten initial recipes at depths4,6,8,10,12:\nr0^2 in{1/16,1,m,sqrt(M),M/16,M/4,M,4M} with v0=0, plus\n(r0^2,v0)=(1,sqrt(M)) and(sqrt(M),sqrt(M)). They suggested the intermediate\nhorizontal scale r0^2 proportional to M^(2/3), with many largest observed\npayoffs occurring without a sign change. The next25 runs used\nr0^2=k M^(2/3),v0=0, k in{1/16,1/4,1/2,1,2}, at the same five depths.\n\nFor the now-uniformly-excluded k=1 and2 recipes, the numerical M*payoffs\nwere respectively\n\n  m4:  0.0746391227, 0.1281488193;\n  m6:  0.0687903731, 0.1259430663;\n  m8:  0.0684937234, 0.1263322527;\n  m10: 0.0689648481, 0.1268277425;\n  m12: 0.0694885381, 0.1272377691.\n\nThose numbers motivated the proof; none is a premise. No exact B_n was\nnumerically evaluated or inferred from M. The proof instead uses(19).\n\nTwo depth10 runs completed with payoff underflow, and two depth12 runs\nstopped at cubic RHS/root underflow; those are not exact zero trajectories.\nOne further payoff was subnormal. Raw states and original statuses remain\npublic. An underflow-sensitive turn diagnostic originally multiplied two\ntiny r-values; saved-state companions correct it using separate sign tests,\nwithout replaying any trajectory. The repair time is included in the ledger.\nNo original raw file was overwritten. All raw hashes were checked at closeout.\n\n## 8. Every trajectory with just one turn is also uniformly excluded\n\nThis strengthening imposes NO initial-state bound(1). Suppose the actual\nparabolic trajectory on H_m has exactly one horizontal sign change.\nIf it ever hits r=0 then all later values vanish, so only nonzero turns\nneed be considered. Reflect horizontally so its initial sign is positive.\n\nThe normal variable u_i=v_i+r_i^2 is nondecreasing for this entire physical\nword, because(7) gives the exact identity\n\n  u_(i+1)-u_i=(h_i-1)r_i^2+r_(i+1)^2>=0.                  (21)\n\nEvery h_i>1 here. Before the first turn, any previous step h_t preserved\npositivity, so h_t<1+u_t. A later step h_i no larger than that previous\nh_t then satisfies h_i<=h_t<1+u_t<=1+u_i and CANNOT turn. Consequently\nthe first turn occurs at a NEW RECORD step, necessarily the first occurrence\nof some level j. In the actual recursive word that step is b_j=1+a c^j,\nits prefix is exactly H_j with mass P=S_j, and its suffix mass is\n\n  T=S_m-S_j-b_j>=S_(m-1).                                 (22)\n\nThe last inequality follows because S_j+b_j is increasing in j and the\ntop-level decomposition is S_m=2S_(m-1)+b_(m-1).\n\nLet r>0,u be the state just before this first turn. The positive prefix\nhas decreasing r-values. Therefore its initial horizontal magnitude R\nand the new magnitude R_after obey\n\n  R=r(1+u)+sum_(t<i)h_t r_t>=r(P+1+u),\n  R_after=r(h_i-1-u).\n\nThe numerator is positive precisely because this step turns. Hence\n\n  R_after/R <=(h_i-1-u)/(P+1+u)\n             <=(h_i-1)/(P+1)<c-2.                         (23)\n\nThe strict final inequality is(14)--(15), with the actual H_j prefix.\nThere is no restart of v: the tail starts at the ACTUAL state produced\nby the turning step. By hypothesis there is no further turn, so Section5\napplies to this suffix with its true initial distance R_after and mass T.\nThe function and its minimum0 remain identical. Thus\n\n  2F_par(X_n)/R^2\n    <=E_par(T)(R_after/R)^2\n    <(c-2)^2 E_par(S_(m-1)).                               (24)\n\nFor monotonicity used here, logarithmic differentiation gives\nE_par'(T)/E_par(T)=4/(2T+3)-2/(T+1)-2/(2T+1)<0; the first term is already\nstrictly smaller than2/(T+1). The suffix mass in(22) is at least1/2.\n\nSince S_(m-1)/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1), the exact benchmark bound(19) gives\n\n  limsup B_(2^m-1) sup_(all exactly-one-turn initial states) P_par\n      <=lambda^2(lambda-2)^2/[4 exp(1)]\n       =1/[4 exp(1)]<1/4.                                 (25)\n\nThe equality uses lambda(lambda-2)=1. Combining with the zero-turn estimate\n(4), every initial state whose actual trajectory has at most one turn has\nuniform benchmark limsup at most lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4. No initial norm,\nvertical-state, amplitude, or smallness restriction was imposed on the\none-turn class. This is a proof about the full actual trajectory and its\nretained normal state, not about a constant-gradient single-cut ansatz.\n\n## 9. Remaining interface\n\nEvery parabolic start whose trajectory stays on one horizontal side is now\nbelow even the global Huber lower witness by(17). Thus a useful parabolic\nmechanism on this ruler must actually TURN. Condition(1) also quantifies\nwhich initial horizontal scales cannot turn. The turning domain\n0<R<S sqrt(c-2), with arbitrary coupled r0,v0, is not excluded here.\n\nThe k=1/4 finite starts had one turn and small observed M*payoff. Section8\nnow excludes the ENTIRE one-turn class, independently of those samples.\nThe k=1/16 starts had several turns; two or more turns remain open. A next\nproof must retain how every turn transports BOTH the remaining horizontal\nmagnitude and the accumulated v into the actual suffix, with its unchanged\nphysical parameters. After the first turn, a later turn need not be a new\nglobal record; applying the first-record argument afresh would be invalid.\nResetting v or treating the two H_j copies independently is also invalid.\n\nThis is a concrete uniform regime result with exact nonlinear block losses,\nnot a solution of the full parabolic family, the critical diagnostic, or the\noriginal all-schedule exact-profile goal. All new statements remain drafts\nuntil separately authorized independent verification.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["3d4647bc6bb886cc"], "files": ["research/max3_c63_parabolic_shared_state_bound.md"], "scope": "Every at-most-one-turn initial state and the earlier explicit no-turn regime; NOT all parabolic starts."}, "glossary": {"R_after": "Actual horizontal magnitude immediately after the first parabolic sign-changing step; equal to the true initial distance for its unchanged-function suffix.", "E_par": "Exact one-sign upper bound (2S+3)²/[8(S+1)²(2S+1)] from the fully reproduced joint proof."}}
{"id": "8550aec0c6d0a268", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:53:28.744926+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C64 handles ARBITRARILY MANY parabolic turns on actual H_m: if the first turn has levelj and terminal u_n=v_n+r_n²<=a c^j, then for m>=128 the TRUE payoff satisfies B_n P<1/2 uniformly in j and all initial states. For j>=1 the full-word bound is P<=E_par(S_j) beta_j^[2(2^(m-j)-1)] c^[-2(m-j)], beta_j=c a c^j/[M_j+a c^(j-1)]<9/10, giving B_n P<729/1600. The j0 case has the explicit doubly exponential product bound. Every shared-v block and every pair retained. Together with C63, the open parabolic class must have>=2 turns AND cross its first turning threshold. Complete UNVERIFIED combined proof; no new trajectory, solve or verification.", "evidence": "# C64 extension: arbitrarily many turns below the first turning threshold\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED. The public combined package appends the COMPLETE C63\nproof, including the global function, every interpolation pair, the true\ndistance, the one-sign block estimates and the exact benchmark comparison.\nThese are reproduced internal arguments, not unverified predecessor facts.\nThe only accepted mathematical input is b98bb807d514e80d for its benchmark\ndefinitions. No numerical evaluation or verification is used here.\n\n## Statement\n\nUse exactly the physical H_m, c,a,S_j,M_j and parabolic trajectory from the\nattached complete proof, with m>=128. Put d_j=a c^j and u_i=v_i+r_i^2.\nSuppose there is a first nonzero horizontal sign change, occurring at level j,\nand suppose\n\n                            u_n<=d_j.                      (A)\n\nThe first-turn level j is allowed to depend arbitrarily on m. There is no\ninitial-state bound, fixed turn count or ambient approximation. If absorption\nat zero occurs at any time then the final payoff is zero and the bounds below\nhold immediately. Otherwise there are exactly2^(m-j)-1 turns.\n\nFor j>=1 set\n\n  k=m-j, N=2^k, L_j=d_(j-1)=d_j/c,\n  C_j=d_j/(M_j+L_j), beta_j=c C_j.\n\nThen the normalized actual payoff satisfies\n\n  P_par<=E_par(S_j) beta_j^[2(N-1)] c^(-2k),\n  B_(2^m-1) P_par<729/1600<1/2.                            (B)\n\nFor j=0 it satisfies\n\n  P_par<=(1-1/m)^[2(2^m-1)] c^(-2m),\n  B_(2^m-1)P_par<=lambda^(1-m)<1/2.                        (C)\n\nThus every trajectory satisfying(A) is uniformly below HALF the exact\nbenchmark for all m>=128, even when its number of turns grows with m.\nTogether with C63's complete one-turn estimate in the attached proof, the\nremaining parabolic case must have AT LEAST TWO turns and u_n>d_j for its\nfirst-turn level j. This does not settle that remaining case or unrestricted W.\n\n## 1. The lower normal threshold persists after the first turn\n\nThe exact identity\n\n  u_(i+1)-u_i=(h_i-1)r_i^2+r_(i+1)^2>=0                    (D)\n\nholds because every physical step is greater than1. A sign change occurs\nexactly when u_i<d_level(i), since the cubic's right side is r_i(u_i-d_level(i)).\nAs proved directly in the attached C63 argument, the first turn must occur\nat a new record step, hence at the first occurrence of level j. Its preceding\nword is exactly H_j, with no earlier turn.\n\nFor j>=1, the earlier first occurrence of b_(j-1)=1+d_(j-1) did NOT turn\nor annihilate. At its input this implies u>d_(j-1). Monotonicity(D) gives\nu>L_j at the first turn and at every later sample. For j=0 simply use u>=0.\nHypothesis(A) and(D) simultaneously give u<=d_j at every sample.\n\nConsequently, after the first turn every lower-level step in H_j preserves\nthe sign, since its d_level<=L_j<u. Every intervening step of level at\nleast j reverses the sign, except that an equality can cause absorption,\nwhich has already been separated as zero payoff. The same u and v are\ninherited across all copies.\n\n## 2. All complete blocks and all coarse bridges remain in order\n\nThe actual word H_m partitions into N=2^(m-j) copies of H_j, interleaved\nwith N-1 bridges. The excess of the bridge after block ell,0<=ell<N-1, is\n\n                 d_j c^(nu_2(ell+1)),                     (E)\n\nwhere nu_2 is binary valuation. No physical step is aggregated or replaced\nin the actual dynamics; this partition is only for applying proved block\ninequalities. The first H_j has one sign by the definition of the first turn.\nEvery later H_j has one sign by Section1. All finite pair inequalities are\nalready supplied by the one fixed function in the attached proof.\n\nLet R_ell be the horizontal magnitude at the entry to an H_j block. Just\nbefore its following bridge, let the actual gradient magnitude be |r| and\nnormal state u. The one-sign block telescope gives\n\n  R_ell>=|r|(M_j+u).\n\nThe bridge is a turn, so its resulting horizontal magnitude is\n|r|(d_j c^s-u), where s=nu_2(ell+1). Since u>=L_j for j>=1,\n\n  R_(ell+1)/R_ell\n    <=(d_j c^s-u)/(M_j+u)\n    <=d_j c^s/(M_j+L_j)=C_j c^s.                         (F)\n\nThe first inequality also holds for the first bridge: its left H_j was\none-sign, and u>=L_j holds at the BRIDGE, regardless of any smaller u at\nthe original starting point. Thus no extra initial-band hypothesis was used.\n\nThe binary counts give\n\n  sum_(ell=0)^(N-2)nu_2(ell+1)=N-1-k.\n\nOne direct proof counts multiples of2^s among1,...,2^k-1 and sums\nsum_(s=1)^(k-1)(2^(k-s)-1)=2^k-1-k. Multiplying(F) therefore yields\n\n  R_(N-1)/R_0<=C_j^(N-1)c^(N-1-k)\n              =beta_j^(N-1)c^(-k).                       (G)\n\nThe final full H_j is one-sign and has its ACTUAL inherited vertical state.\nIts true initial distance is R_(N-1), because the closest minimizer of the\nfixed parabolic function has the same vertical coordinate. Apply the proved\nE_par(S_j) suffix bound, and use(G), to obtain the first inequality in(B).\n\n## 3. Explicit constants and the exact finite benchmark\n\nFrom the exact mass formula in the attached proof,\nd_j/M_j<c-2. Thus\n\n  beta_j=c[d_j/M_j]/[1+(d_j/M_j)/c]\n           <c^2(c-2)/(2c-2).                             (H)\n\nFor m>=128 one has c<73/30. To check this without decimal input, use\nsqrt2<99/70 because9801>9800, and hence\nc<(169/70)(129/128)=21801/8960<73/30.\nThe positive function c^2(c-2)/(2c-2) is increasing for c>2, since both\nc^2 and(c-2)/(2(c-1)) are increasing and positive. Consequently\n\n  beta_j<69277/77400<9/10.                                (I)\n\nAlso a>2/5: from1-1/m>=127/128 and c<73/30,\na>3810/9344>2/5. Because c-2<1/2, A_mass=a/(c-2)>4/5.\nFor j>=1, c>12/5 and the exact mass formula give\n\n  S_j>(4/5)c^j-1>(23/60)c^j>c^j/3.                        (J)\n\nThe second inequality uses1<(5/12)c^j. The elementary one-sign objective\nbound also gives E_par(S)<=3/(8S): bound w+(3/2)q by(3/2)(w+q) in its\nproof and maximize t/(S+t)^2<=1/(4S). Thus(J) implies\nE_par(S_j)<9/(8c^j).\n\nThe attached benchmark proof establishes B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1) from\nthe exact finite recurrence, without an assumed asymptotic constant.\nUsing k=m-j>=1, N-1>=1 and(I), we obtain\n\n  B_(2^m-1)P_par\n    <(9/8)(lambda/c)^m [lambda c^(-k)] beta_j^[2(N-1)]\n    <(9/8)(1/2)(81/100)=729/1600.\n\nHere(lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)<=1/2 by the binomial theorem, and\nlambda c^(-k)<1 for k>=1. This proves the stated finite uniform gap.\n\n## 4. The smallest first-turn level\n\nIf j=0 then(A) says u_n<=a. By monotonicity u_i<=a at every query. Every\nstep has excess d_level>=a; all steps turn unless there is absorption.\nWriting x_i=r_i(1+u_i), the exact horizontal update gives\n\n  |x_(i+1)|/|x_i|=(d_level-u_i)/(1+u_i)<=d_level.\n\nThere are2^m-1 steps and the sum of their levels is2^m-1-m by the same\nbinary count. Since a c=1-1/m,\n\n  |x_n|/|x_0|<=a^(2^m-1)c^(2^m-1-m)\n                =(1-1/m)^(2^m-1)c^(-m).\n\nAt any parabolic state, 2F_par(X)<=x^2, where x is the horizontal\ncoordinate: by direct subtraction the difference is\nr^2[u+u^2-r^2/2]>=0 because u>=r^2. Hence its normalized payoff is at\nmost the squared displayed ratio. The exact benchmark bound then gives\nB P<=lambda^(m+1)c^(-2m)<=lambda^(1-m), proving(C).\n\n## 5. The actual remaining charge, with exact identities\n\nThis result allows any number of turns but assumes the terminal normal state\nhas not passed the first turning excess. It cannot be restarted for free\nwhen u crosses d_j: the remaining word is then only a partial recursive block,\nand its first incoming state already contains the prior turn history.\n\nTwo exact identities preserve that history for a possible future charge.\nLet x_i=r_i(1+u_i), q_i=r_i^2 and define\n\n  Energy_i=x_i^2+v_i^2-v_0^2, Energy_0=R_0^2.\n\nThe two-coordinate recursions x_plus=x-h r, v_plus=v+h r^2 give\n\n  Energy_(i+1)-Energy_i=h_i(h_i-2)q_i(1+q_i),\n  u_(i+1)-u_i=(h_i-1)q_i+q_(i+1).                         (K)\n\nFor the first identity expand both squares and substitute\nx_i r_i=q_i(1+v_i+q_i); the v terms cancel. Also Energy_i>=x_i^2 because\nv_i>=v_0. This is an AUXILIARY identity; it does not assert that the\nrescaled two-dimensional field is a Euclidean gradient, or that Energy\nis monotone at steps above2. Its initial value is the TRUE initial squared\ndistance, so the free vertical starting coordinate is not charged as norm.\n\nA useful next bound must combine the positive large-step terms in(K) with\nthe normal-level crossing they cause, retaining the partially completed\nword. Merely declaring those terms nonpositive, resetting v, or multiplying\nfull-block bounds across an unproved cut would be invalid. No such bound\nis yet established here.\n\n\n# Complete internal preliminary proof (C63)\n\n# C63: a shared-state block bound closes the large horizontal parabolic regime\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED analytic candidate following the bounded direct\nC60 experiment. No new fact_submit. Accepted b98bb807d514e80d is used for\nthe exact benchmark definitions; every further estimate below is derived\ninternally. C60's unverified formulas are reproduced and proved here.\n\nThe result is uniform over an explicit domain of initial states and, in\nSection8, over EVERY trajectory with at most one horizontal sign change.\nIt is NOT an upper bound on every parabolic start or on unrestricted W_n(h).\n\n## 1. Result, with the actual physical parameters\n\nFor m>=5 put\n\n  lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n  n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m, M=1+S.\n\nAll these parameters remain fixed throughout the complete word H_m.\nConsider the ONE function and initial state defined in Section2. Its\nparameters are r_0!=0,v_0>=0. Let\n\n  R=|r_0|(1+v_0+r_0^2),\n  E_par(S)=(2S+3)^2/[8(S+1)^2(2S+1)].\n\nIf\n\n                     R >= S sqrt(c-2),                     (1)\n\nthen every r_i has the same strict sign as r_0, and its actual normalized\nlast-iterate objective satisfies\n\n  P_par(H_m;r_0,v_0)<=E_par(S)<1/(2S+1).                    (2)\n\nIn particular(1) holds uniformly for ALL initial states\n\n                     r_0^2>=M^(2/3), v_0>=0.               (3)\n\nFor the EXACT Zhang--Jiang benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n from the accepted fact,\n\n  B_(2^m-1) sup_(states satisfying(1)) P_par\n       <=lambda^(m+1) E_par(S),\n  limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup_(states satisfying(1)) P_par\n       <=lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4.                            (4)\n\nNo asymptotic equality for B_n is assumed. Section6 proves the scalar upper\nenvelope needed in(4) directly from its exact finite recurrence.\n\nThe payoff bound in(2) also holds for ANY finite nonnegative schedule of\ntotal mass S>=1/2 whenever its whole parabolic trajectory keeps one sign\n(allowing absorption at zero). Thus the candidate bound is not inferred\nfrom the finite sample portfolio. What is particular to the ruler is the\nproved sufficient initial-state condition(1).\n\n## 2. One global legal function, every pair, and true distance\n\nIn R2 define\n\n  K_par={(t,s):s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},\n  F_par(X)=max_(g in K_par)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).\n\nThe maximizer is the unique Euclidean projection onto this nonempty closed\nconvex set: complete the square, use coercivity for existence and strict\nconvexity for uniqueness. The normal inequalities at two projection points\ngive firm nonexpansiveness. Comparing the defining maxima shows that F_par\nis differentiable with gradient equal to the projection; it is globally\nconvex and1-smooth. In detail, for the projection p(X),\n||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>, and\n0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>\n <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.\n\nFor r real and v>=0 put\n\n  u=v+r^2,\n  X(r,v)=(r(1+v+r^2),-v/sqrt(2)),\n  G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).\n\nOne has X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any(t,s) in K_par,\n<X-G,(t,s)-G><=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0. Thus G(r) is the actual gradient\nat X(r,v), and substitution gives\n\n  F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                          (5)\n\nFor EVERY ordered pair of such samples the complete interpolation residual is\n\n  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n      =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n      =-(v_j+r_j^2)(r_i-r_j)^2/2<=0.                       (6)\n\nThe identity follows by writing f_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2 and expanding.\nThere is no adjacent-only interpolation condition in this construction.\n\nThe minimizer set of F_par is exactly{(0,y):y<=0}. Such a point gives a\nnonpositive inner product with every feasible gradient, and0 is feasible.\nA point with positive vertical coordinate has a positive value by testing\n(0,s) with small positive s. A point with nonzero horizontal coordinate\nhas a positive value by testing(t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with small t of the same\nhorizontal sign; the linear term dominates the quadratic and quartic terms.\nHence the closest minimizer to X(r_0,v_0) is(0,-v_0/sqrt(2)), and the\ntrue squared distance is R^2=r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2.\n\nFor every physical step h>=0 actual GD is described by\n\n  v_plus=v+h r^2,\n  r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus=r(1+v+r^2-h).                  (7)\n\nThe cubic's derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1, so it has exactly one real\nsolution. The horizontal and vertical coordinates in(7) are exactly those\nof X-hG. This proves actual GD for the same global F_par at every step.\nThe normalized payoff is\n\n  P_par=[r_n^2(1+v_n)+(3/2)r_n^4]/R^2.                    (8)\n\nReflection of the horizontal coordinate sends r to-r and leaves all values\nand v unchanged. Thus it suffices to prove the remaining statements with\nr_0>0. If r ever becomes zero it remains zero by(7) and the payoff is zero.\n\n## 3. Quantitative block map with one shared vertical state\n\nFirst suppose a consecutive actual block of total mass T has positive\nr-values throughout. At one step, the sign is retained exactly when\nh<1+v+r^2. The increasing cubic in(7), evaluated at the OLD positive r,\nexceeds its right side by h r(1+r^2)>=0. Consequently\n\n                       0<r_plus<=r.                       (9)\n\nWrite the entry state(r_A,v_A), exit state(r_B,v_B), and horizontal\ncoordinate x=r(1+v+r^2). Telescoping the two ACTUAL recursions gives\n\n  Delta_x=x_A-x_B=sum_(block)h_i r_i,\n  Delta_v=v_B-v_A=sum_(block)h_i r_i^2.\n\nBecause r_B<=r_i<=r_A, the following complete finite bounds hold:\n\n  T r_B<=Delta_x<=T r_A,\n  r_B Delta_x<=Delta_v<=r_A Delta_x,\n  T r_B^2<=Delta_v<=T r_A^2,\n  Delta_x^2<=T Delta_v.                                   (10)\n\nThe final inequality is weighted Cauchy--Schwarz; at T=0 both sides vanish.\nIn particular the nonlinear block map obeys\n\n  r_B[1+v_B+r_B^2+T]<=r_A(1+v_A+r_A^2).                   (11)\n\nThese estimates compose along H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), where b_j=1+a c^j,\nby using the ACTUAL exit(r_B,v_B) of the left block as the bridge input,\nthen the bridge's exit as the right block input. The right copy's v is\nnever restarted or frozen. Equations(10)--(11) therefore control full\nnonlinear blocks with all finite masses and all inherited v explicitly\npresent; no limiting differential equation is used.\n\n## 4. The first possible turn has a quantitative initial-radius cost\n\nConsider a first step whose output r is nonpositive. All preceding states\nwere positive and decreasing by(9). Let P be the total earlier step mass,\nh this step, r its positive input, v its input vertical state, q=r^2,\nand u=v+q. Equation(7) forces h>=1+u. Also the prefix bounds(10) give\n\n  v-v_0>=P q,  x_0-x<=sqrt(P(v-v_0)),  x=r(1+u).\n\nTherefore v>=Pq, u<=h-1, and\n\n  x_0<=h sqrt(q)+sqrt(Pv).\n\nFor fixed u with v=u-q, the condition v>=Pq says 0<=q<=u/(P+1).\nOn this interval h sqrt(q)+sqrt(P(u-q)) is nondecreasing in q. For P>0\nthis follows by differentiation, because h>=1 and u-q>=Pq imply\nh^2(u-q)>=Pq. For P=0 it follows directly. Its maximum is therefore\n(P+h)sqrt(u/(P+1)). We have proved the necessary condition for a first\nturn OR a first annihilation:\n\n  x_0<=(P+h) sqrt[(h-1)/(P+1)].                             (12)\n\nThis estimate retains all previous gradient energy in v-v_0; it does not\nreplace the old trajectory by an independent block instance.\n\nFor the actual ruler let S_j=sum H_j and M_j=1+S_j, retaining the SAME\nc,a from the enclosing horizon m. Directly from the concatenation,\n\n  M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j,\n  M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j, A_mass=a/(c-2).             (13)\n\nHere 0<a<c-2. Indeed, writing epsilon=1/m and using lambda^2=2lambda+1,\n\n  c(c-2)-(1-epsilon)\n    =epsilon(2lambda+3)+lambda^2 epsilon^2>0.\n\nThus0<A_mass<1, and for every level j\n\n                      a c^j<(c-2) M_j.                    (14)\n\nBefore any level-j step the elapsed prefix mass P is at least S_j: the\nfirst occurrence of that level is immediately after its left H_j, and\nevery later occurrence is later in the positive word. Since h-1=a c^j,\n\n                (h-1)/(P+1)<c-2,  P+h<=S.                 (15)\n\nCombining(12)--(15) makes x_0<S sqrt(c-2) necessary for any first turn or\nannihilation. This contradicts(1), proving strict sign preservation for\nthe ENTIRE actual word, at every depth and every queried sample.\n\nFor m>=5, c-2<1: lambda<5/2 and1+1/m<=6/5 give c<3. If(3) holds,\nR=|r_0|(1+v_0+r_0^2)>=|r_0|^3>=M>S sqrt(c-2). This proves the claimed\nuniform inclusion without depending on any observed numerical state.\n\n## 5. Exact terminal payoff bound for every one-sign trajectory\n\nApply(10)--(11) to the entire one-sign run, whose total mass is S>=1/2.\nLet q=r_n^2 and w=1+v_n. The actual initial horizontal coordinate obeys\n\n  R>=r_n(S+w+q),  w>=1+S q.\n\nIf r_n=0 the desired bound holds because the numerator vanishes. Otherwise\n(8) gives\n\n  P_par<=(w+(3/2)q)/(S+w+q)^2.                             (16)\n\nSet t=w+q. The constraint on w gives t>=1+(S+1)q, so q<= (t-1)/(S+1).\nConsequently the right side of(16) is at most\n\n              [(2S+3)t-1]/[2(S+1)(S+t)^2], t>=1.\n\nIts derivative has the sign of(2S+3)(S-t)+2. At S>=1/2 the maximizing\npoint t=S+2/(2S+3) lies in the stated domain. Substitution gives exactly\n\n                 E_par(S)=(2S+3)^2/[8(S+1)^2(2S+1)].       (17)\n\nMoreover E_par(S)<1/(2S+1), because\n8(S+1)^2-(2S+3)^2=4S^2+4S-1>0 at S>=1/2. Finally\nS E_par(S) tends to1/4. This proves(2) and a uniform bound on every\none-sign initial state, not only the sufficient domain(1).\n\n## 6. Exact finite benchmark comparison\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d define P_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.\nTheir recurrences, after substituting those shifts, are P_0=B_0=1 and\n\n  P_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),\n  B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l),\n  Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n  G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nHere is a full proof of the needed comparator, so the earlier unverified\nC54 calculation is not used as a premise. Set alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda)\nand Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Both coefficients of Llin exceed1, so\nLlin>=x+y. Direct expansion, using lambda^2=2lambda+1, gives\n\n  alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]\n                   =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.\n\nThus Llin>=Omega(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy=0. At\nz=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial with larger root G(x,lambda y) obeys\n\n  z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2\n      =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.\n\nAlso z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it lies to the right of that\nquadratic's vertex. Therefore G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y) for all\nx,y>=0. Monotonicity of G in its second variable and induction imply\n\n                         B_n<=lambda P_n.                 (18)\n\nPut rho=log_2 lambda>1, D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and ell(z)=log D(exp z).\nFor t=exp z, differentiation yields\n\n  ell''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/D(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket vanishes at0 and has positive derivative for t>0. Convexity\nof ell therefore gives D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 let\nZ=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho. Substitution gives\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy and hence Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y, by integrating\nthe increasing derivative of the positive power. It follows that\nOmega(x,y)<=Z; zero arguments follow directly. Induction in the P recurrence\nnow gives P_n<=(n+1)^rho, and therefore\n\n                     B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).              (19)\n\nBalanced joins in fact attain P_(2^m-1)=lambda^m, although that equality\nis not needed for this upper comparison.\n\nFor the ACTUAL mass(13), A_mass tends to1, c^m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m tends\nto exp(1), and (2/lambda)^m tends to0. Hence S/lambda^m tends to exp(1).\nCombine(17),(19) to obtain both the finite inequality and the limsup in(4).\nThe strict comparison lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4 follows from\nlambda<5/2<exp(1), the latter by the exponential series. No decimal limit\nfor the benchmark was assumed or evaluated by the experiment.\n\n## 7. What the bounded experiment changed\n\nThe CLOSED ledger research/max3_c60_trajectory_budget.json records78 full\nstate-schedule attempts and0.8192669660056708 measured seconds, including\ndiagnostic repair and a read-only closeout audit. The remaining18 calls\nexpired. This is separate from the closed656 conic/PEP calls. No optimizer,\nsoftware installation or verifier call was used.\n\nThree exact calibrations checked h=(0),r0=1/2,v0=3/4; h=(2),r0=1,v0=0;\nand h=(10/3,50/9),r0=1/2,v0=0. Their exact terminal payoffs are17/32,0,\nand518/225. All passed numerically, with scaled cubic residuals below\n4e-16. The root formula used was\n2sqrt(A/3)*sinh(asinh((3B/(2A))*sqrt(3/A))/3 for r^3+A r=B, A>0.\n\nThe first50 actual runs used ten initial recipes at depths4,6,8,10,12:\nr0^2 in{1/16,1,m,sqrt(M),M/16,M/4,M,4M} with v0=0, plus\n(r0^2,v0)=(1,sqrt(M)) and(sqrt(M),sqrt(M)). They suggested the intermediate\nhorizontal scale r0^2 proportional to M^(2/3), with many largest observed\npayoffs occurring without a sign change. The next25 runs used\nr0^2=k M^(2/3),v0=0, k in{1/16,1/4,1/2,1,2}, at the same five depths.\n\nFor the now-uniformly-excluded k=1 and2 recipes, the numerical M*payoffs\nwere respectively\n\n  m4:  0.0746391227, 0.1281488193;\n  m6:  0.0687903731, 0.1259430663;\n  m8:  0.0684937234, 0.1263322527;\n  m10: 0.0689648481, 0.1268277425;\n  m12: 0.0694885381, 0.1272377691.\n\nThose numbers motivated the proof; none is a premise. No exact B_n was\nnumerically evaluated or inferred from M. The proof instead uses(19).\n\nTwo depth10 runs completed with payoff underflow, and two depth12 runs\nstopped at cubic RHS/root underflow; those are not exact zero trajectories.\nOne further payoff was subnormal. Raw states and original statuses remain\npublic. An underflow-sensitive turn diagnostic originally multiplied two\ntiny r-values; saved-state companions correct it using separate sign tests,\nwithout replaying any trajectory. The repair time is included in the ledger.\nNo original raw file was overwritten. All raw hashes were checked at closeout.\n\n## 8. Every trajectory with just one turn is also uniformly excluded\n\nThis strengthening imposes NO initial-state bound(1). Suppose the actual\nparabolic trajectory on H_m has exactly one horizontal sign change.\nIf it ever hits r=0 then all later values vanish, so only nonzero turns\nneed be considered. Reflect horizontally so its initial sign is positive.\n\nThe normal variable u_i=v_i+r_i^2 is nondecreasing for this entire physical\nword, because(7) gives the exact identity\n\n  u_(i+1)-u_i=(h_i-1)r_i^2+r_(i+1)^2>=0.                  (21)\n\nEvery h_i>1 here. Before the first turn, any previous step h_t preserved\npositivity, so h_t<1+u_t. A later step h_i no larger than that previous\nh_t then satisfies h_i<=h_t<1+u_t<=1+u_i and CANNOT turn. Consequently\nthe first turn occurs at a NEW RECORD step, necessarily the first occurrence\nof some level j. In the actual recursive word that step is b_j=1+a c^j,\nits prefix is exactly H_j with mass P=S_j, and its suffix mass is\n\n  T=S_m-S_j-b_j>=S_(m-1).                                 (22)\n\nThe last inequality follows because S_j+b_j is increasing in j and the\ntop-level decomposition is S_m=2S_(m-1)+b_(m-1).\n\nLet r>0,u be the state just before this first turn. The positive prefix\nhas decreasing r-values. Therefore its initial horizontal magnitude R\nand the new magnitude R_after obey\n\n  R=r(1+u)+sum_(t<i)h_t r_t>=r(P+1+u),\n  R_after=r(h_i-1-u).\n\nThe numerator is positive precisely because this step turns. Hence\n\n  R_after/R <=(h_i-1-u)/(P+1+u)\n             <=(h_i-1)/(P+1)<c-2.                         (23)\n\nThe strict final inequality is(14)--(15), with the actual H_j prefix.\nThere is no restart of v: the tail starts at the ACTUAL state produced\nby the turning step. By hypothesis there is no further turn, so Section5\napplies to this suffix with its true initial distance R_after and mass T.\nThe function and its minimum0 remain identical. Thus\n\n  2F_par(X_n)/R^2\n    <=E_par(T)(R_after/R)^2\n    <(c-2)^2 E_par(S_(m-1)).                               (24)\n\nFor monotonicity used here, logarithmic differentiation gives\nE_par'(T)/E_par(T)=4/(2T+3)-2/(T+1)-2/(2T+1)<0; the first term is already\nstrictly smaller than2/(T+1). The suffix mass in(22) is at least1/2.\n\nSince S_(m-1)/lambda^(m-1)->exp(1), the exact benchmark bound(19) gives\n\n  limsup B_(2^m-1) sup_(all exactly-one-turn initial states) P_par\n      <=lambda^2(lambda-2)^2/[4 exp(1)]\n       =1/[4 exp(1)]<1/4.                                 (25)\n\nThe equality uses lambda(lambda-2)=1. Combining with the zero-turn estimate\n(4), every initial state whose actual trajectory has at most one turn has\nuniform benchmark limsup at most lambda/[4 exp(1)]<1/4. No initial norm,\nvertical-state, amplitude, or smallness restriction was imposed on the\none-turn class. This is a proof about the full actual trajectory and its\nretained normal state, not about a constant-gradient single-cut ansatz.\n\n## 9. Remaining interface\n\nEvery parabolic start whose trajectory stays on one horizontal side is now\nbelow even the global Huber lower witness by(17). Thus a useful parabolic\nmechanism on this ruler must actually TURN. Condition(1) also quantifies\nwhich initial horizontal scales cannot turn. The turning domain\n0<R<S sqrt(c-2), with arbitrary coupled r0,v0, is not excluded here.\n\nThe k=1/4 finite starts had one turn and small observed M*payoff. Section8\nnow excludes the ENTIRE one-turn class, independently of those samples.\nThe k=1/16 starts had several turns; two or more turns remain open. A next\nproof must retain how every turn transports BOTH the remaining horizontal\nmagnitude and the accumulated v into the actual suffix, with its unchanged\nphysical parameters. After the first turn, a later turn need not be a new\nglobal record; applying the first-record argument afresh would be invalid.\nResetting v or treating the two H_j copies independently is also invalid.\n\nThis is a concrete uniform regime result with exact nonlinear block losses,\nnot a solution of the full parabolic family, the critical diagnostic, or the\noriginal all-schedule exact-profile goal. All new statements remain drafts\nuntil separately authorized independent verification.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["010d17da94d22907", "3d4647bc6bb886cc"], "files": ["research/max3_c64_parabolic_first_turn_band.md"], "scope": "All trajectories staying below their first turning normal threshold; not those crossing it."}, "glossary": {"beta_j": "Coarse-block amplitude coefficient c*d_j/(M_j+d_(j-1)) for first-turn level j>=1, with d_j=a c^j and M_j=1+sumH_j; local to C64.", "Energy_i": "Auxiliary parabolic energy x_i²+v_i²-v0², starting at the true initial squared distance; x_i is horizontal coordinate."}}

Direct expansion gives (1/4)||x0||²-2f1+Q10+Q0star+Q1star=(1/4)||x0-2g0-2g1||². The value coefficients cancel and every multiplier is nonnegative, hence W<=1/4. Conversely the scalar quadratic f(x)=x²/2 at x0=1 gives x1=-1/2 and2f1=1/4. Alternatively Huber threshold1/4 yields x1=5/8,f1=1/8. This jointly proved calibration is a finding, not a new accepted fact; routine fact_submit is explicitly not allocated.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "scope": "calibration", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "justification_type": "calculation", "confidence": 0.99}, "glossary": {"Q_ij": "Signed smooth convex interpolation residual in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.", "W_1": "Project normalized worst-case objective after one GD step."}}
{"id": "0086eff0be93affb", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:15:16.015877+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "max4 real-character round14 joint candidate: (A) explicit nonuniform all-character simplex gradients for every h_i>1, with every original pair, positive common-anchor coefficients, exact TRUE polar distance and payoff V_eq<=64^(-n); (B) every strictly positive PRODUCT-character kernel with the concrete adjacent-cocoercivity-saturation amplitude rule fails every actual H_m for m>=4. At each level-k bridge k>=3 an original six-pair residual exceeds (1-rho_0^2)||g_middle||^2/36 for ANY anchor and values. A rank-one quadratic shows the singular boundary is essential; exact unsaturated-cycle identities give the necessary repair cost. Complete unverified draft, not a target solution.", "evidence": "# Real character Grams: an explicit legal family and a cycle obstruction\n\nStatus: **self-contained joint candidate, not verifier accepted**. This is\nround 14, the first round of the sixth max4 launch. No optimization,\nparameter search, installation, or fact submission is used. The only\nmathematical predecessors are accepted facts `1252b8823bb5a290` (the\nnecessary smooth-convex interpolation inequalities) and\n`b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact benchmark and its elementary bound).\nAll new formulas used below are proved in this document. The anchored\nsimplex draft `016467bc5c3eb666` was a construction lead, not a premise.\n\nThere are two precisely specified generating rules here.\n\n* Section 2 gives a legal nonuniform character family for every finite\n  schedule with all steps strictly greater than 1. It has a common anchor,\n  one explicit projection function, all ordered interpolation comparisons,\n  and an exact certificate for the true distance. Its exact payoff is\n  exponentially small. It is a calibration construction, not a proposed\n  sharp lower bound.\n* Sections 3--5 study product character weights, with amplitudes defined\n  explicitly by saturating every adjacent cocoercivity constraint. All\n  characters can be present, including the highest frequencies. For every\n  actual critical ruler word H_m with m>=4, every nondegenerate member of\n  this policy violates a quantitative three-sample cycle inequality.\n  This excludes that policy, not general character kernels or arbitrary\n  amplitudes. Section 6 identifies the necessary slack that a different\n  amplitude rule must supply.\n\n## 1. Conventions and the fixed-function interface\n\nNormalize smoothness to 1. A schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) has exactly n\nalgorithm steps and N=n+1 sampled gradients. For vectors g_i and an anchor\nX_0 put X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t. Write\n\n    K_ij=<g_i,g_j>,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.       (1)\n\nEvery ordered Q_ij is nonpositive for legal convex 1-smooth samples, by\naccepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290. The optimum sample is (0,0,0) when used.\n\nFor a finite convex hull C=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n), define the ONE function\n\n    F_C(X)=max_(v in C)(<X,v>-||v||^2/2).             (2)\n\nHere C and the anchor will be fixed before the trajectory is run. The\nmaximizer is the Euclidean projection P_C(X), since completing the square\nturns the maximization into nearest-point minimization. Existence follows\nfrom compactness; strict convexity of squared distance gives uniqueness.\nA vector g in C is this projection exactly when\n\n    <X-g,v-g><=0 for every v in C.                   (3)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance along every segment\nfrom g into C. Conversely, expansion of ||X-v||^2 proves sufficiency.\nAdding (3) for X and X' gives\n||P_C(X)-P_C(X')||^2 <= <X-X',P_C(X)-P_C(X')>.\nComparing the two maximizing expressions in (2) bounds the remainder in\nthe first-order expansion of F_C by ||X-X'||^2. Thus F_C is differentiable,\ngrad F_C=P_C, and its gradient is 1-Lipschitz. It is convex and finite.\n\nBecause 0 is a vertex, F_C>=0. Its minimizers are exactly the polar cone\n\n    C^polar={Z:<Z,g_i><=0 for all i}.                 (4)\n\nIndeed (3) says P_C(Z)=0 exactly on this cone. These observations prove\nthe realization interface used below without assuming the C62 draft.\n\nFor any proposed samples satisfying (3), set Y_i=X_i-g_i and\nf_i=<X_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Direct expansion gives\n\n    <Y_i,g_j-g_i>=Q_(j,i),\n    <Y_i,-g_i>=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2.                    (5)\n\nThus checking every vertex, including 0, checks all normals; (2) then\ninterpolates the stated values and gradients at the prescribed GD points.\n\n## 2. A legal nonuniform character policy with exact true distance\n\nAssume n>=1 and every h_i>1. Define explicit scalar parameters\n\n    H=max_i h_i,\n    kappa=min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4),\n    zeta=kappa/(8H),\n    eta=kappa/[8(H+kappa+1)],\n    gamma_i=eta^i, 0<=i<=n.                          (6)\n\nIn particular h_i>=1+4kappa, 0<zeta<1/8, 0<eta<1/8,\nzeta H=kappa/8, and (kappa+1)eta<=kappa/8.\n\nChoose d to be the least integer with D=2^d>=N, and use the distinct\nstandard binary labels of i=0,...,n in F_2^d. No samples or physical steps\nare appended. For s in F_2^d let chi_s(i)=(-1)^(s dot i), with the dot\nproduct computed modulo 2 only to select the real sign. Set\n\n    w_0=zeta+(1-zeta)/D,\n    w_s=(1-zeta)/D for s!=0,\n    g_i=(gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_(s in F_2^d).     (7)\n\nAll D weights are strictly positive and they are nonuniform. For distinct\nlabels, half the characters have each sign: pairing s with s+e_l for a bit\nwhere the labels differ proves this directly. Hence\n\n    K_ii=gamma_i^2,\n    K_ij=zeta gamma_i gamma_j for i!=j,\n    K=diag(gamma) C_0 diag(gamma),\n    C_0=(1-zeta)I_N+zeta 11^T.                        (8)\n\nThis is positive definite, including when N<D. Here 1 is the N-vector\nwhose entries are all 1, not the constant character itself.\n\nThe anchor and the values are also explicit. Put\n\n    v_i=(kappa+1)gamma_i+zeta sum_(t<i) h_t gamma_t,\n    tau=zeta sum_i v_i/[1+(N-1)zeta],\n    u_i=(v_i-tau)/[(1-zeta)gamma_i],\n    X_0=sum_i u_i g_i,\n    f_i=(kappa+1/2)gamma_i^2.                         (9)\n\nThe letter u here denotes anchor coefficients, not algorithm steps.\nMultiplication of (8) shows\n\n    C_0^(-1)=(1-zeta)^(-1)\n                  [I_N-zeta 11^T/(1+(N-1)zeta)],\n    <X_0,g_i>=gamma_i v_i\n       =(kappa+1)gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t K_ti.         (10)\n\nIn particular <X_i,g_i>=(kappa+1)gamma_i^2 and\n<X_i-g_i,g_i>=kappa gamma_i^2>0.\n\n### 2.1 Every original ordered comparison\n\nFor i<j, substitute (8)--(10) into (1). The two orientations are\n\n    Q_(j,i)=(kappa+1)gamma_j^2-kappa gamma_i^2\n         +(h_i-1)zeta gamma_i gamma_j\n         +zeta gamma_j sum_(i<t<j)h_t gamma_t,         (11)\n\n    Q_(i,j)=(kappa+1-h_i)gamma_i^2-kappa gamma_j^2\n         -zeta gamma_i gamma_j\n         -zeta gamma_i sum_(i<t<j)h_t gamma_t.         (12)\n\nFor (11), gamma_j/gamma_i<=eta and the geometric sum bounds its right\nside divided by gamma_i^2 by\n\n    (kappa+1)eta^2+zeta H eta/(1-eta)-kappa\n       <= kappa/64+kappa/56-kappa <0.                (13)\n\nEquation (12) is at most -3kappa gamma_i^2<0. The optimum comparisons are\n\n    Q_(star,i)=-kappa gamma_i^2<0,\n    Q_(i,star)=0.                                   (14)\n\nEquations (5) and (11)--(14) prove all vertex normals for the ONE function\n(2) with the gradients (7). Consequently actual GD from the anchor (9)\nhas exactly the supplied gradients and values, in the given order.\nThere are no independent function values assigned to characters.\n\n### 2.2 Actual polar distance and a complementarity certificate\n\nWe next prove u_i>0 in (9); this is essential to the exact denominator.\nThe sequence v_i is strictly decreasing because\n\n    v_(i+1)-v_i\n      =gamma_i[zeta h_i-(kappa+1)(1-eta)]\n      <=gamma_i[kappa/8-(kappa+1)+kappa/8]<0.          (15)\n\nMoreover v_n>=zeta h_0 and, for every i,\nv_i-v_n<=(kappa+1)gamma_i. Set D_0=1+(N-1)zeta. Then\n\n    D_0(v_n-tau)\n      =(1-zeta)v_n-zeta sum_i(v_i-v_n)\n      >=zeta[h_0(1-zeta)-(kappa+1)/(1-eta)].          (16)\n\nSince h_0 zeta<=kappa/8 and h_0>=1+4kappa, the first term in brackets\nis at least 1+31kappa/8. The second is at most\n1+kappa+2(kappa+1)eta<=1+5kappa/4, using eta<1/2.\nThe bracket is strictly positive. Thus v_i>=v_n>tau and every u_i>0.\n\nFor Z in C^polar, (9) now gives <X_0,Z><=0. Therefore\n\n    ||X_0-Z||^2=||X_0||^2+||Z||^2-2<X_0,Z>\n                  >=||X_0||^2.                     (17)\n\nThe point 0 is in C^polar and achieves equality. Thus 0 is the actual\nclosest minimizer to X_0, even though F_C can have other minimizers.\nEquivalently, u is a strictly positive maximizer in the polar formula\nmax_(a>=0)(2b^T a-a^T K a), where b_i=<g_i,X_0>: it satisfies Ku=b\nand zero stationarity residual, hence complementarity. To check the\nmaximization directly, for any a the objective is\nb^T K^(-1)b-(a-u)^T K(a-u).\n\nThe TRUE squared initial distance is consequently\n\n    R^2=||X_0||^2\n       =[sum_i v_i^2-zeta(sum_i v_i)^2/D_0]/(1-zeta). (18)\n\nNo replacement of the polar distance by the norm to an arbitrary\nminimizer has been made. Its equality to that norm follows from (17).\n\nThe exact normalized final payoff is\n\n    V_eq(h)=(2kappa+1)eta^(2n)/R^2.                  (19)\n\nSince ||g_0||=1 and <X_0,g_0>=kappa+1, one has\nR^2>=(kappa+1)^2. Hence\n\n    0<V_eq(h)<=eta^(2n)<64^(-n).                    (20)\n\nAccepted fact b98bb807d514e80d gives B_n<=1+4n^p for p=log_2 3<2,\nso B_n V_eq(h)<(1+4n^p)64^(-n)->0 uniformly over this policy's schedules.\nIt therefore cannot give the exact profile lower bound.\n\nThis failure also occurs in a concrete excess-total-step regime: for\nn>=8 take every h_i=n. Then s(h)=n^2 and\n4n^p<2n^2 (at n=8 this is 108<128, and n^(2-p) increases).\nThus 1/(1+2s(h))<1/B_n. Both that Huber witness and (19) miss 1/B_n.\nFor the latter assertion, 1+4n^p<=5n^2<=5*4^(n-1)<64^n for n>=1;\nthe middle bound follows inductively from (n+1)^2/n^2<=4.\nThese statements concern the displayed witnesses, not W_n(h).\n\nThe scope h_i>1 is intentional; this rule does not cover h_i<=1 or zero\nsteps. Its non-dyadic version changes the ambient character space, not\nthe physical horizon, and every comparison above is with the original B_n.\n\n## 3. A different policy: product weights and maximal adjacent amplitudes\n\nFix any integer d>=1 and distinct chronological binary labels i with\n0<=i<=n<2^d. Choose bit correlations -1<rho_l<1, 0<=l<d. They may\ndepend on the whole prescribed schedule and horizon. Define\n\n    w_s=product_(l=0)^(d-1) ((1+(-1)^(s_l)rho_l)/2),\n    c_ij=product_(l: i_l!=j_l)rho_l,\n    g_i=(gamma_i sqrt(w_s)chi_s(i))_s.                (21)\n\nExpanding the product of the d two-term sums proves directly that\nsum_s w_s chi_s(i)chi_s(j)=c_ij. In particular sum_s w_s=1 and\nK_ij=gamma_i gamma_j c_ij. All weights are strictly positive, so the\nfull Walsh matrix gives independent gradients at distinct labels.\nNo limit on chronological coordinate jumps is imposed.\n\nFor h_i>1, set gamma_0=1 and prescribe\n\n    k_i=c_(i,i+1),\n    r_i=[-(h_i-2)k_i\n             +sqrt((h_i-2)^2 k_i^2+4(h_i-1))]/2,\n    gamma_(i+1)=r_i gamma_i.                         (22)\n\nThis is a concrete positive finite formula, not an unknown amplitude\noptimization. The r_i is the largest positive adjacent amplitude ratio\nallowed by cocoercivity: indeed\n\n    Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i)\n       =gamma_i^2[r_i^2+(h_i-2)k_i r_i+1-h_i]=0.     (23)\n\nEvery adjacent sum is thus exactly zero independently of the values f_i\nand anchor. If the entire transcript were legal, each of its two terms\nwould be zero. The question is whether all the other pairs can coexist\nwith these local equalities. The following calculation answers it for\nthe actual critical ruler words.\n\n## 4. Exact triangle identities on three chronological samples\n\nTemporarily label three consecutive samples 0,1,2, with local steps\n1+t,1+u, where t,u>0. The local labels need not be their original binary\nlabels. Suppose their normalized correlations are\n\n    corr(g_0,g_1)=p,\n    corr(g_0,g_2)=z,\n    corr(g_1,g_2)=p z.                               (24)\n\nPut A=||g_0||>0, r=||g_1||/A>0, q=||g_2||/A>0. Define\n\n    d_1=r^2+(t-1)pr-t,\n    d_2=q^2+(u-1)rpzq-u r^2,\n    U=t+pr, V=1-pr.                                 (25)\n\nThus Q_01+Q_10=A^2 d_1 and Q_12+Q_21=A^2 d_2. For arbitrary assigned\nvalues define the two directed triangle sums\n\n    T_rev=Q_20+Q_12+Q_01,\n    T_fwd=Q_02+Q_10+Q_21.                            (26)\n\nThe anchor and every f_i cancel from these sums. Their exact values are\n\n    T_rev/A^2=d_2+U(qz-1),\n    T_fwd/A^2=(1+u)d_1+d_2+V(tu-qz).                (27)\n\nHere is a derivation retaining their orientation. For any anchor set\nY_i=X_i-g_i and N_ij=<Y_i,g_j-g_i>. The array N_ij equals Q_(j,i)\nwhen the values are <X_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. For any other values the\ndifferences are vertex-potential differences and cancel around a cycle.\nLet alpha=N_01 and beta=N_12. Since Y_0-Y_1=tg_0+g_1 and\nY_1-Y_2=ug_1+g_2, expansion gives\n\n    N_10=A^2 d_1-alpha, N_21=A^2 d_2-beta,\n    N_02=alpha+beta+A^2[U(qz-1)-d_1],\n    N_20=A^2(d_1+d_2)-alpha-beta\n                       -A^2[V(qz-tu)-u d_1].        (28)\n\nFor the third equality use\nr(tp+r)=U+d_1. For the fourth use\nr(r-p)=tV+d_1. Summing the appropriate orientations in (28) proves\n(27) for arbitrary original values.\n\nMultiplying (27) by V and U respectively yields the identity\n\n    V T_rev+U T_fwd\n       =A^2[(U+V)d_2+U(1+u)d_1+UV(tu-1)].           (29)\n\nUnder adjacent saturation, d_1=d_2=0. If -1<p<1 then U,V>0 and\n\n    U+V=1+t,\n    UV=r^2(1-p^2).                                 (30)\n\nFor completeness, r is the positive root of\nP(r)=r^2+(t-1)pr-t. If p>0 then P(1/p)=1/p^2-1>0, so pr<1;\nif p<=0, V>0 directly. If p<0 then P(-t/p)=t^2(1/p^2-1)>0,\nso pr>-t; if p>=0, U>0 directly. The product identity in (30) follows\nby substituting d_1=0 in (25).\n\nAll six coefficients on the left side of (29) are positive and their\nsum is 3(U+V). It follows for ANY anchor and ANY assigned values that\n\n    max_(i!=j in {0,1,2}) Q_ij\n       >= (1-p^2)||g_1||^2 (tu-1)/[3(1+t)].         (31)\n\nIf tu>1 this is a strict positive obstruction to full interpolation.\nIt is not merely an inability to choose a convenient anchor or a\nfailure of one individual function-value assignment.\n\n## 5. Apply the obstruction to the actual H_m, without padding\n\nFor a fixed enclosing horizon m>=4 set\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n=2^m-1.                                       (32)\n\nThe same a,c are retained inside all subwords. Equivalently the original\nstep at i is 1+a c^(nu_2(i+1)), where nu_2 is the exponent of 2 dividing\nthe positive integer i+1. Use the standard chronological binary labels\nof the n+1 actual samples in (21).\n\nFor any level 3<=k<=m-1, take consecutive original samples\n\n    j_0=2^k-2, j_1=2^k-1, j_2=2^k.                 (33)\n\nTheir two actual steps are 1+a and 1+a c^k. Their correlations in (21)\nhave exactly the form (24), with\n\n    p=rho_0, z=product_(l=1)^k rho_l.                (34)\n\nIndeed the first change flips only bit 0; the last pair flips bits 0\nthrough k; and the outer pair flips bits 1 through k. The higher bits\nare unchanged. The same calculation applies to every level-k bridge,\nnot just the first occurrence in (33).\n\nBy (22), d_1=d_2=0. Here t=a<sqrt(2)-1<1/2, u=a c^k, and\n\n    tu=a^2 c^k=(1-1/m)^2 c^(k-2)\n       >=(9/16)sigma>9/8.                           (35)\n\nConsequently the original six residuals on (33), for every anchor and\nevery possible value array, obey\n\n    max Q_ij > (1-rho_0^2)||g_(j_1)||^2/36 >0.       (36)\n\nThis excludes the entire product-weight/adjacent-saturation policy on\nevery actual H_m with m>=4. The bit weights can depend arbitrarily on\nm and h inside -1<rho_l<1; there are 2^m positive character weights;\nthe high-frequency characters are not discarded. No normalized dual\ntensor-array obstruction is used: this is an identity for the proposed\nPRIMAL gradients and the original interpolation residuals.\n\nFor a non-dyadic horizon, the same local obstruction applies whenever\nthe original unpadded word contains a triple of the form (24) with tu>1.\nNo universal claim about other non-dyadic words follows.\n\n### Singular boundary is a real escape, with a weak explicit example\n\nThe condition |rho_0|<1 cannot be removed from (36). Set rho_0=-1 and\nrho_l=1 for l>=1. Only the parity character s=e_0 then has nonzero\nweight. With (22), r_i=h_i-1 and the scalar gradients are\n\n    g_i=(-1)^i product_(t<i)(h_t-1).\n\nThey are exactly the legal trajectory of F(x)=x^2/2 from x_0=1. This\nis a concrete counterexample to extending the impossibility statement\nto all singular product kernels. On (32), the exact terminal payoff is\n\n    V_sing=product_(i<n)(h_i-1)^2\n          =(1-1/m)^(2n) c^(-2m).                    (37)\n\nTo check the last equality, among i+1=1,...,2^m-1 the number with\nnu_2(i+1)=k is 2^(m-k-1). Their valuations sum to\nsum_(l=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-l)-1)=2^m-m-1=n-m;\nhence the product is a^n c^(n-m)=(ac)^n c^(-m), and ac=1-1/m.\nThe true initial distance is 1. This singular escape is also far below\nthe exact benchmark, since B_n V_sing<=(1+4n^p)(1-1/m)^(2n)c^(-2m)->0.\nFor example (1-1/m)^(2n)<=exp(-2n/m), which dominates the polynomial\nfactor when n=2^m-1. No perturbation argument transfers legality or\nconstants from this singular example to positive weights.\n\n## 6. Necessary repair cost for unsaturated amplitudes\n\nRetain the product triple (24) and arbitrary positive amplitudes. Define\nthe actual adjacent cocoercivity slacks\n\n    S_01=-(Q_01+Q_10)=-A^2 d_1,\n    S_12=-(Q_12+Q_21)=-A^2 d_2.                      (38)\n\nFor a legal transcript these are nonnegative. If |p|<1 and d_1<=0,\nthen r is at most the positive root of P from Section 4. The same\nroot estimates used there give U>0,V>0. Therefore every legal\ntranscript with these three gradients must satisfy, by (29),\n\n    (1+t)S_12+U(1+u)S_01 >= A^2 UV(tu-1).           (39)\n\nIf the leaf edge stays saturated, S_01=0 and UV A^2=(1-p^2)||g_1||^2.\nThen the bridge alone must pay\n\n    S_12 >= (1-p^2)||g_1||^2(tu-1)/(1+t).            (40)\n\nThus changing the product weights while retaining all adjacent roots\ncannot repair the failure. An amplitude mutation must introduce the\nquantified local slack, or a weight mutation must break the correlation\nfactorization (24), or the construction must use a genuinely singular\ncase with a separate analysis. These are necessary alternatives only.\nEquations (39)--(40) do not imply all other ordered pairs, supply a\ncommon anchor for a repaired policy, or determine an exact payoff.\n\n## 7. Literature engagement and remaining task\n\nMatlas searches for the product Walsh and GD construction mechanisms\nreturned no directly applicable theorem. The exact paper by Arthur C.\nCampello, *Density Estimation on the Binary Hypercube using Transformed\nFourier-Walsh Diagonalizations*, arXiv:2304.05053 (2023), was downloaded\nto downloads/2304.05053.pdf. Its Sections 2.1 and 2.3, including the\nderivation of the product kernel in equations (26)--(28), were read\nthrough the built-in PDF parser after local pdftotext and Python PDF\nlibraries were found unavailable. No installation was attempted.\n\nThe paper concerns density kernels. Its product structure informs the\nchosen correlation policy; it supplies no chronological GD normal\ninequality. No external mathematical result is imported into the proof:\nthe character identities, projection realization, cycle formulas, and\nnormalization are all derived above.\n\nThe original all-schedule exact-profile problem remains unresolved.\nThe remaining character direction is a concrete non-saturating or\nnon-product policy that passes the retained cycles AND every other\noriginal pair, has a controlled true polar distance, and attains the\noriginal B_n benchmark. The two rules resolved here do not do that.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["7c9425b3f049c5f6", "016467bc5c3eb666"], "files": ["local_memory/character_gram_cycle_obstruction.md", "local_memory/check_character_gram_cycles.py"], "status_note": "One joint self-contained unverified candidate. No new fact_submit, solver, parameter search or installation."}, "glossary": {"V_eq": "Exact true-distance normalized payoff of the equicorrelation character rule (6)-(19) in this draft.", "rho_l": "Real bit correlation in (-1,1) for the product-character rule (21).", "chi_s(i)": "Real binary character (-1)^(s dot i) on the standard chronological binary labels.", "w_s": "Explicit character weights, separately specified for the two candidate policies.", "gamma_i": "Positive sampled gradient amplitude, explicitly determined by (6) or (22).", "kappa": "The legal baseline's margin min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4).", "zeta": "The legal baseline's positive off-diagonal correlation kappa/(8 max_i h_i).", "eta": "The legal baseline's chronological amplitude ratio kappa/[8(max_i h_i+kappa+1)].", "U,V": "Positive coefficients t+pr and 1-pr in the local three-sample cycle calculation, not objective values.", "S_01,S_12": "Nonnegative adjacent cocoercivity slacks for a legal local three-sample transcript."}}
{"id": "1caba4ddf7f2ca22", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:26:27.644219+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "max4 round14 strengthening: PRODUCT independence is unnecessary at higher actual ruler bridges. For ANY nonnegative real sign/character weights with two nondegenerate adjacent correlations, if both adjacent cocoercivity inequalities saturate at steps1+t,1+u with1/3<t<1 and (3t-1)u>=1+t, one original directed triangle has an explicit strictly positive residual. Hence every everywhere-saturated character-amplitude policy fails actual H_m for m>=32, at every level k>=4, even with arbitrary dependent weights and distinct labels. An exact strictly positive four-character TWO-STEP legal example shows tu>1 alone is insufficient for this broader claim. Complete self-contained unverified supplement; unrestricted amplitudes and the target remain open.", "evidence": "# An obstruction to adjacent saturation for arbitrary real character weights\n\nStatus: **self-contained unverified supplement from round 14**. No numerical\nsearch, optimization, installation, or fact submission. Accepted fact\n`1252b8823bb5a290` supplies only the necessary ordered interpolation\ninequalities. Every further assertion used here is proved below. The earlier\nround-14 product-kernel draft is awareness, not a mathematical predecessor.\n\nThis supplement removes the product-weight restriction from the saturation\nobstruction, at the cost of a stronger condition on the two physical steps.\nIt rules out all nondegenerate real sign-kernel policies that saturate both\nadjacent cocoercivity constraints at the indicated bridges of actual H_m.\nIt does NOT rule out arbitrary character amplitudes with nonzero slack.\n\n## 1. The local sign constraint\n\nTake three consecutive actual samples X_0,X_1,X_2, whose local GD steps are\nh_0=1+t, h_1=1+u. These are temporary local indices. Let their gradients\nhave a common real sign representation\n\n    g_i=(gamma_i sqrt(w_s) epsilon_(i,s))_s,\n    w_s>=0, sum_s w_s=1, gamma_i>0, epsilon_(i,s) in {-1,1}.\n\nIn particular this includes every real character kernel, with arbitrary\nnonnegative weights, on any three labels. Multiplying each coordinate by\nepsilon_(0,s) does not change its Gram. Set\n\n    A=gamma_0, r=gamma_1/gamma_0, s=gamma_2/gamma_1,\n    p=<g_0,g_1>/(gamma_0 gamma_1),\n    w=<g_1,g_2>/(gamma_1 gamma_2),\n    z=<g_0,g_2>/(gamma_0 gamma_2).                    (1)\n\nThe local letter s is an amplitude ratio; it is not the coordinate index\nin the preceding display. Regard the weights as a probability measure.\nThe signs B=epsilon_0 epsilon_1 and D=epsilon_1 epsilon_2 have means p,w,\nand E[BD]=z. Since |B-D|=1-BD pointwise, the triangle inequality gives\n\n    z<=1-|p-w|.                                    (2)\n\nThis is an elementary real-sign restriction, stronger than positive\nsemidefiniteness of a generic three-by-three correlation matrix. It needs\nno independence of the weights or sign variables. For distinct character\nlabels and every character weight positive, p,w are strictly inside\n(-1,1). The same is true for any nonnegative weights for which the two\nadjacent gradients are not parallel or antiparallel.\n\nAssume now\n\n    1/3<t<1, u>1, (3t-1)u>=1+t,\n    -1<p<1, -1<w<1,                               (3)\n\nand that both adjacent cocoercivity comparisons are saturated. For\narbitrary values f_i define, as in accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290,\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThe saturation conditions are exactly\n\n    r^2+(t-1)pr-t=0,\n    s^2+(u-1)ws-u=0.                               (4)\n\nEvaluating these quadratics at t,1 and 1,u, respectively, proves\n\n    t<r<1, 1<s<u,\n    p=(r^2-t)/[(1-t)r],\n    w=(u-s^2)/[(u-1)s].                            (5)\n\nAll endpoint signs are strict because of the nondegeneracy in (3).\n\n## 2. One original three-sample cycle is necessarily positive\n\nSet T=Q_02+Q_10+Q_21. Values and the anchor cancel from T. Expand the\nthree position differences using the two actual steps. Substituting (4)\nin the resulting Gram expression gives\n\n    T/A^2=u r(r-p)-r s(z-rw)\n         =r s(Z_min-z),\n    Z_min=rw+u(r-p)/s.                             (6)\n\nFor example, before substitution the Gram expression for T is\n-t K_00+K_11+K_22+(t-u-1)K_01+u K_12-K_02, where K_ij=<g_i,g_j>.\nThe two equalities (4) reduce it to\nu(K_11-K_01)+K_12-K_02, proving (6).\n\nWrite Z_max=1-|p-w|, so z<=Z_max by (2). The following two factorizations\nprove Z_min>Z_max and quantify the positive original cycle.\n\nIf w<=p, define\n\n    L_minus=[t(u-1)/r+tu-1]/(1-t).\n\nSubstitute (5) and factor to get\n\n    Z_min-Z_max\n       =(u-s)(1-r)(L_minus-s)/[s(u-1)].             (7)\n\nFor clarity this follows by first writing\ns(Z_min-Z_max)=s w(r-1)+ur-s+(s-u)p, substituting w, and factoring\n(u-s)[ru-1+(r-1)s-(u-1)p]/(u-1).\nSubstitution of p from (5) then gives (7).\nMoreover\n\n    L_minus-u\n       =[t(u-1)(1/r-1)+(3t-1)u-t-1]/(1-t)>0.       (8)\n\nThe first term is strictly positive by (5) and the second is\nnonnegative by (3). Equations (6)--(8) yield\n\n    T >= A^2 r(u-s)(1-r)(L_minus-s)/(u-1)\n       > A^2 r(u-s)^2(1-r)/(u-1)>0.                (9)\n\nIf w>=p, define\n\n    L_plus=[t(u-1)/r+1-tu]/(1-t).\n\nThe other factorization is\n\n    Z_min-Z_max\n       =(u+s)(1+r)(L_plus-s)/[s(u-1)].              (10)\n\nHere s(Z_min-Z_max)=s w(r+1)+ur-s-(u+s)p, and factoring gives\n(u+s)[ru+1-(r+1)s-(u-1)p]/(u-1); (5) proves (10).\n\nTo show the last factor positive, let s_p be the positive root of\nv^2+(u-1)pv-u=0. The function (u-v^2)/[(u-1)v] decreases strictly\nfor v>0, so w>=p implies s<=s_p. Also (3) implies tu>1, because\nt(1+t)>(3t-1) is equivalent to (1-t)^2>0. The polynomial defining\ns_p evaluated at v=1/r is\n\n    (u-1/t)(p/r-1)<0.                              (11)\n\nThe first factor is positive. The second is negative since (4) gives\nr(r-p)=t(1-pr)>0; alternatively substituting (5) gives r>p.\nThus r s_p>1. One has 1-p s_p>0: for p>0 the defining polynomial\nat 1/p is 1/p^2-1>0; for p<=0 this is immediate.\n\nAt w=p, s=s_p and Z_max=1. Direct substitution in Z_min gives\n\n    Z_min(s_p)-1\n       =(1-p^2)(r s_p-1)/(1-p s_p)>0.              (12)\n\nApply (10) at s_p. Its other factors are positive, hence L_plus>s_p.\nTherefore L_plus>s, and\n\n    T >= A^2 r(u+s)(1+r)(L_plus-s)/(u-1)>0.         (13)\n\nIn either case at least one of the THREE actual ordered residuals\nQ_02,Q_10,Q_21 is at least T/3>0. This holds for every anchor and\nevery value assignment. A legal convex 1-smooth function cannot realize\nthese three gradients with the two given steps. No optimum comparison\nor additional remote comparison can remove the positive cycle.\n\n## 3. Consequence for actual H_m and arbitrary character weights\n\nFix m>=32, sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, and\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j) for j<m. These are the actual\nphysical parameters at this enclosing horizon, not reset subword\nparameters. The length is n=2^m-1.\n\nChoose ANY nonnegative real character weights summing to 1, not necessarily\nproduct weights. Take distinct labels for the actual n+1 chronological\nsamples, and positive amplitudes. Whenever the two adjacent normalized\ncorrelations are in (-1,1), the sign inequality (2) applies. In particular\nevery strictly positive character-weight policy has this property.\n\nAt every level-k ruler bridge with k>=4, the preceding step is a leaf:\nthe two actual steps are 1+a and 1+a c^k. Thus t=a and u=a c^k.\nWe have\n\n    3/8<a<1/2,\n    u >= a c^4=(1-1/m)c^3 > (31/32)(12/5)^3\n                                      =1674/125>11. (14)\n\nFor the first lower bound, sigma<5/2 and m>=32 give\na>(31/33)(2/5)=62/165>3/8; the upper bound follows from\na<1/sigma=sqrt(2)-1<1/2. For the bound on u use sigma>12/5.\nThe function (1+t)/(3t-1) decreases for t>1/3 and equals 11 at t=3/8.\nHence (3t-1)u>1+t. All hypotheses of Section 2 hold whenever the two\nadjacent comparisons are saturated and nondegenerate.\n\nConsequently every such saturation policy violates an original cycle\non every actual H_m with m>=32. For standard binary labels, one may use\nsamples 2^k-2,2^k-1,2^k. The proof in fact works for any distinct labels\nassigned to those original samples: it uses the universal real-sign\nconstraint, not a permissible reordering of GD steps.\n\nThis strengthens the product-policy result in the first round-14 draft.\nWeight dependencies alone cannot save an everywhere-saturated amplitude\npolicy at these bridges. Some adjacent slack or a separately analyzed\ndegenerate adjacent correlation is necessary. This is NOT an obstruction\nto arbitrary real character Grams with freely changing amplitudes.\n\n## 4. The stronger step condition cannot be replaced by tu>1\n\nHere is an exact local counterexample to that proposed extension. Set\n\n    t=2/5, u=5, r=99/100, s=24/5,\n    p=5801/5940, w=-451/480,\n    Z_max=1+w-p, Z_min=rw+u(r-p)/s,\n    z=(Z_max+Z_min)/2.                              (15)\n\nThese parameters satisfy (4) and t u=2>1. However the hypothesis\n(3t-1)u>=1+t fails: its two sides are 1 and 7/5. Both adjacent\ncorrelations are strictly between -1 and 1. Equation (7), whose\nalgebra does not require (3), gives\n\n    L_minus=1295/297 <24/5,\n    Z_min-Z_max=-653/14256000<0.                    (16)\n\nThe four binary-character weights at labels 0,1,2 in F_2^2 are\n\n    w_00=(1+p+z+w)/4,\n    w_10=(1-p+z-w)/4,\n    w_01=(1+p-z-w)/4,\n    w_11=(1-p-z+w)/4.                              (17)\n\nAll are strictly positive. Direct substitution gives p>9/10,\n-1<w<-9/10, and p+w>0. The exact correlation interval has length\nZ_max-[-1+|p+w|]=2(1-p)=278/5940.\nIts loss from Z_max to z is 653/28512000<1/1000<278/5940. Hence z lies strictly\ninside both correlation endpoints, which is equivalent to positivity\nof all four displayed weights.\n\nWith gamma=(1,r,rs), these weights give a positive definite three-gradient\nGram: the full four-label sign matrix is H tensor H for\nH=[[1,1],[1,-1]], and H^2=2I proves row independence. Equation (6)\nsays T<0. The reverse triangle is\n\n    T_rev/A^2=rs(tz+rw)-r(tp+r)<0:                  (18)\n\nthe first parentheses are negative (z,w<0), and tp+r>0.\nBoth adjacent sums vanish by (4). Thus assigning values to make each\nadjacent orientation zero also makes BOTH remaining original finite\ncomparisons negative.\n\nFor completeness this local example extends to one actual projection\nfunction, not just a Gram. Define D_0=(1+t)(K_00+K_01)/2 and\nD_1=(1+u)(K_11+K_12)/2. Put\nf_0=1+max_i K_ii/2+|D_0|+|D_1|, f_1=f_0-D_0,\nf_2=f_1-D_1. Then f_i>K_ii/2 for all i, every adjacent Q is zero,\nand the outer Q's are negative by (6),(18). Define\nb_i=f_i+K_ii/2+sum_(j<i)h_j K_ji and X_0=sum_i(K^(-1)b)_i g_i.\nPositive definiteness of K makes this explicit anchor finite. With\nX_i=X_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j, expansion gives\n<X_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>=Q_(j,i)<=0 and\n<X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-f_i+K_ii/2<0. Thus the single function\nmax_(v in conv(0,g_0,g_1,g_2))(<X,v>-||v||^2/2) has exactly these\ngradients and values on the two-step trajectory. Completing the square\nidentifies its gradient as projection; adding the projection normal\ninequalities proves the gradient is 1-Lipschitz. It is finite, convex,\nand minimized on the nonempty polar cone. This example makes no\npayoff claim or claimed equality for its true initial distance.\n\nThe example prevents overextending the obstruction to all tu>1, while\nSection 3 supplies the stronger condition at the actual higher bridges.\n\n## 5. Status and next direction\n\nBoth results are unverified candidates. The precise original-task\nobstruction is to the EXPLICIT rule that chooses the maximal positive\nroot of every adjacent cocoercivity inequality. The proof covers\narbitrary nonnegative character weights whenever the two tested\nadjacent correlations are nondegenerate; it does not require product\nweights, positive-only correlations, a fixed character count, bounded\nconditioning, or chronological label symmetry.\n\nThe one-character quadratic with alternating signs remains a legitimate\nsingular escape and is quantitatively weak as calculated in the first\nround-14 joint draft; that draft is not used as a proof premise here.\nThe next useful construction must specify non-saturating amplitudes\nand control their all-pair and true-distance consequences.
