Derives all non-fact identities anew; no verifier allowance."}, "glossary": {"p,w,z": "The three normalized real gradient correlations at consecutive samples, as defined in (1) of this supplement; w without a subscript is not a character weight.", "r,s": "Positive consecutive gradient-amplitude ratios gamma_1/gamma_0 and gamma_2/gamma_1 in the local triple.", "t,u": "Actual local step excesses h_0-1,h_1-1 in the three-sample obstruction.", "Z_min": "Necessary lower bound rw+u(r-p)/s on the outer normalized correlation when the indicated directed cycle is nonpositive.", "Z_max": "Real-sign upper bound 1-|p-w| on that same outer correlation.", "L_minus,L_plus": "Explicit factorization thresholds defined in (7) and (10); no optimization is used.", "T": "The original ordered interpolation cycle Q_02+Q_10+Q_21 in the temporary local indices."}}
{"id": "8bb6bb90c39813ef", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:27:22.362844+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C65 COMPLETE UNVERIFIED joint proof closes the ENTIRE specified parabolic family on actual H_m, m>=128: sup_(r0!=0,v0>=0) B_(2^m-1) P_par <=729/1600<1/2, including arbitrary multi-turn first-threshold crossings. New inherited-state bootstrap proves u_after<(39/20)d_firstturn for j>=1 and u<3/4<d1 for j0; the NEXT threshold cannot be crossed. Original unrestricted target remains open.", "evidence": "# C65: an amortized threshold budget for the entire parabolic family\n\nStatus: COMPLETE PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit, trajectory experiment,\noptimization, installation, or external theorem is used. This is a proposed\nclosure of the specified parabolic mechanism, NOT of the unrestricted\nworst-case functional or the original all-schedule target. The accepted fact\nb98bb807d514e80d supplies the definition of the exact benchmark. Every other\nargument, including the earlier C60/C63/C64 derivations needed here, is proved\ninside this document; those drafts are not assumed as predecessor facts.\n\n## 1. Statement and scope\n\nFix an integer m>=128. Throughout the WHOLE word, including embedded words,\nkeep the following enclosing-horizon parameters fixed:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n=2^m-1, d_j=a*c^j, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nFor this document only, H_j denotes these physical words, not the conjugate\nquadratics with the same letter in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. All displayed\nscalar r,u,v,x,q and block quantities below are local parabolic variables.\n\nDefine one function on the Euclidean plane by\n\n    K_par={(t,s):s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},\n    F_par(X)=max_(g in K_par)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).\n\nFor arbitrary r_0!=0 and v_0>=0 start GD at\n\n    X_0=(r_0(1+v_0+r_0^2),-v_0/sqrt(2))\n\nand use the ACTUAL physical word H_m. Write the iterates as\n\n    X_i=(x_i,-v_i/sqrt(2)),\n    x_i=r_i(1+u_i), u_i=v_i+r_i^2, q_i=r_i^2.\n\nThe true initial squared distance to the minimizer set is\n\n    R_0^2=r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2=x_0^2.\n\nThe normalized payoff is\n\n    P_par=2F_par(X_n)/R_0^2.\n\nLet B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 be the exact Zhang--Jiang denominator defined by\naccepted b98bb807d514e80d, with its full finite recurrence reproduced below.\nThe proposed conclusion is the UNIFORM proper-family bound\n\n    sup_(r_0!=0,v_0>=0) B_(2^m-1) P_par <=729/1600<1/2.       (1)\n\nIn particular the previously open domain of two or more horizontal sign\nchanges WITH a crossing of the first turning threshold is included.\n\nThe proof gives the following stronger chronological assertions. Discard an\nabsorbing trajectory r_i=0, whose payoff is zero. If the first sign change\nhas level j>=1, put D=d_j. Then\n\n    u_i<(39/20)D<d_(j+1)                                   (2)\n\nat every sample after that turn, whenever j+1 is used as a comparison level.\nHere d_(j+1)=cD is a defined number even at the last physical level. If the\nfirst sign change has level0, then\n\n    u_i<3/4<d_1                                            (3)\n\nthroughout the trajectory. Thus a first turning threshold may be crossed,\nbut the NEXT turning threshold cannot be crossed. This assertion is proved\nby an inherited-state budget, not assumed as an ordering of turn levels.\n\nFor j>=1, set k=m-j, N=2^k,\n\n    L=D/c, C=D/(M_j+L), beta=c*C,\n    E(T)=(2T+3)^2/[8(T+1)^2(2T+1)].\n\nThe more detailed payoff bounds are\n\n    P_par<=E(S_m)                              if there is no turn;\n    P_par<=c^[-2(m-1)]                         if first-turn level j=0;\n    P_par<=E(S_j) beta^[2(N-1)] c^(-2k)         if j>=1.       (4)\n\nThese conclusions concern exactly F_par and the specified starts and words.\nThey do not upper-bound W_n(H_m), do not weaken the accepted finite C59\nadversary, and do not decide the original all-horizon/all-schedule problem.\n\n## 2. One global function, every pair, the true distance, and actual GD\n\nThe set K_par is nonempty, closed and convex. Completing the square shows\nthat the maximum defining F_par is attained uniquely at the Euclidean\nprojection p(X) onto K_par: the equivalent minimization is coercive and\nstrictly convex. The projection normal inequalities at X and Y imply\n\n    ||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>.\n\nHence p is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two defining maxima gives\n\n    0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>\n      <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.\n\nThe remainder divided by ||Y-X|| tends to zero, so F_par is differentiable\nwith gradient p. It is convex as a supremum of affine functions, and its\ngradient is 1-Lipschitz on the whole plane.\n\nFor r real and v>=0 define\n\n    u=v+r^2,\n    X(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v/sqrt(2)),\n    G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).\n\nOne has X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any (t,s) in K_par,\n\n    <X-G,(t,s)-G><=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0.\n\nThus G(r) is the actual projection and gradient, and substitution yields\n\n    F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                       (5)\n\nFor EVERY ordered pair of such states, with f_i=F_par(X_i), expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n        =-(u_j/2)(r_i-r_j)^2<=0.                           (6)\n\nThe construction therefore retains every interpolation pair, not only the\nconsecutive pairs used in the estimates below.\n\nThe minimizer set is exactly {(0,y):y<=0}. At such points all feasible\ninner products are nonpositive and the feasible gradient0 gives value0.\nA point of positive vertical coordinate has positive value by testing a\nsmall positive vertical gradient. A point of nonzero horizontal coordinate\nhas positive value by testing (t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with small t of the same\nhorizontal sign: the linear term dominates the higher powers of t. The\nclosest minimizer to X_0 is consequently (0,-v_0/sqrt(2)), which proves the\nstated true denominator R_0^2. At a later block entry the corresponding\nclosest distance is its actual horizontal magnitude; the global minimum\nvalue is still0 for this SAME function.\n\nFor a physical step h>=0, actual GD is exactly\n\n    v_plus=v+h*r^2,\n    r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus=r(1+v+r^2-h).                 (7)\n\nThe cubic's derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1, so it has one real root.\nEquation(7) produces exactly the coordinates of X-hG. In particular\n\n    x_plus=x-h*r,\n    u_plus-u=(h-1)q+q_plus.                                (8)\n\nEvery step of H_m is greater than1, so u is nondecreasing on the actual\ntrajectory. This monotonicity is NOT asserted for arbitrary smaller steps.\nA sign change occurs precisely when u<d_level; equality makes r_plus=0.\nOnce r is zero, all remaining r-values and objective values are zero.\n\nAnother useful pointwise bound, valid at every state, is\n\n    2F_par(X)<=x^2.                                        (9)\n\nIndeed, after substituting q=r^2 and u=v+q, the difference is\nq[u+u^2-q/2]>=0 since u>=q.\n\n## 3. One-sign blocks retain both coordinates\n\nSuppose a consecutive actual block has a fixed nonzero sign. By horizontal\nreflection it is enough to consider positive r. At a sign-preserving step,\nthe cubic in(7) evaluated at the OLD r exceeds its right side by h*r(1+r^2).\nSince that cubic is increasing, 0<r_plus<=r. Thus for a whole block of mass T,\nentry (r_A,v_A), and exit (r_B,v_B),\n\n    Delta_x=x_A-x_B=sum h_i r_i,\n    Delta_v=v_B-v_A=sum h_i r_i^2,\n    T r_B<=Delta_x<=T r_A,\n    r_B Delta_x<=Delta_v<=r_A Delta_x,\n    T r_B^2<=Delta_v<=T r_A^2.                              (10)\n\nFor either sign, if R_A=|x_A| and u_B=v_B+r_B^2, this proves\n\n    |r_B|(1+T+u_B)<=R_A.                                   (11)\n\nIf u_A>=L>=0, it also proves the following vertical charge, including for\nany initial part of that block:\n\n    Delta_v<=|r_A| R_A=R_A^2/(1+u_A)<=R_A^2/(1+L).          (12)\n\nNo old vertical state is discarded in (10)--(12).\n\nFor a complete one-sign trajectory of mass T>=1/2, put w=1+v_B and q=r_B^2.\nThen R_A>=sqrt(q)(T+w+q), and w>=1+Tq, using v_A>=0. Thus its payoff with\nthe true entry distance is at most\n\n    (w+(3/2)q)/(T+w+q)^2.\n\nWith t=w+q>=1, one has q<=(t-1)/(T+1), so this is at most\n\n    [(2T+3)t-1]/[2(T+1)(T+t)^2].\n\nThe derivative has the sign of (2T+3)(T-t)+2. Its maximum on t>=1 is at\nt=T+2/(2T+3), which belongs to that interval for T>=1/2. Substitution gives\nE(T) as in(4). Two useful consequences are\n\n    E(T)<=3/(8T)                  for T>=1/2;\n    E(T)<9/(32T)                  for T>=8.                 (13)\n\nFor the first, bound w+(3/2)q by (3/2)(w+q) and use\nt/(T+t)^2<=1/(4T); the displayed E(T) inherits this bound where it applies.\nFor the second,\nT E(T)<[2+1/(T+1)]^2/16<=361/1296<9/32.\n\n## 4. Physical masses, binary accounting, and fixed constants\n\nThe recursive mass identity is\n\n    M_(j+1)=2M_j+d_j,\n    M_j=A_mass*c^j+(1-A_mass)*2^j,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2).                                        (14)\n\nHere 0<a<c-2 and hence 0<A_mass<1. To check the nontrivial strict inequality,\nwrite epsilon=1/m and use lambda^2=2lambda+1:\n\n    c(c-2)-(1-epsilon)\n      =epsilon(2lambda+3)+lambda^2 epsilon^2>0.\n\nConsequently\n\n    d_j/M_j<c-2.                                          (15)\n\nFor m>=128 the following convenient rational bounds hold:\n\n    12/5<c<73/30<5/2,\n    2/5<a<5/12,\n    c-2<13/30,\n    d_j>=d_1=1-1/m>=127/128                for j>=1.        (16)\n\nFor the upper bound on c, sqrt(2)<99/70 gives\nc<(169/70)(129/128)=21801/8960<73/30. The lower bound c>12/5 follows from\nsqrt(2)>7/5. Also a<(sqrt(2)-1)<5/12, and\na>(127/128)(30/73)=3810/9344>2/5.\n\nFix j>=1, D=d_j, L=D/c, M=M_j, delta=D/M, C=D/(M+L), beta=cC. Then\n\n    beta=c*delta/(1+delta/c)\n         <c^2(c-2)/(2c-2)\n         <69277/77400<9/10,\n    C<3/8.                                                (17)\n\nThe function c^2(c-2)/(2c-2) is increasing for c>2, because both c^2 and\n(c-2)/(2(c-1)) are positive and increasing. Substitute c=73/30 for the\nsecond inequality. The last bound uses c>12/5 and beta<9/10.\n\nLet p_2(t) be the number of1s in the binary expansion of a nonnegative\ninteger t, and let nu_2(t) be the exponent of2 in a positive integer t.\nCounting multiples of powers of2 gives\n\n    sum_(k=1)^t nu_2(k)=t-p_2(t).                           (18)\n\nFor completeness, write t=sum_s e_s 2^s with e_s in{0,1}. Then\nsum_(b>=1)floor(t/2^b)=sum_s e_s(2^s-1)=t-p_2(t).\n\nH_m consists of N=2^(m-j) chronological H_j blocks, indexed\nell=0,...,N-1. Block ell starts at sample ell*2^j, with inherited state\n(r_(ell*2^j),v_(ell*2^j)); its bridge input is sample (ell+1)*2^j-1 and\nits bridge output is sample (ell+1)*2^j. In particular\nR_ell=|x_(ell*2^j)|. The bridge after block ell has excess\n\n    D*c^[nu_2(ell+1)].                                    (19)\n\nThis follows by induction from H_(j+k+1)=(H_(j+k),1+d_(j+k),H_(j+k)).\nThe midpoint bridge has valuation k; the two child bridge lists have\ntheir earlier valuations unchanged. No physical step is aggregated in\nthe actual dynamics by writing this partition.\n\nWe shall need one explicit convergent series. Put rho=81/100. Then for c<5/2,\n\n    sum_(ell>=1) rho^(ell-1)c^[nu_2(ell+1)]<23.             (20)\n\nSeparate k=ell+1 by its binary valuation. The s=0 contribution is\nrho/(1-rho^2). For s>=1 the contribution is\n\n    t_s=c^s rho^(2^s-2)/(1-rho^(2^(s+1))).\n\nUse rho<5/6 and rho^2<2/3. The contributions for s=0,1,2,3,4 are respectively\nstrictly less than 3,5,6,5,3. Here the latter four can be bounded by\n\n    (5/2)/(1-(2/3)^2)<5,\n    (25/4)(2/3)/(1-(2/3)^4)<6,\n    (125/8)(2/3)^3/(1-(2/3)^8)<5,\n    (625/16)(2/3)^7/(1-(2/3)^16)<3.\n\nEach inequality follows by multiplying its positive integer denominators.\nFor the tail, (2/3)^15<1/400 and (2/3)^16<1/600 show t_5<1/3. For s>=5,\n\n    t_(s+1)/t_s\n      =c rho^(2^s)/(1+rho^(2^(s+1)))<1/240.\n\nThus the tail starting at s=5 is less than (1/3)/(1-1/240)<1. Summing the\nsix bounds gives(20). This is a scalar geometric-series proof, not a\ntrajectory evaluation or numerical search.\n\n## 5. First-turn structure and the actual crossing times\n\nIf a first turn occurs at step i, every earlier step preserved its sign and\nsatisfied d_level(t)<u_t. If the current step were no larger than some\nearlier step, monotonicity u_i>=u_t would forbid the turn. Thus the first\nturn is a NEW RECORD level j. Its preceding word is exactly the first H_j.\n\nFor j>=1, the preceding first occurrence of level j-1 did not turn or\nannihilate, so the first-turn input has\n\n    L=d_(j-1)<=u<D=d_j.                                   (21)\n\nLet q=r^2 at this input. The one-sign H_j prefix and(10) give\nv>=S_j q, and therefore\n\n    q<=u/M_j.                                            (22)\n\nLet R_1 be the actual horizontal magnitude immediately after that turn,\nthe entry to the next H_j block. From the exact two-coordinate update,\n\n    v_after=u+Dq,\n    R_1^2=q(D-u)^2.                                      (23)\n\nThese formulas keep the initial vertical state: v_after is its actual\naccumulated value, not an increment from a newly chosen origin.\n\nDefine the first crossing of a normal threshold D after the first turn by\n\n    tau(D)=min{t in{2^j,...,n}: u_t>=D},\n\nwith tau(D)=infinity when the set is empty. Define a putative escape from\nthe larger band by\n\n    sigma(2D)=min{t in{2^j,...,n}: u_t>2D}.\n\nThe first turning input is sample2^j-1 and its output is sample2^j;\nthe minimum of an empty set is infinity in both definitions.\n\nAll block entries and crossing states below are samples of the SAME\ntrajectory. A first escape may be inside a block or the output of a bridge;\nneither location is rounded into a fresh independent H_j instance.\n\n## 6. Amortized crossing budget when j>=1\n\nAfter the first turn u>=L. Hence every H_j block thereafter has one sign:\nits steps have excess at most L, and equality would cause absorption,\nalready excluded. Assume for contradiction that sigma(2D) is finite.\nUntil its input all bridge inputs have u<=2D.\n\nWrite R_ell for the actual horizontal magnitude entering block ell.\nFor a completed block ell>=1 and its next bridge, let w be the normal\nstate at that bridge input and s=nu_2(ell+1). Formula(11) and actual GD give\n\n    R_(ell+1)/R_ell<=|D c^s-w|/(M+w)<=C c^s.              (24)\n\nIf D c^s>=w, use w>=L and drop the subtraction in the numerator. If\nD c^s<w<=2D, then 0<w-D c^s<=D, so the ratio is at most C<=C c^s.\nThus(24) continues to hold after the FIRST threshold D has been crossed.\nIt also bounds the OUTPUT of the putative escape step when that step is\na bridge, because only its input normal state was used.\n\nFor each block entry reached by that time, (18) gives\n\n    R_ell<=R_1 C^(ell-1)c^[ell-p_2(ell)]\n           =R_1 beta^(ell-1)c^[1-p_2(ell)]\n           <=R_1 beta^(ell-1), ell>=1.                   (25)\n\nWithin a one-sign block the horizontal magnitude only decreases. The same\nbound therefore controls a partial final block if the first escape occurs\nthere. In particular the squared gradient at the escape output is at most\n\n    q_escape<=R_1^2/(1+L)^2.                             (26)\n\nBy(12), all complete or partial H_j blocks after the first turn contribute\nat most R_1^2/[(1+L)(1-rho)] to the vertical increment. At a bridge after\nblock ell, (11) bounds its squared input gradient by\nR_ell^2/(M+L)^2; its actual vertical increment is exactly\n(1+D c^s) times that quantity. Equations(20),(25) therefore show that,\nup to and INCLUDING the putative first escape output,\n\n    u_escape<=u+Dq+(K/D)R_1^2,                           (27)\n\nwhere one may use\n\n    K = D/[(1+L)(1-rho)]\n        +C^2[(1/D)/(1-rho)+23]\n        +D/(1+L)^2\n      <18.                                               (28)\n\nThere is no uncharged last fragment: (12) covers a partial block, the\nbridge sum covers an escape bridge, and(26) supplies the current q in\nu=v+q. If the first escape were the first turn's own output, the unused\nnonnegative future-charge allowances in(27) still bound it.\n\nHere are explicit constants for(28). Since L=D/c,\nD/(1+L)<c<5/2 and D/(1+L)^2<=c/4<5/8. Also\n(1/D)/(1-rho)<=12800/2413<11/2, and C<3/8. Therefore\n\n    K<250/19+(9/64)(11/2+23)+5/8\n      <53/4+513/128+5/8\n      =2289/128<18.\n\nPut z=u/D in[0,1] and delta=D/M. From(22),(23),(27),\n\n    u_escape/D<=z+q+Kq(1-z)^2\n                 <=(1+delta)z+K delta z(1-z)^2.\n\nSince z(1-z)<=1/4,\n\n    u_escape/D\n      <=(1+delta)z+(K delta/4)(1-z)\n      <=max{1+delta,K delta/4}<39/20<2.                  (29)\n\nWe used delta<13/30, K<18, and 1+13/30<39/20. This contradicts the definition\nof sigma(2D). Thus no escape exists. Applying the same finite charges at\nany actual sample proves(2), not just the absence of escape.\n\nNow(24) holds through the entire word. It also holds at the FIRST bridge:\nblock0 was one-sign by the definition of first turn, and its bridge input\nhas u>=L. Multiplying from block0 instead of block1 gives\n\n    R_(N-1)/R_0<=C^(N-1)c^[N-1-k]\n                 =beta^(N-1)c^(-k), k=m-j.               (30)\n\nThe final H_j is one-sign, has its inherited vertical state, and has true\nentry distance R_(N-1). The function's global final value is the same\nF_par(X_n). Apply Section3 to that final block and then(30), obtaining the\nj>=1 line of(4) with the ORIGINAL denominator R_0^2.\n\n## 7. First turn at level0: small post-turn amplitude and exact alignment\n\nHere the first turn is the very first step, with 0<=u_0<a. Put\n\n    A=x_1^2=q_0(a-u_0)^2,\n    b=ac=1-1/m, D=d_1=b.\n\nSince q_0<=u_0, maximizing t(a-t)^2 on[0,a] gives\n\n    A<=4a^3/27<1/90,\n    a+a^2<85/144<3/5,\n    D>=127/128.                                         (31)\n\nThe maximum is at t=a/3 (the endpoints give0); its derivative is\n(a-t)(a-3t). The strict bound1/90 follows from a<5/12 and\n4(5/12)^3/27=125/11664<1/90.\n\nIf u never reaches a, every step turns. Whenever all inputs through time t-1\nhave u<a, the exact horizontal update and(18) give\n\n    |x_t|<=R_0 b^t c^[-p_2(t)],\n    |x_t|<=sqrt(A) b^(t-1)c^[1-p_2(t)]   for t>=1.        (32)\n\nThe same inequalities hold at the OUTPUT of the first crossing, since its\ninput still had u<a. If there is no crossing, (9),(32) at t=n already give\nP_par<=b^(2n)c^(-2m), which is stronger than(4).\n\nOtherwise let tau=min{t>=1:u_t>=a}. We next bound the actual crossing state.\nIf tau=1, v_tau=u_0+a q_0<=a+a^2. If tau>1, its input index i=tau-1 is\npositive. If its level ell is0, (32) gives q_i<=A and\nh_i q_i<2A. If ell>=1, the integer i has at least ell trailing binary1s,\nso p_2(i)>=ell. Consequently\n\n    h_i q_i<=A[c^(2-2ell)+a c^(2-ell)]<=A(1+ac)<2A.\n\nSince v_i<=u_i<a, in this case v_tau<a+2A<=a+a^2; the latter uses\n2A<=8a^3/27<a^2. In every case(32) also gives |x_tau|^2<=A. Therefore\n\n    v_tau<=a+a^2,\n    u_tau<=a+a^2+A<3/5+1/90<D.                            (33)\n\nIf tau=n, all inputs in the word were still below a, and the first line\nof(32) again proves the stronger all-turn product bound. Suppose tau<n.\n\nIf tau is even, it is already the entry to an actual H_1 block. If tau is\nodd, retain the NEXT ACTUAL step, at index tau. It has level\nell=nu_2(tau+1)>=1. Its input satisfies u_tau<D<=d_ell by(33), so it turns\nand its horizontal factor is at most d_ell. Both estimates in(32) therefore\ncontinue to hold at time tau+1. The same trailing-ones calculation bounds\nthis one alignment bridge's vertical increment by2A.\n\nWe have now reached an even time2K, with K>=1, which is an actual entry to\nH_1; no state was reset. Its inherited state satisfies\n\n    u_(2K)>=a,\n    v_(2K)<=a+a^2+2A,\n    |x_(2K)|<=sqrt(A)b^(2K-1)c^[1-p_2(K)],\n    |x_(2K)|<=R_0 b^(2K)c^[-p_2(K)],\n    u_(2K)<=a+a^2+3A<D.                                  (34)\n\nThe identity p_2(2K)=p_2(K) explains the two retained binary weights.\n\nFrom here all H_1 low steps are one-sign, because u>=a. Bootstrap up to a\nputative first output with u>D. At a coarse bridge its input u<=D, so all\nsuch bridges turn and(24) applies with j=1, L=a, M_1=2+a and\n\n    C=D/(2+2a), beta=cC<9/10.\n\nLet R_ell=|x_(2ell)| for actual H_1 block entries ell>=K. The exact product\nover the inherited suffix, not a restarted word, gives\n\n    R_ell<=R_K beta^(ell-K)c^[p_2(K)-p_2(ell)]\n           <=sqrt(A) beta^(ell-K)c^[1-p_2(ell)].           (35)\n\nFor ell>=1 and s=nu_2(ell+1) one has\n\n    c^s c^[2-2p_2(ell)]<=c.                              (36)\n\nIf s=0, p_2(ell)>=1 gives a bound1. If s>=1, the last s binary digits of\nell are1, so p_2(ell)>=s and the expression is at most c^(2-s)<=c.\nThis identity is the missing chronological charge for a crossing close\nto a high-level bridge. Dropping the binary weight in(34) would lose it.\n\nBy(12),(35), the vertical cost of all remaining complete or partial H_1\nblocks is at most A/(1-rho). By(11),(35),(36), their bridges contribute at\nmost\n\n    A(1+D c)/[(2+2a)^2(1-rho)]< (7/8)A/(1-rho),\n\nbecause D<1, c<5/2 and 2+2a>=2. The squared gradient at any current sample\nis at most A, including the output of the proposed first escape; (35)\napplies to an escape bridge output using only its input normal state.\nThus the remaining cost to v+q is less than\n\n    [1+(1+7/8)/(1-rho)]A=(413/38)A<11A.\n\nTogether with(34), this yields\n\n    u_escape<a+a^2+13A<3/5+13/90=67/90<3/4<D.             (37)\n\nThis contradicts the proposed first crossing of D. The same bound applies\nat every subsequent sample; (33),(34) and u<a before tau cover all earlier\nones. This proves(3).\n\nThere are 2^(m-1) actual H_1 blocks, and the last has index\nJ=2^(m-1)-1 with p_2(J)=m-1. Combining the first inequality in(35) with\nthe second original-radius bound in(34) gives\n\n    R_J/R_0<=b^(2K) beta^(J-K)c^[-(m-1)]\n             <=c^[-(m-1)].                              (38)\n\nThe final H_1 step is one-sign, so |x_n|<=R_J. Equations(9),(38) give the\nj=0 payoff in(4). The previously separated cases of no crossing and a\ncrossing at the final sample have even smaller bounds. This accounts for\nevery possible location of the first threshold crossing, including inside\na partial block.\n\n## 8. What the threshold result says about the turns\n\nThis paragraph is a consequence of the budget, not an assumption used to\nobtain it. For a first turn of level j, all lower levels have stopped\nturning already: for j>=1 use u>=d_(j-1), and for j=0 there are no lower\nlevels. By(2),(3), u never reaches d_(j+1). Thus every level strictly\ngreater than j turns at each of its actual occurrences. Level j turns\nexactly at the occurrences whose INPUT index is less than tau(d_j), and\nnever again thereafter, apart from the separately excluded equality\nabsorption case. Monotonicity proves this chronological-prefix assertion.\n\nIn the N=2^(m-j) block decomposition there are N/2 occurrences of level j\nand N/2-1 bridges at higher levels. If ell_j is the number of level-j turns,\nthen the exact total number of turns is\n\n    N/2-1+ell_j, with 1<=ell_j<=N/2.                       (39)\n\nFor no threshold crossing ell_j=N/2. With a crossing it is the number of\nactual level-j occurrences before that crossing, not a separately chosen\nrestart count. The normal thresholds crossed after the first turn consist\nof at most the single level d_j. All earlier displacement and vertical\nhistory was charged in(23),(27) or(32)--(37).\n\n## 9. Exact benchmark comparison, with no asymptotic B_n assumption\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d, the exact recursions can be written with\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 as\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nHere C_n is a benchmark sequence, different from the block constant C in\nSections4--7. We prove directly the needed finite comparator\n\n    B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).                              (40)\n\nSet alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda) and\nLlin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Both coefficients in Llin exceed1, so Llin>=x+y.\nDirect expansion using lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives\n\n    alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]\n      =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.\n\nThus Llin>=Omega(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy. At\nz=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial having larger root G(x,lambda y) is\n\n    z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2\n      =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.\n\nAlso z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it lies to the right of that\nquadratic's vertex. Therefore G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y). Induction\nin the exact finite recurrences, using monotonicity of G in its second\nargument, proves B_n<=lambda C_n.\n\nPut eta=log_2(lambda)>1, Dpow(t)=(1+t)^eta-1 and ell(z)=log Dpow(exp z).\nFor t=exp z, differentiation yields\n\n    ell''(z)=eta*t*(1+t)^(eta-2)\n      *[(1+t)^eta-1-eta*t]/Dpow(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket vanishes at0 and has positive derivative for positive t.\nConvexity therefore gives Dpow(t)Dpow(1/t)>=Dpow(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 put\nZ=(x^(1/eta)+y^(1/eta))^eta. Substituting t=(y/x)^(1/eta) gives\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy, hence Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y by the increasing\nderivative of the positive power. It follows that Omega(x,y)<=Z; zero\narguments follow directly. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^eta. Together with\nB_n<=lambda C_n this proves(40).\n\nIt remains to compare(4) with(40). Equations(14),(16) imply\nA_mass>4/5 and hence, for j>=1,\n\n    S_j>(4/5)c^j-1>(23/60)c^j>c^j/3.                     (41)\n\nThe middle step uses c^j>12/5. For a first turn j>=1, k=m-j>=1,\n(13),(17),(40),(41) give\n\n    B_n P_par\n      <(9/8)(lambda/c)^m [lambda*c^(-k)]\n           beta^[2(2^k-1)]\n      <(9/8)(1/2)(81/100)=729/1600.                       (42)\n\nHere (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)<=1/2 by the binomial theorem,\nlambda c^(-k)<1, and beta<9/10.\n\nFor j=0, (4),(40) give\n\n    B_n P_par<=lambda^(m+1)c^[-2(m-1)]\n                <=lambda^(3-m)<1/8                      (43)\n\nfor m>=128. For no turns, c^m>20 gives S_m>(3/4)c^m and S_m>8, so(13)\nand(40) yield\n\n    B_n P_par<(3/8)lambda(lambda/c)^m<=3lambda/16\n                <729/1600.                             (44)\n\nFor the last strict comparison, lambda<17/7<243/100, since sqrt(2)<10/7;\nthen 3lambda/16<729/1600. Absorbing trajectories have payoff0. These cases\nexhaust all starts and establish(1) as a complete proof draft.\n\n## 10. Audit and unresolved original task\n\nEvery threshold argument used the exact h_i>1 recursion. The physical c,a\nwere kept fixed through all embedded H_j. Every local closest-distance\nestimate was multiplied by an explicit ratio to R_0, either(30) or(38).\nThe j=0 crossing was aligned by retaining its next physical bridge and a\nbinary-popcount weight; no right child was reset or independently optimized.\nThe finite benchmark comparison came from its recurrence, not a conjectured\nlimit for B_n. Equations(6),(7) supply global all-pair legality independently\nof the estimates.\n\nThe proof is UNVERIFIED and has not entered the fact graph. It is a concrete\ncandidate for separate selection if the main agent allocates a verifier gate.\nEven acceptance would close only this fixed parabolic mechanism on H_m for\nm>=128. {"id": "ea3a113bf973e199", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:33:12.013225+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact named dense-family obstruction candidate: independent weighted arithmetic/harmonic midpoint redistributions in BOTH exact-ZJ root children, with arbitrary weights in[1/2,3/2] and quarter root compensation, obey sum h<=S_bullet(n)+27(n-1)/8 whenever all actual steps remain nonnegative. Hence W_n(h)>=1/[B_n+27(n-1)/4], uniformly giving liminf W_n(h)/U_n^ZJ>=1. Entire full certificate may be reselected; no support/rank restriction. This is an unverified joint proof, not all dense directions or the original target.", "evidence": "# Dense full-model discovery and a common-offset mass obstruction\n\nStatus: joint **unverified proof draft**, cumulative round 16. The experiment\nis closed. No fact_submit is allocated or has been made. This is a named-family\nobstruction and a finite discovery report, not the original minimax theorem.\n\nAccepted inputs: b98bb807d514e80d defines the exact benchmark and supplies the\nHuber lower bound; 1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the complete interpolation/Gram\nmodel. The new argument below is proved in full, not imported from an earlier\nunverified finding. No numerical output is a premise of the proof.\n\n## 1. The actual experiment\n\nThe public runner `max_dense_full_pep.py` adapts only the tested public source\n`max3_c58_full_pep_batch_used.py`, SHA256\ne8e1d0ed399b924eff7be7414fa61fd5c5d94dd7966294f6f4dbd84ce8cc5bc5.\nThe original runner was not executed. The separate public ledger is\n`max_exact_benchmark_discovery_budget.json`; it is CLOSED at 16 evaluations\nand 98.02878378597961 measured import/build/solve seconds. Its unused time\ndoes not authorize another solve. The closed prior conic and direct ledgers\nare unchanged. Totals: 672 conic evaluations, 171.40263413495765 measured\nconic seconds; including the separate closed direct batch gives\n172.22190110096332 measured seconds.\n\nEvery solve uses the full Gram of (x0,g0,...,gn), every distinct ordered pair\non {star,0,...,n}, common function values, optimum zero, and ||x0||^2<=1.\nThere is no imposed rank, ray, mode, jump support, recursive dual support,\nor incoming-terminal deletion. The complete multipliers are reselected.\nFor M=1+sum h, the computational variables are\nGhat=Gram(x0,M*g0,...,M*gn), fhat=M*f. Diagnostics, flow, full remainders,\nand pair residuals are also evaluated in the original model.\n\nRaw files `max_dense_01_*_raw.json` through `max_dense_16_*_raw.json` retain\nthe evaluated steps and exact recipes, primal values and both Grams, every\nordered-pair label and raw multiplier, both optimum incidences, full raw\nremainders, objective gap, original/scaled signs/flow/PSD diagnostics, and\nmeasured cost. `max_dense_discovery_summary.json` indexes paths and hashes.\nNo eigenvalue or multiplier was repaired or truncated in those raw files.\n\nThe one-step exact calibration is h=(3/2), q=1/4, with\n\n q||x0||^2-2f1+Q10+Q0star+Q1star=||x0-2g0-2g1||^2/4.\n\nThe quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x0=1, attains q. The numerical solve matched this.\nThe second calibration is the deterministic exact-recurrence 15-step word,\nevaluated at 80 decimal digits, with root split 9. The full recursively\nconstructed source array has evaluated flow residual <4.5e-16 and full\nmatrix coefficient residual <1.8e-15. The solver matched 1/B15 to about\n6e-9 relative. Radical evaluation and tie selection are NOT interval\ncertificates; all candidate split values and the complete tree are retained.\n\nThe three dense directions use chronological tilt 0,-1/2,+1/2 in section 2.\nEvery step changes at n=15 and n=31. A five-step instance changes four steps\nand exposes a small geometric subproblem. All 14 concrete proposals were\nentered before filtering, and all passed the retained necessary screens.\n\nSelected numerical full-model ratios q*B_n (primal and dual agree at the\nshown precision; neither is asserted to be an exact certificate):\n\n| n | tilt | parameter | ratio |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| 5 | 0 | .02 | 1.02146826 |\n| 15 | 0 | .002 | 1.01213546 |\n| 15 | -1/2 | .002 | 1.01208733 |\n| 15 | +1/2 | .002 | 1.01240829 |\n| 15 | 0 | .02 | 1.12811266 |\n| 15 | -1/2 | .02 | 1.12763576 |\n| 15 | +1/2 | .02 | 1.13106896 |\n| 15 | 0 | .1 | 1.82570595 |\n| 15 | -1/2 | .1 | 1.82299772 |\n| 15 | +1/2 | .1 | 1.84671595 |\n| 31 | -1/2 | .0002 | 1.00274382 |\n| 31 | -1/2 | .002 | 1.02959946 |\n| 31 | -1/2 | .02 | 1.33895652 |\n| 31 | +1/2 | .02 | 1.35257699 |\n\nThe three n15 parameter .1 cases and the n31 back-tilted case returned\n`optimal_inaccurate`. The largest displayed original full-dual negative\neigenvalue magnitude is about6.3e-6; all such diagnostics are retained.\nThese data do not assert exact feasibility, local or global optimality,\nor nonexistence of other dense improving schedules. No apparent small gain\nis inferred from the baseline's floating discrepancy.\n\nWhat the complete model changed: it defeated directions that passed the\nglobal Huber, all single-cut, four fixed-curvature quadratic, and symmetric\nplateau-to-quadratic screens. The primal Grams have several substantial\ndirections, and the reselected arrays have nonzero optimum-source mass.\nRaw rank counts are tolerance dependent, particularly as the parameter\nshrinks; they are not exact rank theorems.\n\n## 2. The named dense family, enlarged beyond the experiment\n\nLet h^0 be any exact dominant word attaining S_bullet(n), and let k be its\nroot bridge index. Its root children are exact primitive and dominant source\nwords, possibly empty. On each nonempty child I of length m_I put\n\n d_i=h_i^0-1>0,        1/2<=w_i<=3/2,\n W_I=sum_(i in I)w_i,\n A_I=sum w_i*d_i/W_I,  H_I=W_I/(sum w_i/d_i),\n c_I=(A_I+H_I)/2,     v_i=w_i(d_i-c_I),\n V_I=sum_(i in I)v_i.\n\nChoose independent REAL amplitudes t_I for the children, and define\n\n h_i=h_i^0+t_I*v_i                    (i in child I),\n h_k=h_k^0-(1/4)sum_I t_I*V_I.        (root)\n\nRequire every actual h_i>=0. The amplitudes, weights, chosen maximizing\ntree, and horizon may all vary. The experiment used one common positive\namplitude and w_i=1+tilt*(2j/(m_I-1)-1), with singleton weight1.\nNo certificate restriction is made in this definition.\n\n**Claim.** Every such schedule satisfies, for n>=1,\n\n sum h_i <= S_bullet(n)+(27/8)(n-1),\n W_n(h) >= 1/[B_n+(27/4)(n-1)].                    (1)\n\nIn particular, uniformly over this entire enlarged family,\n\n W_n(h)/U_n^ZJ >= [1+27(n-1)/(4B_n)]^(-1) ->1.    (2)\n\nThis is a lower bound for unrestricted worst-case performance on the named\nphysical schedules. Full dual reselection, higher-rank remainders, both\noptimum incidences and arbitrarily many new edges cannot evade it.\n\n## 3. A block inequality from nonnegativity\n\nConsider any nonempty base block h_i^0=1+d_i>1 of length m. Let its minimum\nstep be h_min, attained at j, and assume weights in [w_min,w_max] with\nw_min>0. Define A,H,c,v,V as above. Write d_min=h_min-1.\n\nThe elementary weighted arithmetic/harmonic inequality H<=A follows from\n\n (sum w_i*d_i)(sum w_i/d_i) >= (sum w_i)^2:\n\nexpand the difference as sum_(i<j)w_i*w_j*(d_i/d_j+d_j/d_i-2), whose terms\nare nonnegative because (d_i-d_j)^2/(d_i*d_j)>=0. Also H>=d_min, since\nsum w_i/d_i <= (sum w_i)/d_min. Therefore, with W=sum w_i,\n\n V=W(A-H)/2=W(c-H)>=0,\n V<=W(c-d_min).                                    (3)\n\nPut delta=c-d_min>=0. If delta=0, (3) forces V=0. If delta>0 and t>=0\nkeeps the jth step nonnegative, then\n\n 0<=h_min-t*w_j*delta,\n t*delta<=h_min/w_j,\n t*V<=W*h_min/w_j <=m*(w_max/w_min)*h_min.          (4)\n\nIf t<0, t*V<=0, so the same upper bound holds. This is valid for each block\nseparately and permits independent amplitudes. It uses one actual small\nstep in that block; it is not a bound on a compressed recursion.\n\nMore generally the same argument works for any center c satisfying\nH<=c<=A and A-c<=c-d_min. The midpoint rule meets these conditions.\n\n## 4. Source words have a bounded small step in every nonempty child\n\nEvery source step is strictly greater than1. For a primitive bridge, writing\nA=1+sum(left), C=1+sum(right), the positive number b-1 solves\n(b-1)^2+(A+C)(b-1)-AC=0. For a dominant bridge it has the form\nb=1+A/r with r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2>1 and D>0.\n\nEvery nonempty primitive source word contains a step sqrt(2): descend its\nfinite binary tree to a node with two empty children, where phi(0,0)=sqrt(2).\nFor a nonempty dominant word, if any primitive child on its dominant spine\nis nonempty it contains such a step. Otherwise the last node on that spine\nhas two empty children, giving psi(0,0)=3/2. Thus every nonempty source word\nhas h_min<=3/2, without any assumption about maximizing split patterns.\n\nApply (4) to each root child, with w_max/w_min<=3 and h_min<=3/2. Then\n\n sum_I t_I V_I <= (9/2)sum_I m_I=(9/2)(n-1).\n\nThe root compensation makes the change of the TOTAL physical step sum\nexactly (3/4)sum_I t_I V_I. This proves the first inequality of (1), even\nwhen one child is empty or both children are singletons.\n\nFor any nonnegative schedule of total sum S, the legal scalar Huber\nfunction of threshold a=1/(1+2S), started at1, has all iterates >=a,\nx_n=1-aS and2f(x_n)=a. It has smoothness1 and unique minimizer0.\nConsequently W_n(h)>=1/(1+2S), as also proved in b98bb807d514e80d.\nSubstitute the sum bound to get the second inequality of (1).\n\n## 5. Uniform asymptotics from the exact recurrence\n\nNo unverified decimal asymptotic constant is needed. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2)\nand P_j=1+S_circle(j). The legal balanced primitive construction gives\n\n P_(2^m-1)>=lambda^m,\n\nby induction from P_0=1 and the primitive mass join\nOmega(A,A)=lambda*A. The dominant mass join is\nG(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. Taking a primitive prefix of length2^m-1\nand an empty dominant suffix gives B_(2^m)>=2lambda^m.\nMoreover B_(j+1)>=G(1,B_j)>B_j, by the allowed empty-prefix split.\nFor2^m<=n<2^(m+1),\n\n B_n/n >= (lambda/2)^m ->infinity.\n\nTogether with (1), this proves the uniform lower-bound ratio (2).\nNo conclusion is asserted about W_n(h) having a matching upper bound.\n\n## 6. Scope, stress tests, and what remains\n\nThe calculation covers changes at every index, nonsymmetry, arbitrary\namplitudes for both children, and arbitrary bounded positive weights. Its\nobstruction is the common subtractive center and the small source step,\nnot a fixed terminal library, sparse exception set, fixed support, or Huber\ntightness of an upper certificate.\n\nThe proof identifies its load-bearing assumptions. An unbounded ratio\nw_max/w_min only gives O((w_max/w_min)*n) added mass. Groups without a\nbounded small step, a different center failing (3), or index-dependent\namplitudes inside one group leave this argument. Repeated separate\nnonnegative mutations also accumulate their own cost and are not silently\ncovered by the single-round estimate. Removing those assumptions requires\na new construction and a new full-model/certificate argument; the generic\nunknown SDP remains blocked as an asymptotic proof route.\n\nThis finding does not claim that all dense perturbations fail, or that the\nexact benchmark is a local/global minimum over unrestricted schedules.\nThe original all-horizon/all-schedule target remains open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_dense_common_offset_obstruction.md", "summary": "research/max_dense_discovery_summary.json", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["07a7985341f04ccf", "65ed20c4197a6ba9"], "status": "joint_candidate_unverified_no_routine_gate", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "scope": "named_family_only", "justification_type": "calculation"}, "glossary": {"d_i": "Base source-step gap h_i^0-1>0 in this dense-family draft.", "w_i": "An arbitrary weight in[1/2,3/2] on an index in a root child.", "A_I": "Weighted arithmetic mean of source-step gaps in root child I.", "H_I": "Weighted harmonic mean of source-step gaps in root child I.", "c_I": "Half the sum of A_I and H_I, the common subtractive center.", "V_I": "Total direction mass sum_(i in I)w_i(d_i-c_I).", "t_I": "Independent real amplitude of the redistribution in root child I.", "B_n": "Exact benchmark mass1+2S_bullet(n), as in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d."}}
{"id": "50c66ac5e173e9b1", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:45:02.237338+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C65 strengthened COMPLETE UNVERIFIED parabolic-family candidate: on actual enclosing-parameter H_m, m>=128, sup B_n P_par<=729/1600; uniformly over ALL horizon-dependent initial states, limsup sup B_n P_par <=(1+sqrt2)/(4e)<1/4. First-turn level j>=1 has the stronger limsup bound(3+2sqrt2)/(32e), and j0 tends0. Genuine multiple-turn threshold-crossing domain included. Only this proper family is closed by the draft; unrestricted original target is open.", "evidence": "# C65: an amortized threshold budget for the entire parabolic family\n\nStatus: COMPLETE PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit, trajectory experiment,\noptimization, installation, or external theorem is used. This is a proposed\nclosure of the specified parabolic mechanism, NOT of the unrestricted\nworst-case functional or the original all-schedule target. The accepted fact\nb98bb807d514e80d supplies the definition of the exact benchmark. Every other\nargument, including the earlier C60/C63/C64 derivations needed here, is proved\ninside this document; those drafts are not assumed as predecessor facts.\n\n## 1. Statement and scope\n\nFix an integer m>=128. Throughout the WHOLE word, including embedded words,\nkeep the following enclosing-horizon parameters fixed:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n=2^m-1, d_j=a*c^j, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nFor this document only, H_j denotes these physical words, not the conjugate\nquadratics with the same letter in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. All displayed\nscalar r,u,v,x,q and block quantities below are local parabolic variables.\n\nDefine one function on the Euclidean plane by\n\n    K_par={(t,s):s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},\n    F_par(X)=max_(g in K_par)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).\n\nFor arbitrary r_0!=0 and v_0>=0 start GD at\n\n    X_0=(r_0(1+v_0+r_0^2),-v_0/sqrt(2))\n\nand use the ACTUAL physical word H_m. Write the iterates as\n\n    X_i=(x_i,-v_i/sqrt(2)),\n    x_i=r_i(1+u_i), u_i=v_i+r_i^2, q_i=r_i^2.\n\nThe true initial squared distance to the minimizer set is\n\n    R_0^2=r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2=x_0^2.\n\nThe normalized payoff is\n\n    P_par=2F_par(X_n)/R_0^2.\n\nLet B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 be the exact Zhang--Jiang denominator defined by\naccepted b98bb807d514e80d, with its full finite recurrence reproduced below.\nThe proposed conclusion is the UNIFORM proper-family bound\n\n    sup_(r_0!=0,v_0>=0) B_(2^m-1) P_par <=729/1600<1/2.       (1)\n\nThe same joint proof, with the uniform comparison in Section9.1, gives\nthe stronger asymptotic candidate conclusion\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(r_0!=0,v_0>=0)\n          B_(2^m-1) P_par <=lambda/(4e)<1/4.              (1a)\n\nHere e is the base of the natural logarithm. On the subdomain whose first\nturn has level at least1, the right side improves to\n(3+2sqrt(2))/(32e). The first-turn-level0 subdomain has limit superior0.\nAll suprema allow initial states depending arbitrarily on the horizon.\n\nIn particular the previously open domain of two or more horizontal sign\nchanges WITH a crossing of the first turning threshold is included.\n\nThe proof gives the following stronger chronological assertions. Discard an\nabsorbing trajectory r_i=0, whose payoff is zero. If the first sign change\nhas level j>=1, put D=d_j. Then\n\n    u_i<(39/20)D<d_(j+1)                                   (2)\n\nat every sample after that turn, whenever j+1 is used as a comparison level.\nHere d_(j+1)=cD is a defined number even at the last physical level. If the\nfirst sign change has level0, then\n\n    u_i<3/4<d_1                                            (3)\n\nthroughout the trajectory. Thus a first turning threshold may be crossed,\nbut the NEXT turning threshold cannot be crossed. This assertion is proved\nby an inherited-state budget, not assumed as an ordering of turn levels.\n\nFor j>=1, set k=m-j, N=2^k,\n\n    L=D/c, C=D/(M_j+L), beta=c*C,\n    E(T)=(2T+3)^2/[8(T+1)^2(2T+1)].\n\nThe more detailed payoff bounds are\n\n    P_par<=E(S_m)                              if there is no turn;\n    P_par<=c^[-2(m-1)]                         if first-turn level j=0;\n    P_par<=E(S_j) beta^[2(N-1)] c^(-2k)         if j>=1.       (4)\n\nThese conclusions concern exactly F_par and the specified starts and words.\nThey do not upper-bound W_n(H_m), do not weaken the accepted finite C59\nadversary, and do not decide the original all-horizon/all-schedule problem.\n\n## 2. One global function, every pair, the true distance, and actual GD\n\nThe set K_par is nonempty, closed and convex. Completing the square shows\nthat the maximum defining F_par is attained uniquely at the Euclidean\nprojection p(X) onto K_par: the equivalent minimization is coercive and\nstrictly convex. The projection normal inequalities at X and Y imply\n\n    ||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>.\n\nHence p is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two defining maxima gives\n\n    0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>\n      <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.\n\nThe remainder divided by ||Y-X|| tends to zero, so F_par is differentiable\nwith gradient p. It is convex as a supremum of affine functions, and its\ngradient is 1-Lipschitz on the whole plane.\n\nFor r real and v>=0 define\n\n    u=v+r^2,\n    X(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v/sqrt(2)),\n    G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).\n\nOne has X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any (t,s) in K_par,\n\n    <X-G,(t,s)-G><=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0.\n\nThus G(r) is the actual projection and gradient, and substitution yields\n\n    F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                       (5)\n\nFor EVERY ordered pair of such states, with f_i=F_par(X_i), expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n        =-(u_j/2)(r_i-r_j)^2<=0.                           (6)\n\nThe construction therefore retains every interpolation pair, not only the\nconsecutive pairs used in the estimates below.\n\nThe minimizer set is exactly {(0,y):y<=0}. At such points all feasible\ninner products are nonpositive and the feasible gradient0 gives value0.\nA point of positive vertical coordinate has positive value by testing a\nsmall positive vertical gradient. A point of nonzero horizontal coordinate\nhas positive value by testing (t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with small t of the same\nhorizontal sign: the linear term dominates the higher powers of t. The\nclosest minimizer to X_0 is consequently (0,-v_0/sqrt(2)), which proves the\nstated true denominator R_0^2. At a later block entry the corresponding\nclosest distance is its actual horizontal magnitude; the global minimum\nvalue is still0 for this SAME function.\n\nFor a physical step h>=0, actual GD is exactly\n\n    v_plus=v+h*r^2,\n    r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus=r(1+v+r^2-h).                 (7)\n\nThe cubic's derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1, so it has one real root.\nEquation(7) produces exactly the coordinates of X-hG. In particular\n\n    x_plus=x-h*r,\n    u_plus-u=(h-1)q+q_plus.                                (8)\n\nEvery step of H_m is greater than1, so u is nondecreasing on the actual\ntrajectory. This monotonicity is NOT asserted for arbitrary smaller steps.\nA sign change occurs precisely when u<d_level; equality makes r_plus=0.\nOnce r is zero, all remaining r-values and objective values are zero.\n\nAnother useful pointwise bound, valid at every state, is\n\n    2F_par(X)<=x^2.                                        (9)\n\nIndeed, after substituting q=r^2 and u=v+q, the difference is\nq[u+u^2-q/2]>=0 since u>=q.\n\n## 3. One-sign blocks retain both coordinates\n\nSuppose a consecutive actual block has a fixed nonzero sign. By horizontal\nreflection it is enough to consider positive r. At a sign-preserving step,\nthe cubic in(7) evaluated at the OLD r exceeds its right side by h*r(1+r^2).\nSince that cubic is increasing, 0<r_plus<=r. Thus for a whole block of mass T,\nentry (r_A,v_A), and exit (r_B,v_B),\n\n    Delta_x=x_A-x_B=sum h_i r_i,\n    Delta_v=v_B-v_A=sum h_i r_i^2,\n    T r_B<=Delta_x<=T r_A,\n    r_B Delta_x<=Delta_v<=r_A Delta_x,\n    T r_B^2<=Delta_v<=T r_A^2.                              (10)\n\nFor either sign, if R_A=|x_A| and u_B=v_B+r_B^2, this proves\n\n    |r_B|(1+T+u_B)<=R_A.                                   (11)\n\nIf u_A>=L>=0, it also proves the following vertical charge, including for\nany initial part of that block:\n\n    Delta_v<=|r_A| R_A=R_A^2/(1+u_A)<=R_A^2/(1+L).          (12)\n\nNo old vertical state is discarded in (10)--(12).\n\nFor a complete one-sign trajectory of mass T>=1/2, put w=1+v_B and q=r_B^2.\nThen R_A>=sqrt(q)(T+w+q), and w>=1+Tq, using v_A>=0. Thus its payoff with\nthe true entry distance is at most\n\n    (w+(3/2)q)/(T+w+q)^2.\n\nWith t=w+q>=1, one has q<=(t-1)/(T+1), so this is at most\n\n    [(2T+3)t-1]/[2(T+1)(T+t)^2].\n\nThe derivative has the sign of (2T+3)(T-t)+2. Its maximum on t>=1 is at\nt=T+2/(2T+3), which belongs to that interval for T>=1/2. Substitution gives\nE(T) as in(4). Two useful consequences are\n\n    E(T)<=3/(8T)                  for T>=1/2;\n    E(T)<9/(32T)                  for T>=8.                 (13)\n\nFor the first, bound w+(3/2)q by (3/2)(w+q) and use\nt/(T+t)^2<=1/(4T); the displayed E(T) inherits this bound where it applies.\nFor the second,\nT E(T)<[2+1/(T+1)]^2/16<=361/1296<9/32.\n\n## 4. Physical masses, binary accounting, and fixed constants\n\nThe recursive mass identity is\n\n    M_(j+1)=2M_j+d_j,\n    M_j=A_mass*c^j+(1-A_mass)*2^j,\n    A_mass=a/(c-2).                                        (14)\n\nHere 0<a<c-2 and hence 0<A_mass<1. To check the nontrivial strict inequality,\nwrite epsilon=1/m and use lambda^2=2lambda+1:\n\n    c(c-2)-(1-epsilon)\n      =epsilon(2lambda+3)+lambda^2 epsilon^2>0.\n\nConsequently\n\n    d_j/M_j<c-2.                                          (15)\n\nFor m>=128 the following convenient rational bounds hold:\n\n    12/5<c<73/30<5/2,\n    2/5<a<5/12,\n    c-2<13/30,\n    d_j>=d_1=1-1/m>=127/128                for j>=1.        (16)\n\nFor the upper bound on c, sqrt(2)<99/70 gives\nc<(169/70)(129/128)=21801/8960<73/30. The lower bound c>12/5 follows from\nsqrt(2)>7/5. Also a<(sqrt(2)-1)<5/12, and\na>(127/128)(30/73)=3810/9344>2/5.\n\nFix j>=1, D=d_j, L=D/c, M=M_j, delta=D/M, C=D/(M+L), beta=cC. Then\n\n    beta=c*delta/(1+delta/c)\n         <c^2(c-2)/(2c-2)\n         <69277/77400<9/10,\n    C<3/8.                                                (17)\n\nThe function c^2(c-2)/(2c-2) is increasing for c>2, because both c^2 and\n(c-2)/(2(c-1)) are positive and increasing. Substitute c=73/30 for the\nsecond inequality. The last bound uses c>12/5 and beta<9/10.\n\nLet p_2(t) be the number of1s in the binary expansion of a nonnegative\ninteger t, and let nu_2(t) be the exponent of2 in a positive integer t.\nCounting multiples of powers of2 gives\n\n    sum_(k=1)^t nu_2(k)=t-p_2(t).                           (18)\n\nFor completeness, write t=sum_s e_s 2^s with e_s in{0,1}. Then\nsum_(b>=1)floor(t/2^b)=sum_s e_s(2^s-1)=t-p_2(t).\n\nH_m consists of N=2^(m-j) chronological H_j blocks, indexed\nell=0,...,N-1. Block ell starts at sample ell*2^j, with inherited state\n(r_(ell*2^j),v_(ell*2^j)); its bridge input is sample (ell+1)*2^j-1 and\nits bridge output is sample (ell+1)*2^j. In particular\nR_ell=|x_(ell*2^j)|. The bridge after block ell has excess\n\n    D*c^[nu_2(ell+1)].                                    (19)\n\nThis follows by induction from H_(j+k+1)=(H_(j+k),1+d_(j+k),H_(j+k)).\nThe midpoint bridge has valuation k; the two child bridge lists have\ntheir earlier valuations unchanged. No physical step is aggregated in\nthe actual dynamics by writing this partition.\n\nWe shall need one explicit convergent series. Put rho=81/100. Then for c<5/2,\n\n    sum_(ell>=1) rho^(ell-1)c^[nu_2(ell+1)]<23.             (20)\n\nSeparate k=ell+1 by its binary valuation. The s=0 contribution is\nrho/(1-rho^2). For s>=1 the contribution is\n\n    t_s=c^s rho^(2^s-2)/(1-rho^(2^(s+1))).\n\nUse rho<5/6 and rho^2<2/3. The contributions for s=0,1,2,3,4 are respectively\nstrictly less than 3,5,6,5,3. Here the latter four can be bounded by\n\n    (5/2)/(1-(2/3)^2)<5,\n    (25/4)(2/3)/(1-(2/3)^4)<6,\n    (125/8)(2/3)^3/(1-(2/3)^8)<5,\n    (625/16)(2/3)^7/(1-(2/3)^16)<3.\n\nEach inequality follows by multiplying its positive integer denominators.\nFor the tail, (2/3)^15<1/400 and (2/3)^16<1/600 show t_5<1/3. For s>=5,\n\n    t_(s+1)/t_s\n      =c rho^(2^s)/(1+rho^(2^(s+1)))<1/240.\n\nThus the tail starting at s=5 is less than (1/3)/(1-1/240)<1. Summing the\nsix bounds gives(20). This is a scalar geometric-series proof, not a\ntrajectory evaluation or numerical search.\n\n## 5. First-turn structure and the actual crossing times\n\nIf a first turn occurs at step i, every earlier step preserved its sign and\nsatisfied d_level(t)<u_t. If the current step were no larger than some\nearlier step, monotonicity u_i>=u_t would forbid the turn. Thus the first\nturn is a NEW RECORD level j. Its preceding word is exactly the first H_j.\n\nFor j>=1, the preceding first occurrence of level j-1 did not turn or\nannihilate, so the first-turn input has\n\n    L=d_(j-1)<=u<D=d_j.                                   (21)\n\nLet q=r^2 at this input. The one-sign H_j prefix and(10) give\nv>=S_j q, and therefore\n\n    q<=u/M_j.                                            (22)\n\nLet R_1 be the actual horizontal magnitude immediately after that turn,\nthe entry to the next H_j block. From the exact two-coordinate update,\n\n    v_after=u+Dq,\n    R_1^2=q(D-u)^2.                                      (23)\n\nThese formulas keep the initial vertical state: v_after is its actual\naccumulated value, not an increment from a newly chosen origin.\n\nDefine the first crossing of a normal threshold D after the first turn by\n\n    tau(D)=min{t in{2^j,...,n}: u_t>=D},\n\nwith tau(D)=infinity when the set is empty. Define a putative escape from\nthe larger band by\n\n    sigma(2D)=min{t in{2^j,...,n}: u_t>2D}.\n\nThe first turning input is sample2^j-1 and its output is sample2^j;\nthe minimum of an empty set is infinity in both definitions.\n\nAll block entries and crossing states below are samples of the SAME\ntrajectory. A first escape may be inside a block or the output of a bridge;\nneither location is rounded into a fresh independent H_j instance.\n\n## 6. Amortized crossing budget when j>=1\n\nAfter the first turn u>=L. Hence every H_j block thereafter has one sign:\nits steps have excess at most L, and equality would cause absorption,\nalready excluded. Assume for contradiction that sigma(2D) is finite.\nUntil its input all bridge inputs have u<=2D.\n\nWrite R_ell for the actual horizontal magnitude entering block ell.\nFor a completed block ell>=1 and its next bridge, let w be the normal\nstate at that bridge input and s=nu_2(ell+1). Formula(11) and actual GD give\n\n    R_(ell+1)/R_ell<=|D c^s-w|/(M+w)<=C c^s.              (24)\n\nIf D c^s>=w, use w>=L and drop the subtraction in the numerator. If\nD c^s<w<=2D, then 0<w-D c^s<=D, so the ratio is at most C<=C c^s.\nThus(24) continues to hold after the FIRST threshold D has been crossed.\nIt also bounds the OUTPUT of the putative escape step when that step is\na bridge, because only its input normal state was used.\n\nFor each block entry reached by that time, (18) gives\n\n    R_ell<=R_1 C^(ell-1)c^[ell-p_2(ell)]\n           =R_1 beta^(ell-1)c^[1-p_2(ell)]\n           <=R_1 beta^(ell-1), ell>=1.                   (25)\n\nWithin a one-sign block the horizontal magnitude only decreases. The same\nbound therefore controls a partial final block if the first escape occurs\nthere. In particular the squared gradient at the escape output is at most\n\n    q_escape<=R_1^2/(1+L)^2.                             (26)\n\nBy(12), all complete or partial H_j blocks after the first turn contribute\nat most R_1^2/[(1+L)(1-rho)] to the vertical increment. At a bridge after\nblock ell, (11) bounds its squared input gradient by\nR_ell^2/(M+L)^2; its actual vertical increment is exactly\n(1+D c^s) times that quantity. Equations(20),(25) therefore show that,\nup to and INCLUDING the putative first escape output,\n\n    u_escape<=u+Dq+(K/D)R_1^2,                           (27)\n\nwhere one may use\n\n    K = D/[(1+L)(1-rho)]\n        +C^2[(1/D)/(1-rho)+23]\n        +D/(1+L)^2\n      <18.                                               (28)\n\nThere is no uncharged last fragment: (12) covers a partial block, the\nbridge sum covers an escape bridge, and(26) supplies the current q in\nu=v+q. If the first escape were the first turn's own output, the unused\nnonnegative future-charge allowances in(27) still bound it.\n\nHere are explicit constants for(28). Since L=D/c,\nD/(1+L)<c<5/2 and D/(1+L)^2<=c/4<5/8. Also\n(1/D)/(1-rho)<=12800/2413<11/2, and C<3/8. Therefore\n\n    K<250/19+(9/64)(11/2+23)+5/8\n      <53/4+513/128+5/8\n      =2289/128<18.\n\nPut z=u/D in[0,1] and delta=D/M. From(22),(23),(27),\n\n    u_escape/D<=z+q+Kq(1-z)^2\n                 <=(1+delta)z+K delta z(1-z)^2.\n\nSince z(1-z)<=1/4,\n\n    u_escape/D\n      <=(1+delta)z+(K delta/4)(1-z)\n      <=max{1+delta,K delta/4}<39/20<2.                  (29)\n\nWe used delta<13/30, K<18, and 1+13/30<39/20. This contradicts the definition\nof sigma(2D). Thus no escape exists. Applying the same finite charges at\nany actual sample proves(2), not just the absence of escape.\n\nNow(24) holds through the entire word. It also holds at the FIRST bridge:\nblock0 was one-sign by the definition of first turn, and its bridge input\nhas u>=L. Multiplying from block0 instead of block1 gives\n\n    R_(N-1)/R_0<=C^(N-1)c^[N-1-k]\n                 =beta^(N-1)c^(-k), k=m-j.               (30)\n\nThe final H_j is one-sign, has its inherited vertical state, and has true\nentry distance R_(N-1). The function's global final value is the same\nF_par(X_n). Apply Section3 to that final block and then(30), obtaining the\nj>=1 line of(4) with the ORIGINAL denominator R_0^2.\n\n## 7. First turn at level0: small post-turn amplitude and exact alignment\n\nHere the first turn is the very first step, with 0<=u_0<a. Put\n\n    A=x_1^2=q_0(a-u_0)^2,\n    b=ac=1-1/m, D=d_1=b.\n\nSince q_0<=u_0, maximizing t(a-t)^2 on[0,a] gives\n\n    A<=4a^3/27<1/90,\n    a+a^2<85/144<3/5,\n    D>=127/128.                                         (31)\n\nThe maximum is at t=a/3 (the endpoints give0); its derivative is\n(a-t)(a-3t). The strict bound1/90 follows from a<5/12 and\n4(5/12)^3/27=125/11664<1/90.\n\nIf u never reaches a, every step turns. Whenever all inputs through time t-1\nhave u<a, the exact horizontal update and(18) give\n\n    |x_t|<=R_0 b^t c^[-p_2(t)],\n    |x_t|<=sqrt(A) b^(t-1)c^[1-p_2(t)]   for t>=1.        (32)\n\nThe same inequalities hold at the OUTPUT of the first crossing, since its\ninput still had u<a. If there is no crossing, (9),(32) at t=n already give\nP_par<=b^(2n)c^(-2m), which is stronger than(4).\n\nOtherwise let tau=min{t>=1:u_t>=a}. We next bound the actual crossing state.\nIf tau=1, v_tau=u_0+a q_0<=a+a^2. If tau>1, its input index i=tau-1 is\npositive. If its level ell is0, (32) gives q_i<=A and\nh_i q_i<2A. If ell>=1, the integer i has at least ell trailing binary1s,\nso p_2(i)>=ell. Consequently\n\n    h_i q_i<=A[c^(2-2ell)+a c^(2-ell)]<=A(1+ac)<2A.\n\nSince v_i<=u_i<a, in this case v_tau<a+2A<=a+a^2; the latter uses\n2A<=8a^3/27<a^2. In every case(32) also gives |x_tau|^2<=A. Therefore\n\n    v_tau<=a+a^2,\n    u_tau<=a+a^2+A<3/5+1/90<D.                            (33)\n\nIf tau=n, all inputs in the word were still below a, and the first line\nof(32) again proves the stronger all-turn product bound. Suppose tau<n.\n\nIf tau is even, it is already the entry to an actual H_1 block. If tau is\nodd, retain the NEXT ACTUAL step, at index tau. It has level\nell=nu_2(tau+1)>=1. Its input satisfies u_tau<D<=d_ell by(33), so it turns\nand its horizontal factor is at most d_ell. Both estimates in(32) therefore\ncontinue to hold at time tau+1. The same trailing-ones calculation bounds\nthis one alignment bridge's vertical increment by2A.\n\nWe have now reached an even time2K, with K>=1, which is an actual entry to\nH_1; no state was reset. Its inherited state satisfies\n\n    u_(2K)>=a,\n    v_(2K)<=a+a^2+2A,\n    |x_(2K)|<=sqrt(A)b^(2K-1)c^[1-p_2(K)],\n    |x_(2K)|<=R_0 b^(2K)c^[-p_2(K)],\n    u_(2K)<=a+a^2+3A<D.                                  (34)\n\nThe identity p_2(2K)=p_2(K) explains the two retained binary weights.\n\nFrom here all H_1 low steps are one-sign, because u>=a. Bootstrap up to a\nputative first output with u>D. At a coarse bridge its input u<=D, so all\nsuch bridges turn and(24) applies with j=1, L=a, M_1=2+a and\n\n    C=D/(2+2a), beta=cC<9/10.\n\nLet R_ell=|x_(2ell)| for actual H_1 block entries ell>=K. The exact product\nover the inherited suffix, not a restarted word, gives\n\n    R_ell<=R_K beta^(ell-K)c^[p_2(K)-p_2(ell)]\n           <=sqrt(A) beta^(ell-K)c^[1-p_2(ell)].           (35)\n\nFor ell>=1 and s=nu_2(ell+1) one has\n\n    c^s c^[2-2p_2(ell)]<=c.                              (36)\n\nIf s=0, p_2(ell)>=1 gives a bound1. If s>=1, the last s binary digits of\nell are1, so p_2(ell)>=s and the expression is at most c^(2-s)<=c.\nThis identity is the missing chronological charge for a crossing close\nto a high-level bridge. Dropping the binary weight in(34) would lose it.\n\nBy(12),(35), the vertical cost of all remaining complete or partial H_1\nblocks is at most A/(1-rho). By(11),(35),(36), their bridges contribute at\nmost\n\n    A(1+D c)/[(2+2a)^2(1-rho)]< (7/8)A/(1-rho),\n\nbecause D<1, c<5/2 and 2+2a>=2. The squared gradient at any current sample\nis at most A, including the output of the proposed first escape; (35)\napplies to an escape bridge output using only its input normal state.\nThus the remaining cost to v+q is less than\n\n    [1+(1+7/8)/(1-rho)]A=(413/38)A<11A.\n\nTogether with(34), this yields\n\n    u_escape<a+a^2+13A<3/5+13/90=67/90<3/4<D.             (37)\n\nThis contradicts the proposed first crossing of D. The same bound applies\nat every subsequent sample; (33),(34) and u<a before tau cover all earlier\nones. This proves(3).\n\nThere are 2^(m-1) actual H_1 blocks, and the last has index\nJ=2^(m-1)-1 with p_2(J)=m-1. Combining the first inequality in(35) with\nthe second original-radius bound in(34) gives\n\n    R_J/R_0<=b^(2K) beta^(J-K)c^[-(m-1)]\n             <=c^[-(m-1)].                              (38)\n\nThe final H_1 step is one-sign, so |x_n|<=R_J. Equations(9),(38) give the\nj=0 payoff in(4). The previously separated cases of no crossing and a\ncrossing at the final sample have even smaller bounds. This accounts for\nevery possible location of the first threshold crossing, including inside\na partial block.\n\n## 8. What the threshold result says about the turns\n\nThis paragraph is a consequence of the budget, not an assumption used to\nobtain it. For a first turn of level j, all lower levels have stopped\nturning already: for j>=1 use u>=d_(j-1), and for j=0 there are no lower\nlevels. By(2),(3), u never reaches d_(j+1). Thus every level strictly\ngreater than j turns at each of its actual occurrences. Level j turns\nexactly at the occurrences whose INPUT index is less than tau(d_j), and\nnever again thereafter, apart from the separately excluded equality\nabsorption case. Monotonicity proves this chronological-prefix assertion.\n\nIn the N=2^(m-j) block decomposition there are N/2 occurrences of level j\nand N/2-1 bridges at higher levels. If ell_j is the number of level-j turns,\nthen the exact total number of turns is\n\n    N/2-1+ell_j, with 1<=ell_j<=N/2.                       (39)\n\nFor no threshold crossing ell_j=N/2. With a crossing it is the number of\nactual level-j occurrences before that crossing, not a separately chosen\nrestart count. The normal thresholds crossed after the first turn consist\nof at most the single level d_j. All earlier displacement and vertical\nhistory was charged in(23),(27) or(32)--(37).\n\n## 9. Exact benchmark comparison, with no asymptotic B_n assumption\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d, the exact recursions can be written with\nC_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 as\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nHere C_n is a benchmark sequence, different from the block constant C in\nSections4--7. We prove directly the needed finite comparator\n\n    B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).                              (40)\n\nSet alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda) and\nLlin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Both coefficients in Llin exceed1, so Llin>=x+y.\nDirect expansion using lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives\n\n    alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]\n      =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.\n\nThus Llin>=Omega(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy. At\nz=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial having larger root G(x,lambda y) is\n\n    z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2\n      =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.\n\nAlso z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it lies to the right of that\nquadratic's vertex. Therefore G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y). Induction\nin the exact finite recurrences, using monotonicity of G in its second\nargument, proves B_n<=lambda C_n.\n\nPut eta=log_2(lambda)>1, Dpow(t)=(1+t)^eta-1 and ell(z)=log Dpow(exp z).\nFor t=exp z, differentiation yields\n\n    ell''(z)=eta*t*(1+t)^(eta-2)\n      *[(1+t)^eta-1-eta*t]/Dpow(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket vanishes at0 and has positive derivative for positive t.\nConvexity therefore gives Dpow(t)Dpow(1/t)>=Dpow(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 put\nZ=(x^(1/eta)+y^(1/eta))^eta. Substituting t=(y/x)^(1/eta) gives\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy, hence Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y by the increasing\nderivative of the positive power. It follows that Omega(x,y)<=Z; zero\narguments follow directly. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^eta. Together with\nB_n<=lambda C_n this proves(40).\n\nIt remains to compare(4) with(40). Equations(14),(16) imply\nA_mass>4/5 and hence, for j>=1,\n\n    S_j>(4/5)c^j-1>(23/60)c^j>c^j/3.                     (41)\n\nThe middle step uses c^j>12/5. For a first turn j>=1, k=m-j>=1,\n(13),(17),(40),(41) give\n\n    B_n P_par\n      <(9/8)(lambda/c)^m [lambda*c^(-k)]\n           beta^[2(2^k-1)]\n      <(9/8)(1/2)(81/100)=729/1600.                       (42)\n\nHere (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)<=1/2 by the binomial theorem,\nlambda c^(-k)<1, and beta<9/10.\n\nFor j=0, (4),(40) give\n\n    B_n P_par<=lambda^(m+1)c^[-2(m-1)]\n                <=lambda^(3-m)<1/8                      (43)\n\nfor m>=128. For no turns, c^m>20 gives S_m>(3/4)c^m and S_m>8, so(13)\nand(40) yield\n\n    B_n P_par<(3/8)lambda(lambda/c)^m<=3lambda/16\n                <729/1600.                             (44)\n\nFor the last strict comparison, lambda<17/7<243/100, since sqrt(2)<10/7;\nthen 3lambda/16<729/1600. Absorbing trajectories have payoff0. These cases\nexhaust all starts and establish(1) as a complete proof draft.\n\n### 9.1. Uniform asymptotic sharpening, including varying first-turn levels\n\nNo limit for the EXACT benchmark B_n is assumed. We continue to use only\nthe finite comparator (40). Write A_m=a_m/(c_m-2), explicitly showing\nthe enclosing-horizon dependence already fixed earlier. The physical\ndefinitions and (14) give\n\n    c_m->lambda, a_m->1/lambda=lambda-2, A_m->1,\n    (lambda/c_m)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)->1/e,\n    S_j/c_m^j=A_m+(1-A_m)(2/c_m)^j-c_m^(-j).              (45)\n\nFor all j>=m/2, the final expression tends to1 UNIFORMLY: the last two\nterms have absolute values bounded by\n|1-A_m|(5/6)^(m/2)+(5/12)^(m/2), since c_m>12/5.\nAlso S_j tends to infinity uniformly on that range. The explicit rational\nformula E(T) has T E(T)->1/4, so\n\n    sup_(m/2<=j<=m) |c_m^j E(S_j)-1/4| ->0.              (46)\n\nFor no turns, (4),(40),(45),(46) yield\n\n    limsup B_n P_par <=lambda/(4e).                      (47)\n\nFor first turn j=0, (43) gives a uniformly vanishing bound. For j>=1,\nput k=m-j as before. The detailed bound (4) and comparator (40) give\n\n    B_n P_par\n      <= (lambda/c_m)^m [c_m^j E(S_j)]\n                         lambda c_m^(-k) beta_j^[2(2^k-1)]. (48)\n\nUniformly in j, (17) gives\n\n    beta_j<bar_beta_m=c_m^2(c_m-2)/(2c_m-2)<9/10,\n    bar_beta_m ->beta_star=lambda/(2sqrt(2)).              (49)\n\nThe limit in (49) uses lambda^2(lambda-2)=lambda. First consider\nj>=m/2. Then k>=1; because c_m>1 and bar_beta_m<1, the last two factors\nin (48) are at most (lambda/c_m)*bar_beta_m^2. Equation (46) therefore\nproves a uniform limit-superior bound beta_star^2/(4e) on this range.\n\nFor 1<=j<m/2, k>m/2. Equations (13),(41) give\nc_m^j E(S_j)<9/8. In (48), the other non-dyadic factors are bounded by\nlambda, whereas\n\n    beta_j^[2(2^k-1)]\n       <=(9/10)^[2(2^(m/2)-1)] ->0.\n\nThus the smaller-j range is uniformly negligible as well. This explicit\nsplit justifies the supremum over ALL horizon-dependent first-turn\nlevels; a fixed-j calculation alone would not suffice. We have proved\nthe sharper turned-domain candidate bound\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(first-turn level j>=1)\n            B_n P_par <=beta_star^2/(4e)\n                       =(3+2sqrt(2))/(32e).              (50)\n\nTaking the maximum of (47),(50), the vanishing j=0 bound, and the zero\nabsorbing payoff proves (1a). Indeed beta_star^2=lambda^2/8<lambda,\nand e>1+1+1/2=5/2>lambda, so lambda/(4e)<1/4. The no-turn and\nturning estimates all use the ORIGINAL initial distance.\n\n## 10. Audit and unresolved original task\n\nEvery threshold argument used the exact h_i>1 recursion. The physical c,a\nwere kept fixed through all embedded H_j. Every local closest-distance\nestimate was multiplied by an explicit ratio to R_0, either(30) or(38).\nThe j=0 crossing was aligned by retaining its next physical bridge and a\nbinary-popcount weight; no right child was reset or independently optimized.\nThe finite benchmark comparison came from its recurrence, not a conjectured\nlimit for B_n. Equations(6),(7) supply global all-pair legality independently\nof the estimates.\n\nThe proof is UNVERIFIED and has not entered the fact graph. It is a concrete\ncandidate for separate selection if the main agent allocates a verifier gate.\nEven acceptance would close only this fixed parabolic mechanism on H_m for\nm>=128.
