No allowance renews. This document is\nan unverified proof candidate, and the project's original target remains\nunresolved.\n\n## 8. (33)\n\nThe residuals are y_0=(m-1)U+Klen V, y_1=zU+Klen V,\ny_2=(Klen-1)V, y_3=0. The three GD updates have steps a,b,a.\nHere Klen>1 and 0<rho<1. At samples 0 and 1 the normals against V are\n-a and 0, and the normals against 0 are -(m-1),-z. Normals against rho V\nare no larger than those against V because all the V/residual pairings\nare nonnegative. At sample 2 the normals against U and 0 equal\n-(Klen-1)eta, and the normal against rho V equals\n-(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta. At sample 3 all residuals vanish. Normals against\neach sample's own gradient vanish. This checks every distinct vertex of\nconv(0,U,V,rho V). Section 2 supplies the actual common function, values,\nand TRUE distance Rnew, since x_0=rho V+(a+b)U+aV is a nonnegative\ncombination of the actual gradients.\n\nThis seed still has positive defect, with\n\n    kappa_new=A^2 rho^2 eta/Rnew^2 >1+1/2000.            (34)\n\nIt also has an explicit legal first transverse join and an optimal signed\ninitial/gradient minimum at that join. Take r_0=theta A and use transverse\nbases U on the left and\n(Klen sqrt(eta)/Rnew, -m/Rnew) on the right in the orthonormal coordinates\n(U,V/sqrt(eta)). The latter vector is perpendicular to x_0/Rnew and has\nnorm one. Let the scalar cross map be c in [0,1]. The scaled physical\ncross matrix on the pair (U,V) is exactly\n\n    r_0 rho eta/Rnew^2 * [[Klen*c, -m*c],[-m,-z]].       (35)\n\nThis follows directly from (9) with w=rho V and\nalpha=r_0 rho sqrt(eta)/Rnew. It is realized by (10), with scalar\nS_C=sqrt(1-c^2), so no PSD correction is implicit.\n\nAll cross gradient/residual inner products in BOTH directions are\nnonpositive. To check the only possibly positive terms, remove the\npositive prefactor in (35). Pair a right U with the three nonzero left\nresiduals; the results are\n\n    Klen[(m-1)c-m], Klen[zc-m], -m(Klen-1),\n\nwhich are nonpositive because c<=1 and m-z=a+1>0. Pair a left U with\nthe three nonzero right residuals; the results are\n\n    -Klen*c, -Klen*(a+1)*c, -m*c*(Klen-1),\n\nwhich are nonpositive because c>=0. Every remaining pair uses the V row\nor column of (35), whose entries are nonpositive, with nonnegative\nresidual coefficients; rho V contributes an additional positive factor.\nThe fourth residuals are zero. Subtracting the nonnegative energies in\n(13) checks every cross Q. Thus this is an actual legal seven-step join\nfor EVERY c in [0,1], with true distance Rnew and ratio kappa_new^2.\n\nThe retained signed correlations of its right U,V gradients are\n\n    c_U=r_0 rho*m*z*(c-1)/Rnew^4,\n    c_V=-r_0 rho*eta*(m^2 c+Klen*z)/Rnew^4.\n\nChoose\n\n    c_opt=(a+1)Klen/[m(m+Klen)] in (0,1).               (36)\n\nThen c_U=c_V=-r_0 k_new/(m+Klen), where k_new=rho*z/Rnew^2.\nThe right rho V gradient has correlation rho*c_V and is less negative;\nthe left gradients have positive initial correlations. Equation\nm*g_right,U+Klen*g_right,V=-r_0 w proves that their weighted average\nhas this value for ANY transverse coupling, so (36) maximizes the\nminimum signed initial/gradient correlation over every allowed coupling\nat the first join. It is an exact argument, not a numerical optimizer.\n\nThe last queried gradient in (33) is w/rho, rather than w. Thus the\nendpoint-facing row threshold (30) becomes c_R,j>=-1/(rho*r). The\npositive weighted combination inherited from these two first-join\ngradients would, at the fourth balanced bridge r_3=theta lambda^3 A,\nhave normalized residual Charge_new-1, where\n\n    Charge_new=r_3 rho*r_0 k_new/(m+Klen)<1-1/4000.     (37)\n\nConsequently that inherited two-row certificate is strictly NEGATIVE\nfor this changed actual seed; it cannot exclude the 63-step parent.\nNeither existence of legal intervening 15-,31-step general-coupling\nchildren nor feasibility of its 63-step parent is asserted here.\n\nFor a mechanically checkable sign audit of (34),(37), their exact\nexpressions in Q(sqrt(2)) are\n\n    kappa_new-1 =\n      [-19019047380837966731+13474265781546157318*s]\n          /40842989603870102500,\n\n    1-Charge_new =\n      [341554841617983701-241357907739927883*s]\n          /603687956995287500.\n\nFor the first margin, write the displayed positive integers in its\nnumerator and denominator as P1,Q1,D1, so the numerator is -P1+Q1*s.\nThen\n\n    2(2000 Q1)^2-(2000 P1+D1)^2\n       =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0.\n\nBoth compared quantities are positive, so this proves (34). For the\nsecond margin write its numerator as P2-Q2*s and its denominator as D2.\nThe quantity 4000 P2-D2 is positive, and\n\n    (4000 P2-D2)^2-2(4000 Q2)^2\n       =789713290761361657120990345679724250000>0.\n\nThis proves (37). Full seed existence remains open.", "evidence": "# Full four-step seed: a compact domain from legal cut functions\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, not verifier-accepted. This round has no\nnumerical or fact_submit allowance. No unverified finding is used as a premise.\nThe accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the interpolation convention Q<=0;\nthe particular functions and all algebra below are derived explicitly.\n\n## 1. Interface and energy\n\nLet h=(h_0,h_1,h_2,h_3)>=0, s=sum h_i, C=1+s>=193/25.\nIndices i,j range over0,...,4. Set p_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, so p_0=1,p_4=C.\nLet e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T. Define T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,T_it=0 for t>i,\nLambda_ii=0,Lambda_ij>=0,L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\nomega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,2s)^T, and\nD=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s(s+2)).\nThe full interface is\n\n    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                 (1)\n\nNo multiplier support, rank, remainder, symmetry, or step order is fixed.\nFor arbitrary formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n      -s(s+1)||g_4||^2.\n\nPut delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 and y_i=x_i-g_i=x_0-(Tg)_i.\nThen Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>, while\nV=-omega^T delta+sum_i D_ii||g_i||^2. Value flow in(1) therefore gives\n\n    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>. (2)\n\nIn particular every feasible full certificate implies V>=0 on every legal\nconvex differentiable1-smooth trajectory with a minimizer. This direction\nrequires neither certificate necessity nor any duality assertion.\n\n## 2. Legal cut witnesses at every position\n\nFix j<4, put b=h_j,P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i, and first assume b>1.\nSet a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0. Define the scalar function\n\n    f(x)=x-1/2                     if x>=1,\n         x^2/2                     if -a<=x<=1,\n         -a*x-a^2/2                if x<=-a.\n\nIts derivative is clamp(x,[-a,1]), which is continuous, nondecreasing,\nand1-Lipschitz. The function is convex1-smooth and has a minimizer at0.\nStart at x_0=P+1. Before and at j all gradients are1, and x_j=1.\nAfter step j, x_(j+1)=1-b=-a(R+1). All subsequent gradients equal-a,\nsince the remaining nonnegative steps move monotonically to x_4=-a.\nEvery sampled value obeys f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and x_4=g_4.\n\nFor any vector-valued data obeying these two identities, GD telescoping gives\n\n    2sum_(i<4)h_i f_i+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n      =||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2,\n    V=||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.                         (3)\n\nApplied to this scalar function, nonnegative V implies\n\n    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+P)(1+R), for every j=0,...,3.      (4)\n\nIf h_j<=1 the same inequality is automatic. Thus(4) holds on every\nzero-step boundary too. Failure of any inequality is a specific legal\nnegative-V function, hence rules out the full interface(1).\n\n## 3. Prefix bounds and compact capacity\n\nFor fixed C>1 define the increasing rational map\n\n    R_C(z)=1+2Cz/(C+z), z>=0,\n    r_k=R_C iterated k times at1, k=0,1,2,3.\n\nRearrangement of(4) gives p_(i+1)<=R_C(p_i). Conversely this inequality\nis equivalent to(4) on the nonnegative schedule domain. Induction yields\np_i<=r_i. The cut constraints are invariant under reversing the four\nsteps, regardless of whether the original certificate admits reversal.\nApplying the same induction to the reversed cut inequalities gives\n\n    p_i >= C+1-r_(4-i).                               (5)\n\nOnly the cases i=1,2,3 are needed; r_0=1. Explicitly,\n\n    r_1=(3C+1)/(C+1),\n    r_2=(7C^2+6C+1)/(C^2+4C+1),\n    r_3=(15C^3+23C^2+9C+1)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1).          (6)\n\nTaking i=2 in(5) and p_2<=r_2 gives C+1<=2r_2, hence\n\n    C^3-9C^2-7C-1<=0.                                (7)\n\nThis polynomial is strictly increasing for C>=7: its derivative\n3C^2-18C-7 is positive there and increasing. At C=39/4 its value is\n131/64>0. Since C>=193/25>7, we conclude\n\n    193/25 <= C < 39/4.                               (8)\n\nIn particular this argument covers all larger candidate sums; it did not\nreplace the target by the slice sum h=168/25.\n\n## 4. All four steps are uniformly positive and bounded\n\nThe first and last cut inequalities give\n\n    h_0,h_3 <= 2C/(C+1) < 78/43.                      (9)\n\nFor the lower bounds, subtract the upper prefix bound from the lower\nbound for the next prefix in(5). This gives\n\n    h_0,h_3 >= C-r_3 = N(C)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1),\n    h_1,h_2 >= C+1-r_1-r_2 = N(C)/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)), (10)\n    N(C)=C^4-4C^3-16C^2-8C-1.\n\nThe endpoint lower bound exceeds1/2 exactly when\n\n    A(C)=2C^4-9C^3-43C^2-23C-3>0.\n\nThe middle lower bound exceeds4/5 exactly when\n\n    B(C)=5C^4-24C^3-100C^2-60C-9>0.\n\nAt C=77/10 these polynomials respectively equal1922412/10000 and\n2197285/10000. They are strictly increasing for C>=7: their derivatives\nare bounded below respectively by29C^2-86C-23 and68C^2-200C-60,\nboth positive and increasing on that interval. Since193/25>77/10,\n\n    h_0,h_3>1/2, h_1,h_2>4/5.                         (11)\n\nFor completeness, the middle upper bound uses the same cuts, without\nassuming either middle step exceeds1. Write\n\n    G_C(z)=R_C(z)-z=1+z(C-z)/(C+z).\n\nFor1<=z<=r_1<3 its derivative is\n(C^2-2Cz-z^2)/(C+z)^2>0: its numerator is at least C^2-6C-9,\nwhich is positive for C>=15/2. Thus h_1<=G_C(p_1)<=G_C(r_1), and\nthe reversed cut constraints give the same bound for h_2. Calculation yields\n\n    G_C(r_1)=4C^3/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)).                  (12)\n\nThe right side is strictly increasing in C>0: its logarithmic derivative\nhas positive numerator5C^2+10C+3 after multiplication by positive\ndenominators. At39/4 its value is237276/92923<13/5, with the difference\n21619/464615. Consequently\n\n    1/2<h_0,h_3<78/43, 4/5<h_1,h_2<13/5.             (13)\n\nThis excludes every zero-step boundary for this threshold by actual\nwitnesses. It does not assert h_i>1, ordering, or reversal of certificates.\n\n## 5. Full multiplier compactness from the common-gradient kernel\n\nThis step bounds all20 original multipliers, including every cycle.\nThe vector p=Te has entries p_i. Since\n\n    sum_i D_ii=-s^2-sum_(i<4)h_i^2,\n    omega^T p=s^2+sum_(i<4)h_i^2,\n\nequations(1) give e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0.\nFor clarity this implication follows by inserting e+t v into the quadratic\nform: a nonzero linear coefficient would violate nonnegativity for one\nsign of sufficiently small t. Calculation of each component gives\n\n    2De+T^T omega=-diag(p)omega,\n    Lp=diag(p)omega.                                  (14)\n\nDefine A_ij=Lambda_ij p_j for i!=j and A_ii=0. Since omega_i=out_i-in_i,\nequation(14) says sum_j Lambda_ij p_j=p_i in_i. Therefore\n\n    sum_j A_ij=sum_j A_ji for every i.                 (15)\n\nThus A is a nonnegative circulation. Write c_i=1/p_i. Substituting\nLambda_ij=A_ij c_j into the original value flow and using(15) gives\n\n    sum_i c_i omega_i\n      =sum_(i,j) A_ij(c_i c_j-c_i^2)\n      =-1/2 sum_(i,j)A_ij(c_i-c_j)^2.\n\nEquivalently, the entire circulation has the fixed Dirichlet energy\n\n    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij (p_i-p_j)^2/(p_i^2 p_j)\n       =4[sum_(i<4)h_i/p_i-s/C] <4s<35.              (16)\n\nAll steps exceed1/2 by(13). Consequently |p_i-p_j|>1/2 for i!=j.\nAlso every p_i<10 by(8), so p_i^2 p_j<1000. Every coefficient of\nLambda_ij on the left of(16) exceeds1/4000. Hence\n\n    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij < 140000.                      (17)\n\nThis is a bound on the complete multiplier vector, not a choice of sparse\nsupport. No skew freedom or PSD remainder is fixed. In particular all\npossible solutions of(1) at the requested threshold belong to the closed\nbounded box obtained by replacing the strict inequalities in(8),(13),(17)\nby non-strict ones. The equations and PSD conditions are continuous, so\ntheir solution set in this box is compact (possibly empty).\n\n## 6. Remaining problem\n\nThe necessary cut domain is not asserted sufficient. The full original\nfeasibility problem is still open inside the compact region above.\nNeither a feasible seed nor a global separator has been produced.\nAll20 multipliers, all five formal gradients, and every remaining step\ndegree of freedom are retained. C78 is a conditional research bridge,\nnot a premise or an original-target solution.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_four_step_global_domain", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/four_step_seed_compact_domain.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "status": "complete_joint_proof_draft_unverified", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "scope": "full four-step certificate interface at capacity>=193/25", "new_numerical_evaluations": 0, "new_fact_submit_calls": 0, "fixed_rational_constant_checks": 5}, "glossary": {"C_seed": "Capacity 1+sum_i h_i of the four-step seed; written C locally.", "p_seed_i": "1+sum_(t<i)h_t; written p_i locally.", "R_C": "Increasing rational map z ->1+2Cz/(C+z).", "r_cut_k": "R_C iterated k times at1; written r_k locally.", "V_seed": "Intrinsic energy defined in Section1; written V locally.", "Lambda_seed": "Full nonnegative five-node off-diagonal interpolation multiplier matrix; written Lambda locally.", "M_seed": "Full five-gradient PSD matrix D+sym(LT) of the assigned seed interface; written M locally.", "A_seed_ij": "Destination-weighted multiplier Lambda_ij p_j, a balanced circulation; written A_ij locally."}}

Previous max drafts motivate this calculation but are\nnot premises; every seed and join identity needed here is rederived.\n\n## 1. Statement and scope\n\nWrite lambda=1+sqrt(2), theta=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda, and put\n\n a_lo=14127/10000, a_hi=14128/10000,\n b_lo=20043/10000, b_hi=20044/10000,\n M_bar=251/250, P_pair=189/200, eta_tree=99981/100000.\n\nAt every leaf choose independently\n\n a_lo<a<a_hi, b_lo<b<b_hi, 0<=rho<=1, E>=0.\n\nDefine c=a-1, z=b-1, m=a+b, Klen=a+rho, eta=z/Klen,\nA=1+2a+b, epsilon=sqrt(E), and vectors in R^3\n\n U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho sqrt(eta),epsilon).\n\nThe four gradients and four positions of the three-step atom are\n\n g=(U,U,V,W),\n x=(mU+aV+W, bU+aV+W, aV+W, W).\n\nLet y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>, and set\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. The word is(a,b,a), its mass is A, its\ninitial vector is p=x_0, and its endpoint is W. Require\n\n                 0<=E<=(1-rho)e,  e=(a+rho-1)eta.       (1)\n\nBuild a balanced binary tree with N=2^J such leaves. At an internal node\nwith children of masses A_L,A_R, use the bridge step1+r, where\n\n r>0, r(A_L+A_R+r)=A_L A_R,\n A_parent=A_L+A_R+r.                                  (2)\n\nEmbed both children isometrically, retaining the entire left child and a\nscaled copy of the entire right child, including all positions, gradients,\nvalues, and both terminal samples. If the left endpoint is w_L and the\nright initial vector has norm R_R, the right scale and alignment are\n\n alpha=r||w_L||/R_R,\n alpha J_R(p_R)=-r J_L(w_L).                           (3)\n\nRight positions and gradients are multiplied by alpha, and right function\nvalues by alpha^2; left values are retained without rescaling.\n\nAll isometries and ambient dimensions may be chosen anew at every node,\nand all choices may depend on the full target horizon. A tree is legal\nonly if EVERY original interpolation inequality Q_ij<=0, including the\noptimum, holds for its final data. No other restriction on the transverse\nmaps is imposed.\n\nFor any node define its actual core ratio\n\n             kappa_node=A_node^2 ||w_node||^2/R_node^2,\n\nwhere R_node is its true initial distance to the minimizer set of the\nprojection interpolant constructed below. Then any legal tree with J>=5\nobeys\n\n kappa_root <= M_bar^16 eta_tree^(N/16-1).              (4)\n\nIn particular kappa_root tends to0 exponentially in the number of leaves,\nuniformly over all legal choices in this family. The normalized final\nfunction gap for its actual full word of length n=4N-1 is exactly\n\n 2(F(x_n)-min F)/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2\n                  =kappa_root/A_root^2.              (5)\n\nIts ratio to the ORIGINAL benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n tends uniformly to0.\n\nThis family contains the actual atom used in the rational15/31 drafts:\na=(311sqrt(2)-16)/300, b=(590+8sqrt(2))/300,\nrho=599/600, E=1/10000. For the a,b inclusion, use\n141421/100000<sqrt(2)<707107/500000, whose two strict inequalities follow\nby squaring, and substitute in the affine definitions of a,b. The claim\nalso allows nonidentical a,b in the displayed box and arbitrary legal\nrho,E, rather than just small terminal angles.\n\nThis is an obstruction to a specified witness family. It is not an upper\nbound on W_n(h), a no-go theorem for arbitrary projection trajectories,\nor a resolution of the original minimax problem. Section9 supplies an\nexplicit counterexample to the unrestricted geometric generalization.\n\n## 2. Every atomic row, one global function, and true distance\n\nThe residuals are\n\n y0=(m-1)U+Klen V+epsilon e3,\n y1=zU+Klen V+epsilon e3,\n y2=(Klen-1)V+epsilon e3,\n y3=0,\n\nwhere e3=(0,0,1), and the energies are(m-1,z,e,0). At receiving\nsample0 the other gradient vertices0,V,W have normals\n\n -(m-1), -a, -a-(1-rho)z+E.\n\nAt receiving sample1 the vertices0,V,W give\n\n -z, 0, -(1-rho)z+E.\n\nAt receiving sample2 the vertices0,U,W give\n\n -e, -e, -(1-rho)e+E.\n\nThe omitted repeated U vertex has the same normal, the receiving vertex\nitself has normal0, and all normals at sample3 vanish. Because0<e<z,\nthese inequalities hold if and only if(1) holds. This is a full atomic\naudit, including the positive E charge from the third coordinate.\n\nFor any finite collection of such projection jets, define\n\n K_body=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),\n F(x)=max_(v in K_body)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).               (6)\n\nCompleting the square makes the unique maximizing vector the Euclidean\nprojection P_K_body(x). The two projection inequalities, added, show\n\n ||P_K_body(x)-P_K_body(x')||^2\n       <=<P_K_body(x)-P_K_body(x'),x-x'>.\n\nEvaluating the maximum at the two maximizers proves differentiability\nwith gradient P_K_body, and the displayed inequality proves that this\ngradient is1-Lipschitz. Thus F is a finite convex differentiable1-smooth\nfunction. The vertex normals imply P_K_body(x_i)=g_i and F(x_i)=f_i.\nUsing the signed convention of accepted1252b8823bb5a290 gives exactly\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n     =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                  (7)\n\nThe optimum is0, with Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-e_i.\nThus a legal complete tree is interpolated by ONE function(6) with all\nits original values. No successive change of the function is involved.\n\nThe minimizer set of(6) is K_body^polar. If x is in this polar, the\nmaximum equals0. Conversely, if F(x)=0, insert tv for each v in K_body\nand0<t<=1, divide by t, and let t decrease to0; then <x,v><=0.\nAt a leaf p=mU+aV+W is a positive combination of retained vertices.\nConsequently <p,v><=0 for v in K_body^polar, and\n||p-v||^2>=||p||^2. Zero is a closest minimizer. At each join the initial\nvector and these first-leaf vertices are retained by a left isometry.\nThe same argument therefore gives the true squared initial distance\nR_node^2=||p_node||^2 at every legal node.\n\nFor a leaf, direct inner products give\n\n R^2=m^2+Klen z+E,\n N_leaf=||W||^2=rho^2 eta+E,\n D_leaf=<p,W>=rho z+E.                                (8)\n\nThere is no normalization loss hidden in these formulas.\n\n## 3. Exact coordinates for the energy-angle tradeoff\n\nDefine two dimensionless coordinates of a leaf:\n\n sigma=rho+E/e,\n u=c sqrt((sigma-rho)/(c+rho)).                        (9)\n\nBy(1), 0<=rho<=sigma<=1. The value u is also exactly\nc epsilon sqrt(eta)/e. These definitions permit zero E and zero rho.\nPut\n\n C0=a(m^2+az), C1=m^2+2az,\n D(sigma)=C0+C1 sigma+z sigma^2,\n D1=D(1)=(a+1)(m^2+(a+1)z),\n T=(m^2+z)/c^2, M=A^2 z/D1.\n\nSolving(9) for rho gives\n\n rho=(sigma-u^2/c)/(1+u^2/c^2).\n\nSubstitution in(8), with the common denominator a+sigma+u^2/c^2,\ngives the exact identities\n\n kappa=A^2 z(sigma^2+u^2)/(D(sigma)+T u^2),\n d:=D_leaf/R^2\n     =z[sigma(sigma+a)-u^2/c]/(D(sigma)+T u^2).         (10)\n\nIn particular,\n\n M-kappa = M * [(1-sigma){C0+(C0+C1)sigma}\n                      +(T-D1)u^2]/[D(sigma)+T u^2].   (11)\n\nThe following rational bounds hold throughout the a,b box:\n\n 233/40<A<583/100,\n 34.01<D1<34.04, 74<T<75,\n C0>18.49, C1>14.51,\n 34.04<A^2 z<34.14,\n 1<M<M_bar=251/250.                                   (12)\n\nAll terminating decimals in this manuscript denote exact rationals.\nHere is enough endpoint arithmetic to check every bound in(12):\n\n 3.417<m<3.4172, .4127<c<.4128,\n 1.0043<z<1.0044, 5.8297<A<5.83;\n\n 2.4127[3.417^2+2.4127(1.0043)]>34.01,\n 2.4128[3.4172^2+2.4128(1.0044)]<34.04;\n\n (3.417^2+1.0043)>74(.4128)^2,\n (3.4172^2+1.0044)<75(.4127)^2;\n\n 1.4127[3.417^2+1.4127(1.0043)]>18.49,\n 3.417^2+2(1.4127)(1.0043)>14.51;\n\n (5.8297)^2(1.0043)>34.04,\n (5.83)^2(1.0044)<34.14,\n 34.14/34.01<251/250.\n\nThe positive coefficients make endpoint substitution legitimate in\neach displayed expression. Equation(11), T>D1, and sigma<=1 show that\nEVERY legal leaf has0<=kappa<=M<M_bar. This is an exact global envelope\nover rho,E in this atomic family, not a numerical maximization.\n\n## 4. Quantitative cost when a leaf has high energy\n\nFor the rest of this section suppose\n\n kappa>47/50=.94,\n delta=M_bar-kappa<8/125=.064.                        (13)\n\nThen\n\n delta >= (5/4)(1-sigma)+u^2.                         (14)\n\nTo prove this, first sigma>19/20=.95. Indeed(10) is a weighted average\nof A^2 z sigma^2/D(sigma) and A^2 z/T when u>0; for u=0 it is the first\nquantity. The first ratio is increasing in sigma>=0, since the numerator\nof its derivative after removing a positive factor is\nsigma(2C0+C1 sigma). At sigma=.95 its value is at most\n\n 34.14(.95)^2/[18.49+14.51(.95)+(.95)^2]<.94,\n\nwhile A^2 z/T<34.14/74<.94. This proves the claim about sigma.\n\nDividing(11) by kappa and dropping the first nonnegative term yields\n\n (M-kappa)/kappa\n   >= (T/D1-1)u^2/(1+u^2) >= (117/100)u^2/(1+u^2).\n\nHere T/D1-1>74/34.04-1>117/100. If u=0 the following bound is immediate;\nif u>0 the last inequality is strict. Thus(13) implies\n\n u^2 < (8/125)/[(47/50)(117/100)-8/125]\n     =320/5179<1/16.\n\nThe denominator in(11) is therefore below34.04+75/16=38.7275.\nIts coefficient of u^2, including M>1, is greater than\n(74-34.04)/38.7275>1. Its coefficient of1-sigma is greater than\n\n [18.49+(18.49+14.51)(.95)]/38.7275>5/4.\n\nSince delta>=M-kappa, this proves(14).\n\nWe also need the following one-sided angle estimate, valid for every\nleaf and hence in particular under(13):\n\n                      d>.071-delta/5.                (15)\n\nFor a direct proof set\n\n ell=[(a+1)T+D1/c]/[A^2(T-D1)],\n beta=z[(a+1)+1/c]/(T-D1),\n d0=z/[m^2+(a+1)z].\n\nThen ell M-beta=d0. On subtracting ell kappa-beta from(10), the u^2\ncoefficient cancels. The remaining numerator is a polynomial in sigma,\nvanishing at1, with constant beta C0>0 and linear coefficient\naz+beta C1>0. It factors as\n\n (1-sigma)[beta C0+(az+beta C1+beta C0)sigma]>=0.\n\nConsequently d>=ell kappa-beta=d0-ell(M-kappa).\nThe endpoint bounds give d0>1.004/14.11>.071. They also give\n\n ell < [(2.42)(75)+(34.04)(2.5)]/[(33.9)(39.9)]<1/5.\n\nSince0<=M-kappa<=delta, inequality(15) follows.\n\nFinally, define the perpendicular ratio of the terminal gradient by\n\n t_perp=||W-(<p,W>/R^2)p||/R\n        =sqrt(kappa/A^2-d^2).\n\nUnder(13), d>.071-.064/5=.0582=291/5000, and\nkappa/A^2<M_bar/(233/40)^2<74/2500. Hence\n\n t_perp^2<74/2500-(291/5000)^2\n           =655319/25000000<(81/500)^2,\n                         t_perp<81/500=.162.          (16)\n\nEquations(14)-(16) retain the E/e contribution to the axial coefficient\nsigma. In particular maximal legal E makes sigma=1; it is not permissible\nto keep its transverse benefit while discarding this axial charge.\n\n## 5. An original-row condition at every large bridge\n\nConsider a node whose two children have at least two atomic leaves.\nLet the LAST leaf of the left child have parameters with subscript L,\nand let the FIRST leaf of the right child have subscript R. Write the\nlast leaf's overall scale inside the left child as b_scale>0. Its\nendpoint and penultimate residual are isometric copies of\n\n b_scale W_L, b_scale y2_L,\n y2_L=c_L V_L+W_L.\n\nThe receiving energy is e_rec=b_scale^2 e_L>0. Direct projection onto\nthe W_L axis gives\n\n <W_L,y2_L>/e_L=sigma_L,\n ||(y2_L)_perp W_L||=c_L epsilon_L sqrt(eta_L/N_L).\n\nWithin the right child, the FIRST bridge joins its first two leaves.\nIf its excess is r_first and the second leaf's mass is A_second, the\nsource identity p_second=m_second U_second+a_second V_second+W_second\nand the bridge alignment give\n\n m_second G4+a_second G6+G7=-r_first W_R.\n\nHere W_R is embedded from the right child's first leaf, and G4,G6,G7\nare its actual retained gradients, including all intervening scales.\nDividing by A_second produces a convex combination v_R of these three\ngradients, because m_second+a_second+1=A_second. Thus\n\n                 v_R=-tau W_R,\n                 tau=r_first/A_second>0.             (17)\n\nThis vector identity is retained by EVERY subsequent left embedding,\nregardless of earlier transverse choices. The initial vector of the\nwhole right child is the same isometric copy of p_R from its first leaf.\n\nApply the proposed parent alignment(3). The component of the weighted\nnew right gradient along the left endpoint gives exactly\nr e_rec tau sigma_L d_R. Cauchy--Schwarz bounds its perpendicular\ncomponent below by-r e_rec tau u_L t_perp,R. Subtract the receiving\nenergy and use(7): the corresponding convex combination of ORIGINAL\ncross interpolation rows satisfies\n\n sum weights Q / e_rec\n        >= r tau [sigma_L d_R-u_L t_perp,R]-1.         (18)\n\nThis argument permits every real transverse contraction, every ambient\ndimension and all earlier maps. It has not optimized a reduced Gram\nmatrix or deleted any interpolation rows.\n\nFor a node containing N_node leaves, the three rows in(18) have actual\nindices Q_(2N_node+4,2N_node-2), Q_(2N_node+6,2N_node-2), and\nQ_(2N_node+7,2N_node-2), with the positive weights in(17).\n\nAt a balanced parent of depth at least5, the factor r tau has the\nuniform bound\n\n                            r tau>169/5.              (19)\n\nFirst, the mass ratio q=A_first/A_second of any two leaves is greater\nthan999/1000, using5.8297<A<5.83. Substitution of r_first=tau A_second\nin(2) gives tau^2+(q+1)tau-q=0. This increasing polynomial is negative\nat tau=207/500=.414: its value is strictly below\n.585396-(.999)(.586)=-.000018. Thus tau>207/500.\n\nNext, the positive root r in(2) is increasing in either mass: it lies\nstrictly between0 and min(A_L,A_R), and implicit differentiation gives\ndr/dA_L=(A_R-r)/(2r+A_L+A_R)>0, with the analogous right derivative.\nEqual child masses A have parent mass lambda A. Induction therefore\nplaces every depth-j mass above lambda^j(233/40). At depth at least5\nthe new excess is consequently greater thanlambda^3(233/40).\nSince lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)>281/20, one obtains\n\n r tau>(281/20)(233/40)(207/500)\n        =13552911/400000>169/5,\n\nwhich proves(19).\n\n## 6. Each middle pair costs at least a fixed product factor\n\nWe claim that every legal node of depth at least5 must satisfy\n\n                       kappa_L kappa_R<P_pair=189/200, (20)\n\nwhere these are the two LEAF ratios in Section5, not the child ratios.\n\nSuppose instead kappa_L kappa_R>=189/200. Since each ratio is below\nM_bar, each exceeds(189/200)/M_bar=945/1004>47/50. Put\ndelta_L=M_bar-kappa_L and delta_R=M_bar-kappa_R. The arithmetic-geometric\nmean inequality gives\n\n delta_L+delta_R<=2M_bar-2sqrt(189/200)<8/125,\n\nbecause(243/250)^2<189/200. Thus(14)-(16) apply to both leaves.\nSet D_bound=.071-delta_R/5>0. Equations(14)-(16) imply\n\n sigma_L d_R-u_L t_perp,R\n >=D_bound[1-(4/5)delta_L+(4/5)u_L^2]-(81/500)u_L.\n\nFor0<=u_L<=sqrt(delta_L), the right side is decreasing in u_L.\nIts derivative is(8/5)D_bound u_L-81/500<0, using\nD_bound<=.071 and u_L<sqrt(.064)<.255. Therefore\n\n sigma_L d_R-u_L t_perp,R\n             >=.071-delta_R/5-(81/500)sqrt(delta_L).\n\nFor delta_L+delta_R<=8/125, the largest possible subtracted expression\nis(81/500)sqrt(8/125). Indeed the derivative of\n(8/125-delta_L)/5+(81/500)sqrt(delta_L) is positive throughout\n0<delta_L<=8/125. Since sqrt(8/125)<51/200, it follows that\n\n sigma_L d_R-u_L t_perp,R\n         >71/1000-(81/500)(51/200)=2969/100000.         (21)\n\nCombining(18),(19),(21) gives the strict ORIGINAL-row violation\n\n sum weights Q/e_rec\n        >(169/5)(2969/100000)-1=1761/500000>0.\n\nThis contradicts legality and proves(20). No numerical infeasibility\nflag or assumption about an optimal transverse map enters this argument.\n\n## 7. Disjoint pairs and the exact normalization product\n\nAt each internal node, the endpoint norm is\n||w_parent||^2=r^2||w_L||^2||w_R||^2/R_R^2, by(3). Its true initial\ndistance is R_L, by Section2. Since A_parent r=A_L A_R, one obtains\ndirectly\n\n                kappa_parent=kappa_child,L kappa_child,R.\n\nInducting gives kappa_root=product_(all leaves)kappa_leaf. If an\nendpoint vanishes, this product and all subsequent endpoint energies\nare zero and(4) is immediate. Thus the positive-scale argument above\nloses no zero case relevant to the bound.\n\nNumber the N=2^J leaves from1 toN in chronological order. A midpoint\npair belonging to a node of depth at least5 is exactly a pair\n\n                 (16k,16k+1), 1<=k<N/16.\n\nTo check this bijection, write a split position as2^(d-1)(2s+1): its\n2-adic valuation is d-1. Thus d>=5 is equivalent to divisibility by16.\nAll these pairs are disjoint. Their number is M_pairs=N/16-1.\nEach pair has product below P_pair by(20), and each remaining leaf has\nkappa<M_bar. Hence\n\n kappa_root<=P_pair^M_pairs M_bar^(N-2M_pairs)\n            =M_bar^16(P_pair M_bar^14)^M_pairs.        (22)\n\nFor completeness, M_bar^14<529/500 follows by setting x=1/250 and\nbounding the binomial coefficients from their quadratic term onwards.\nThe ratio of each next term to its predecessor is at most4x for these\nterms, so\n\n (1+x)^14<=1+14x+91x^2/(1-4x)\n          =1+7/125+91/61500<529/500.\n\nTherefore P_pair M_bar^14<(189/200)(529/500)=99981/100000.\nEquation(22) proves(4). This is stronger than merely locating one\nlow-energy leaf inside each disjoint subtree: the actual pair costs\nat ALL large split positions are charged without double counting.\n\n## 8. The original final objective and exact B comparison\n\nThe final residual is0, so F(x_n)=||w_root||^2/2. The positive-cone\nargument proves the true distance R_root, yielding(5) without a tail,\nextra coordinate or boundary reset. There are exactly3N+(N-1)=4N-1\nsteps. By the mass monotonicity already proved,\n\n A_root>lambda^J(233/40).\n\nLet p_exp=log_2(3). Accepted factb98bb807d514e80d gives the bound for\nthe EXACT original dominant recurrence B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1:\n\n                  B_n<=1+4n^p_exp<1+36N^p_exp.\n\nConsequently the actual full-word witness ratio obeys\n\n B_(4N-1) * 2F(x_(4N-1))/R_root^2\n <= [(1+36N^p_exp) M_bar^16 eta_tree^(N/16-1)]\n       /[(233/40)^2 lambda^(2J)] ->0.                 (23)\n\nThe bound is uniform over all choices made at this horizon, including\nnonidentical leaves and every transverse map. It does not require a\nsingle atom or a single function to serve multiple horizons. For each\nchosen run, however, all samples are realized by the ONE function(6).\n\nNo objective-tail lemma is used. Appending a different final tail or\naltering inherited values is a different construction whose original\nrows and distance require separate analysis. Equation(23) bounds the\npayoff of these specified witnesses, never the supremum W_n(h).\n\n## 9. Scope test: a different atom iterates with growing energy\n\nIt is false that every isometrically retained source-matched projection\ntree must have vanishing core energy. Take the single global quadratic\nF(x)=x^2/2 on R, x_0=1, and the atom h0=(2,0,0). Every sample has\ng_i=x_i, y_i=0, f_i=x_i^2/2. Its endpoint is-1 and its mass is A0=3.\nAll original Q rows equal0, including the optimum. If desired, it is\nalso realized by(6), taking the finite convex hull of the gradients of\nthe final chosen trajectory; all sample points belong to this interval.\n\nDefine h_(j+1)=(h_j,1+r_j,h_j), A_j=3lambda^j,\nr_j=theta A_j. The gradient-descent endpoint w_j from initial1 obeys\n\n w_0=-1, w_(j+1)=-r_j w_j^2.\n\nThe first equality is the literal atom. The second follows because the\nleft copy multiplies the initial point by w_j, the bridge by-r_j, and\nthe right copy again by w_j. Thus every child is an actual scaled\nisometric copy on the SAME quadratic and the bridge obeys(2)-(3).\nThe true initial distance remains1. Its energy satisfies\n\n kappa_0=9, kappa_(j+1)=kappa_j^2,\n                       kappa_j=9^(2^j).\n\nThis example violates any proposed extension of(4) to unrestricted\natomic projection trajectories. It does not violate the stated result:\nits word, mass, and zero receiving residuals fall outside Section1.\nIt also supplies no improved schedule or uniform minimax converse.\n\n## 10. A general boundary condition for a changed atom\n\nFor any proposed join of projection jets with the value structure(7),\nlet p_R be the right initial vector, R_R its\nnorm, and w_L the nonzero left endpoint. For any left receiving residual\ny with positive energy e_rec=<g,y>, decompose y along w_L:\n\n a_axis=<y,w_L>/||w_L||,\n b_perp=||y-a_axis w_L/||w_L||||.\n\nFor EVERY convex combination v of retained right gradients, write\nv_perp=v-(<v,p_R>/R_R^2)p_R. Alignment(3) and Cauchy--Schwarz give the\nnecessary original-row condition\n\n alpha[-a_axis <p_R,v>/R_R-b_perp||v_perp||]<=e_rec,\n                  alpha=r||w_L||/R_R.                (24)\n\nTaking the supremum of the left side over the actual retained convex\nhull gives the strongest bound furnished by this single-row Cauchy\nargument. This is still a necessary condition, not simultaneous\nsufficiency for all receiving rows and the full transverse contraction.\n\nThe present proof uses the retained source combination(17) in(24),\nthen prices the leaf's transverse allowance by(11). A successful new\natom must change this quantitative relationship, the retained source\ngeometry, the source-matched join rule, or the value/residual structure.\nThe quadratic example avoids the receiving obstruction because every\ny is zero. It illustrates an escape in geometry, while leaving the\noriginal all-schedule objective-profile problem unresolved.\n\n## Provenance and glossary\n\nThe fact ids1252b8823bb5a290 andb98bb807d514e80d are the only verified\npredecessors used. {"id": "6a3a13d94112727b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T03:28:45.081407+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Strengthened independent four-step domain DRAFT: EVERY feasible full seed with sum h>=168/25 has h_1>1 and h_2>1, and at least one endpoint>1. The proof adds an all-Q two-coordinate cut/quadratic repair, a five-coefficient exact negative Bernstein quartic, general repaired-cut tests at both middle cuts, an endpoint-sum bound, and a nonlinear clipped-tail condition. Full compactness6695d6fd665245ef is rederived in the SAME joint proof; no new fact or seed.", "evidence": "# Full four-step seed: a compact domain from legal cut functions\n\nStatus: complete joint proof DRAFT, not verifier-accepted. This round has no\nnumerical or fact_submit allowance. No unverified finding is used as a premise.\nThe accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the interpolation convention Q<=0;\nthe particular functions and all algebra below are derived explicitly.\n\n## 1. Interface and energy\n\nLet h=(h_0,h_1,h_2,h_3)>=0, s=sum h_i, C=1+s>=193/25.\nIndices i,j range over0,...,4. Set p_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, so p_0=1,p_4=C.\nLet e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T. Define T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,T_it=0 for t>i,\nLambda_ii=0,Lambda_ij>=0,L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\nomega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,2s)^T, and\nD=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s(s+2)).\nThe full interface is\n\n    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                 (1)\n\nNo multiplier support, rank, remainder, symmetry, or step order is fixed.\nFor arbitrary formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n      -s(s+1)||g_4||^2.\n\nPut delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 and y_i=x_i-g_i=x_0-(Tg)_i.\nThen Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>, while\nV=-omega^T delta+sum_i D_ii||g_i||^2. Value flow in(1) therefore gives\n\n    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>. (2)\n\nIn particular every feasible full certificate implies V>=0 on every legal\nconvex differentiable1-smooth trajectory with a minimizer. This direction\nrequires neither certificate necessity nor any duality assertion.\n\n## 2. Legal cut witnesses at every position\n\nFix j<4, put b=h_j,P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i, and first assume b>1.\nSet a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0. Define the scalar function\n\n    f(x)=x-1/2                     if x>=1,\n         x^2/2                     if -a<=x<=1,\n         -a*x-a^2/2                if x<=-a.\n\nIts derivative is clamp(x,[-a,1]), which is continuous, nondecreasing,\nand1-Lipschitz. The function is convex1-smooth and has a minimizer at0.\nStart at x_0=P+1. Before and at j all gradients are1, and x_j=1.\nAfter step j, x_(j+1)=1-b=-a(R+1). All subsequent gradients equal-a,\nsince the remaining nonnegative steps move monotonically to x_4=-a.\nEvery sampled value obeys f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and x_4=g_4.\n\nFor any vector-valued data obeying these two identities, GD telescoping gives\n\n    2sum_(i<4)h_i f_i+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n      =||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2,\n    V=||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.                         (3)\n\nApplied to this scalar function, nonnegative V implies\n\n    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+P)(1+R), for every j=0,...,3.      (4)\n\nIf h_j<=1 the same inequality is automatic. Thus(4) holds on every\nzero-step boundary too. Failure of any inequality is a specific legal\nnegative-V function, hence rules out the full interface(1).\n\n## 3. Prefix bounds and compact capacity\n\nFor fixed C>1 define the increasing rational map\n\n    R_C(z)=1+2Cz/(C+z), z>=0,\n    r_k=R_C iterated k times at1, k=0,1,2,3.\n\nRearrangement of(4) gives p_(i+1)<=R_C(p_i). Conversely this inequality\nis equivalent to(4) on the nonnegative schedule domain. Induction yields\np_i<=r_i. The cut constraints are invariant under reversing the four\nsteps, regardless of whether the original certificate admits reversal.\nApplying the same induction to the reversed cut inequalities gives\n\n    p_i >= C+1-r_(4-i).                               (5)\n\nOnly the cases i=1,2,3 are needed; r_0=1. Explicitly,\n\n    r_1=(3C+1)/(C+1),\n    r_2=(7C^2+6C+1)/(C^2+4C+1),\n    r_3=(15C^3+23C^2+9C+1)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1).          (6)\n\nTaking i=2 in(5) and p_2<=r_2 gives C+1<=2r_2, hence\n\n    C^3-9C^2-7C-1<=0.                                (7)\n\nThis polynomial is strictly increasing for C>=7: its derivative\n3C^2-18C-7 is positive there and increasing. At C=39/4 its value is\n131/64>0. Since C>=193/25>7, we conclude\n\n    193/25 <= C < 39/4.                               (8)\n\nIn particular this argument covers all larger candidate sums; it did not\nreplace the target by the slice sum h=168/25.\n\n## 4. All four steps are uniformly positive and bounded\n\nThe first and last cut inequalities give\n\n    h_0,h_3 <= 2C/(C+1) < 78/43.                      (9)\n\nFor the lower bounds, subtract the upper prefix bound from the lower\nbound for the next prefix in(5). This gives\n\n    h_0,h_3 >= C-r_3 = N(C)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1),\n    h_1,h_2 >= C+1-r_1-r_2 = N(C)/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)), (10)\n    N(C)=C^4-4C^3-16C^2-8C-1.\n\nThe endpoint lower bound exceeds1/2 exactly when\n\n    A(C)=2C^4-9C^3-43C^2-23C-3>0.\n\nThe middle lower bound exceeds4/5 exactly when\n\n    B(C)=5C^4-24C^3-100C^2-60C-9>0.\n\nAt C=77/10 these polynomials respectively equal1922412/10000 and\n2197285/10000. They are strictly increasing for C>=7: their derivatives\nare bounded below respectively by29C^2-86C-23 and68C^2-200C-60,\nboth positive and increasing on that interval. Since193/25>77/10,\n\n    h_0,h_3>1/2, h_1,h_2>4/5.                         (11)\n\nFor completeness, the middle upper bound uses the same cuts, without\nassuming either middle step exceeds1. Write\n\n    G_C(z)=R_C(z)-z=1+z(C-z)/(C+z).\n\nFor1<=z<=r_1<3 its derivative is\n(C^2-2Cz-z^2)/(C+z)^2>0: its numerator is at least C^2-6C-9,\nwhich is positive for C>=15/2. Thus h_1<=G_C(p_1)<=G_C(r_1), and\nthe reversed cut constraints give the same bound for h_2. Calculation yields\n\n    G_C(r_1)=4C^3/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)).                  (12)\n\nThe right side is strictly increasing in C>0: its logarithmic derivative\nhas positive numerator5C^2+10C+3 after multiplication by positive\ndenominators. At39/4 its value is237276/92923<13/5, with the difference\n21619/464615. Consequently\n\n    1/2<h_0,h_3<78/43, 4/5<h_1,h_2<13/5.             (13)\n\nThis excludes every zero-step boundary for this threshold by actual\nwitnesses. It does not assert h_i>1, ordering, or reversal of certificates.\n\n## 5. Full multiplier compactness from the common-gradient kernel\n\nThis step bounds all20 original multipliers, including every cycle.\nThe vector p=Te has entries p_i. Since\n\n    sum_i D_ii=-s^2-sum_(i<4)h_i^2,\n    omega^T p=s^2+sum_(i<4)h_i^2,\n\nequations(1) give e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0.\nFor clarity this implication follows by inserting e+t v into the quadratic\nform: a nonzero linear coefficient would violate nonnegativity for one\nsign of sufficiently small t. Calculation of each component gives\n\n    2De+T^T omega=-diag(p)omega,\n    Lp=diag(p)omega.                                  (14)\n\nDefine A_ij=Lambda_ij p_j for i!=j and A_ii=0. Since omega_i=out_i-in_i,\nequation(14) says sum_j Lambda_ij p_j=p_i in_i. Therefore\n\n    sum_j A_ij=sum_j A_ji for every i.                 (15)\n\nThus A is a nonnegative circulation. Write c_i=1/p_i. Substituting\nLambda_ij=A_ij c_j into the original value flow and using(15) gives\n\n    sum_i c_i omega_i\n      =sum_(i,j) A_ij(c_i c_j-c_i^2)\n      =-1/2 sum_(i,j)A_ij(c_i-c_j)^2.\n\nEquivalently, the entire circulation has the fixed Dirichlet energy\n\n    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij (p_i-p_j)^2/(p_i^2 p_j)\n       =4[sum_(i<4)h_i/p_i-s/C] <4s<35.              (16)\n\nAll steps exceed1/2 by(13). Consequently |p_i-p_j|>1/2 for i!=j.\nAlso every p_i<10 by(8), so p_i^2 p_j<1000. Every coefficient of\nLambda_ij on the left of(16) exceeds1/4000. Hence\n\n    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij < 140000.                      (17)\n\nThis is a bound on the complete multiplier vector, not a choice of sparse\nsupport. No skew freedom or PSD remainder is fixed. In particular all\npossible solutions of(1) at the requested threshold belong to the closed\nbounded box obtained by replacing the strict inequalities in(8),(13),(17)\nby non-strict ones. The equations and PSD conditions are continuous, so\ntheir solution set in this box is compact (possibly empty).\n\n## 6. A suffix witness and a full two-coordinate mixed witness\n\nThe first new constraint is\n\n    C |(1-h_2)(1-h_3)| <= 1+h_0+h_1.                  (18)\n\nTo prove it, take the1-smooth convex function with derivative min(x,1),\nwhose values are x^2/2 for x<=1 and x-1/2 for x>=1. Start at\nx_0=1+h_0+h_1. The first two gradients are1, x_2=1, x_3=1-h_2<=1,\nand x_4=(1-h_2)(1-h_3). Every sample satisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.\nWithout imposing x_4=g_4, the same telescoping calculation as(3) gives\n\n    V=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                     (19)\n\nIf x_4<=1, then g_4=x_4 and(19) is precisely(18). If x_4>1,\ng_4=1 and x_4+s>C>x_0, since h_2+h_3>0 by(13), giving V<0.\nThis latter case is excluded, proving(18) for every candidate.\n\nHere is a second necessary inequality with its complete interpolation\nproof. Suppose u=h_0>1, b=h_1>1, put a=u-1,A=1+u,\nR=h_2+h_3,D_tail=1+R, eta=(b-1)/D_tail, and assume a*eta<1.\nThen every feasible seed satisfies\n\n    H_mix(u,C,eta)=u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta\n                   -(u-1)C^2 eta^2 >=0.             (20)\n\nTake the base cut data from Section2 with j=1. Its gradients are1\nat i=0,1 and-eta at i=2,3,4; its initial point is A and its final\npoint and gradient are-eta. Its proximal points y_i=x_i-g_i are\ny_0=u,y_1=0 and y_i=-eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.\nIts energy is F_base=A^2-C^2 eta^2.\n\nDefine auxiliary scalar data by x_0=g_0=1, x_1=g_1=-a,\ng_i=a*eta for i>=2, and actual GD recursion for x_i. Thus\nx_2=a(b-1)=a*eta*D_tail, x_4=g_4=a*eta; all earlier auxiliary\nproximal points are0 and y_i=a*eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.\nAssign f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and include the zero optimum jet.\n\nFor any data with this value assignment, direct expansion gives\nQ_ij=(g_i-g_j)y_j. Optimum residuals are Q_(i,star)=0 and\nQ_(star,i)=-g_i y_i. All auxiliary optimum residuals are nonpositive.\nThe only positive auxiliary finite residuals are, for j=2,3,\n\n    Q_aux,(0,j)=(1-a*eta)a*eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.\n\nEvery other finite residual is nonpositive: target j=0,1,4 has y_j=0;\ntwo post-cut gradients agree; the pre-cut gradient-a is smaller than\na*eta. The corresponding base residuals are\n\n    Q_base,(0,j)=-(1+eta)eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.\n\nWith epsilon=(1+eta)/(a(1-a*eta))>0, take the orthogonal sum of the\nbase and sqrt(epsilon) times the auxiliary positions and gradients,\nand the sum of their function values with weight epsilon. All Q add;\nthese two possible positive residuals cancel, and every other one remains\nnonpositive. The full finite array, including the optimum, is realized\nby one globally convex1-smooth function by fact1252b8823bb5a290.\nIts energy is\n\n    F_base+epsilon(1-C^2 a^2 eta^2)\n       = u*H_mix(u,C,eta)/(a(1-a*eta)).                (21)\n\nThis equality follows by expanding both sides and using\n1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2). All denominators are positive, proving(20).\nEvery original directed interpolation pair is included in this argument.\n\n## 7. Both middle steps are strictly greater than one\n\nFor this section write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1.\nThe previously proved bounds imply\n\n    a,d<9/11; b,c<14/9; a+b+c+d=C-5>=68/25.           (22)\n\nIndeed78/43<20/11 and237276/92923<23/9; the latter difference is\n1745/836307. These bounds allow negative a,b,c,d initially.\n\nFirst suppose h_1<=1, so b<=0. Then\n\n    c+d >=68/25-a-b >68/25-9/11=523/275.\n\nAs c,d<14/9 and523/275>14/9, both c and d are positive.\nThe identity (14/9-c)(14/9-d)>0 implies\n\n    cd > (14/9)(523/275-14/9)=11998/22275.\n\nBut(18) requires Ccd<=3+a+b<42/11. This is impossible, since\n\n    (193/25)(11998/22275)-42/11=189364/556875>0.\n\nHence h_1>1.\n\nNext suppose h_2<=1, so c<=0. From(22),\nb>=68/25-a-d>68/25-18/11>0, so the second-step cut is available.\nIts inequality is\n\n    Cb <= (2+a)(3+c+d).\n\nIf a<=3/5, its left side is at least\n(193/25)(68/25-3/5-9/11), while its right side is at most\n(2+3/5)(3+9/11). The first exceeds the second by844/6875>0.\nThus a>3/5 and u=h_0 lies in(8/5,20/11).\n\nLet C_0=193/25,D_0=42/11 and\n\n    eta=b/(3+c+d)=(C-1-u)/(3+c+d)-1,\n    eta_0(u)=(C_0-1-u)/D_0-1.\n\nThe denominator3+c+d is positive and at most D_0, while C-1-u>0.\nTherefore eta>=eta_0(u)>0 on this interval. The cut itself yields\n\n    a*eta <= a(1+u)/C=(u^2-1)/C<1.\n\nConsequently the mixed necessary inequality(20) applies. Since\nu>=8/5>sqrt(2), its coefficient u(2-u^2) is negative and u-1>0.\nH_mix decreases as eta>0 increases and as C>0 increases. Thus\n\n    0 <= H_mix(u,C,eta) <= H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u)).       (23)\n\nThe final expression is a rational quartic in u. Put\nu=8/5+(20/11-8/5)z for0<=z<=1, and define\nB_(4,k)(z)=binomial(4,k)z^k(1-z)^(4-k). Direct multiplication of the\ndisplayed definition of H_mix gives\n\n    H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u))=sum_(k=0)^4 beta_k B_(4,k)(z),\n\nwhere the exact coefficients in increasing k are\n\n    -124646528/95703125,\n    -3776352043/3158203125,\n    -1682896352/1654296875,\n    -98764097577/127380859375,\n    -36497765912/76428515625.\n\nAll are strictly negative. The Bernstein basis terms are nonnegative\nand sum to1; hence this quartic is strictly negative throughout the\nwhole interval, contradicting(23). This proves\n\n    h_1>1 and h_2>1.                                  (24)\n\nNo conclusion h_0>1 or h_3>1 has been assumed or obtained here.\nThe fixed quartic expansion is small exact algebra, not a numerical\nparameter scan or a solver certificate.\n\n## 8. General cut/quadratic repair and two additional tests\n\nThe mixed construction has a useful full-pair version at either middle\ncut, retaining the entire quadratic prefix. Fix j in{1,2}, let h_j>1,\nP=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i,eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R), and define\n\n    z_0=1,z_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for1<=i<=j,\n    q=-eta*z_j,\n    beta=max(0,max_(0<=i<=j) q(z_i-q)).\n\nThen another necessary inequality is\n\n    beta*((1+P)^2-C^2 eta^2)\n       +(1+eta)eta*(1-C^2 q^2) >=0.                  (25)\n\nHere is a joint derivation, including beta=0. Use the cut function of\nSection2 as the base. For auxiliary data set x_i=g_i=z_i for i<=j,\nset g_i=q for i>j, and follow GD. The first post-cut point is\nx_(j+1)=q(1+R), and x_4=q. All auxiliary proximal points before or\nat j are0; later they equal q times the remaining step sum. Assign\nf_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 and include the zero optimum. The optimum residuals\nare nonpositive. A possible positive finite residual has source i<=j\nand target k>j and equals q(z_i-q) times that remaining step sum.\nThe corresponding base residual is-(1+eta)eta times that sum.\n\nIf beta>0, multiply the auxiliary Gram/values by\nepsilon=(1+eta)eta/beta and orthogonally add to the base. Every original\nresidual is nonpositive; fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives one legal function.\nIts energy is the left side of(25) divided by beta, proving the inequality.\nIf beta=0 the auxiliary data alone are legal, and their energy is\n1-C^2 q^2. This proves(25) in this case too, without division by zero.\nNo Gram from a different schedule was reused.\n\nFull-curvature quadratic data give the additional necessary condition\n\n    C*|product_(i=0)^3(1-h_i)| <=1.                   (26)\n\nThis is the actual function f(x)=x^2/2 started at1; equation(3) applies.\n\nThere is also a nonlinear scalar condition that is stronger than merely\nimposing(26). Since both middle steps exceed1 by Section7, put\nq_mid=(h_1-1)(h_2-1)>0. Then\n\n    C*|q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1)| <=1+h_0.              (27)\n\nUse again the function with derivative min(x,1), this time started at\nx_0=1+h_0. Its first step reaches1; the next two points are\nx_2=1-h_1 and x_3=q_mid. Thus x_4=q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1).\nAll values have the saturated form, so(19) holds. If x_4>1 then\nx_4+s>C>1+h_0 and V<0. Otherwise g_4=x_4 and(19) gives(27).\nThe inequality in(27) itself forces |x_4|<1, because 1+h_0<C.\n\nThe cuts yield a quantitative endpoint-sum constraint as well. Put\nA=1+h_0,D_end=1+h_3,E_end=A+D_end and b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1.\nRearranging the cuts at1 and2 gives\n\n    b<=A(C-A)/(C+A), c<=D_end(C-D_end)/(C+D_end).\n\nThe function z(C-z)/(C+z) has second derivative-4C^2/(C+z)^3<0.\nAdding these two inequalities and using concavity at their midpoint yields\n\n    C-E_end-1=b+c<=E_end*(2C-E_end)/(2C+E_end),\n    E_end >= 2C(C-1)/(3C+1).                         (28)\n\nIn particular at least one endpoint exceeds1. Indeed if both were at\nmost1 then E_end<=4, and(28) would imply C^2-7C-2<=0. That polynomial\nis positive at193/25 (value2224/625) and strictly increasing thereafter.\nThus a remaining candidate has three or four steps>1; the possible step\nat most1 can only be one endpoint. No endpoint has been deleted.\n\n## 9. Why the clipped three-step test cannot be omitted\n\nAn exact scalar example separates(27) from the preceding cuts, the\nfull-curvature test, the suffix pair test, and the two middle-cut repairs.\nIt is an explicitly constructed counterexample to the sufficiency of\nthat smaller necessary-condition list, not a candidate seed.\n\nTake\n\n    h=(1,217/100,41/20,3/2), s=168/25, C=193/25.\n\nAll four cuts(4) hold. At j=0 the left side is0. At j=1 the two sides\nare22581/2500 and91/10; at j=2 they are4053/500 and417/40;\nat j=3 they are193/50 and311/50. The suffix pair constraint(18)\nhas left side4053/1000 and right side417/100. The full-curvature\nproduct in(26) vanishes. At both middle cuts the auxiliary prefix\nproduct z_j is zero since h_0=1, so q=beta=0 and(25) is positive.\nThe first mixed test(20) requires h_0>1 and does not apply here.\n\nNevertheless the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at2,\nhas the exact trajectory\n\n    x=(2,1,-117/100,2457/2000,-543/2000),\n    g=(1,1,-117/100,1,-543/2000).\n\nIts energy equals\n\n    V=4-(104799/50000)^2<0,\n\nbecause104799>100000. This is precisely a violation of(27), arising\nfrom the return to the affine part at x_3>1. It demonstrates the need\nto retain nonlinear chronological transitions even after compactness,\noriginal coefficient signs, and full-curvature screening are imposed.\n\n## 10. Remaining problem\n\nThe necessary cut domain is not asserted sufficient. The full original\nfeasibility problem is still open inside the compact region above, now\nwith both middle steps>1, at least one endpoint>1, and the additional\nconstraints(18),(25)--(28), plus(20) wherever its hypotheses hold.\nNeither a feasible seed nor a global separator has been produced.\nAll20 multipliers, all five formal gradients, and every remaining step\ndegree of freedom are retained. {"id": "7b447329aac48edd", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T03:33:49.380889+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C80 completed adjacent-pair necessary condition now covers EVERY b,c>1, without an extra D>BC hypothesis: P²D >=BC[P²+2BP+BU], with P=1+prefix sum,B=b-1,C=c-1,D=1+suffix sum,U=b+c+suffix sum.
